Summary

What if Kryptonians built a huge spaceship that would save some small part of them from extinction?

What if Kara would never go to Earth?

What if Astra would still get to the Earth and use Myriad, enslaving millions of people, making them flee and scatter all over the galaxy?

What if Kara would meet Lena in different circumstances?

Notes

It's my first time joining the SCBB and I am really excited! =D

First chapter is the introductory one, where I tried to establish the customs or Krypton and Kara's backstory, her sisterly bond with Alex, and also some other characters. She is yet to meet Lena but she will, soon ;-)

If it gives you Cyberpunk 2077 vibes, you are right! As it should be.

    LOG.1: Truth, justice and the kryptonian way.

    “They raided the warehouse again!” - Kal-El scoffed from the dimly lit green screen in the corner of the room. He turned his body camera around and put it back on his chest, walking around and showing her the destruction that The Sawflies caused to their storage area. They took everything that could last, plus some rare metals and reagents that Kryptonians used to build their legendary spaceship engines. As was typical for the Sawflies, the guards weren’t killed, only left unconscious on the floor or pinned to the wall by nails that one of The Sawflies used as ammo. “The Sawfly” logo, in bright neon green, was sprayed on the walls all over the place either as an act of vanity or just to make a mess of the space.

     

    “And you just let them go?” - Kara scoffed, tapping her fingers on the armrests of her chair. Kal-El was a good person, but still a young and inexperienced Space Guard. He had a tendency to be a little bit too nervous, and a little bit too eager sometimes. Kara could have been softer towards her own cousin, but as a Space Guard so he should have been able to stand his ground and be strong when facing the obstacles, even if the obstacle is his own blood.

     

    “N-no, Commander! Lieutenant Danvers placed a tracker on their vehicle this time. Sending you the coordinates!” - Kal-El babbled hastily, and Kara heard his clothes rustling against the microphone of his body camera when he searched for his communicator in a slight panic. “ Still too young ”, Kara thought, rubbing her forehead and sighing quietly, so as not to scare her young cousin into full on panic. She only ever sent him on the safest missions and only with Alex, her chosen sister, or accompanying him herself. The Sawflies were relatively harmless, bold, messy, greedy, but they never killed a living being when they ransacked, unlike many other raiders. 

     

    “Kara?” - she heard her sister’s voice through the intermittent communications. She sounded deeply concerned and Kara stiffened in her seat. - “They stole some Kryptonite.”

     

    “How much?” - Kara frowned, biting her lower lip trying to ward off a sudden anxiety attack. Kryptonite was behind a triple encrypted door masked as a concrete wall so nicely even Kara herself had a hard time finding it. Why would The Sawflies need it? The material is hardly useful for anything but harming and killing Kryptonians, so it was only a direct danger to her people who are left very few in numbers after her home planet perished.

     

    Of course the destruction of Krypton was the handiwork of the Kryptonians themselves. They overtaxed the resources of their planet and abused it for so long and so harshly that the core of the planet collapsed and exploded, leaving the whole civilisation homeless. 

     

    Luckily, before that, they managed to build an enormous spaceship that could house three and a half million people, which was hardly an adequate number, scarcely enough to save the Kryptonian race. The selection to board it was harsh, ruthless even, and sometimes Kara wishes she would have died with the remaining 98.467% of her people on Krypton. Their pleas and cries still ring in her ears, the sight of terrified people holding out their babies to her parents - the ruling family, begging them for salvation, still fresh in her eyes, despite more than twenty years passing by. 

     

    Ninety eight point four six seven percent of her people died in a global, man made catastrophe, which they could have prevented if her parents would have listened to her aunt Astra. She saw it coming and tried to stop it, but her methods were too unethical for the tastes of her parents - Alura and Zor-El. So the great House of El split, sister went after sister and Alura imprisoned Astra in Fort-Rozz - the prison that floated somewhere in the Phantom Zone, the most hopeless place in all the galaxies. Kara, her parents, and other members of the great House of El were “chosen” to board The Exodus as the first passengers. Not all other members of the ruling Council of Krypton survived, or chose to board the ship, deciding to die with their people; so in the end, the House of El remained the sole rulers of the people of The Exodus. 

     

    As a daughter of the ruling family of El, Kara shouldn’t be here, she should be in a council room, procrastinating over unimportant state affairs and countless amendments to the old laws that hardly worked now that they are nomads. But here she was, sitting in front of a dim, flickering screen, watching her cousin’s erratic movements across the warehouse, located on one of the friendly planets, inhabited by small, harmless, semi-sentient creatures that could survive its harsh atmosphere and breathe its poisonous air. 

     

    She chose this to escape the silent judgement from her parents and the not so quiet whispers and gossip about her personal life, or, better to say - a total lack of it. Because of her lack of attachment and her age she was seen as an unfortunate spinster, a burden to her family and to society, unable to produce an heir and to add her seed to repopulate the Kryptonian race. 

     

    Every year she ran her genome through the matching matrix and came up with a big, flashy, red, ugly “no match”. Every year she disappeared into space and plunged into her work as a Commander of the Space Guards - the law enforcement agency that protected all of the galaxy. It was not only an important, quite fun job, but also allowed her to just forget the judgement and whispers, to hide in the depths of space and cover her status and surname under the uniform.

     

    “The entire stash; there was around 2 pounds of it in this particular warehouse.” - Alex switched on her camera and showed Kara the empty back room. She turned around and stepped out of it through the huge hole that was left by the pinpoint explosion of a force so great it could penetrate a reinforced wall. Kara squinted, looking at the edges of the triple steel, torn and slightly fused by the explosion. - “Kara? Are you there? I am going after The Sawflies. These rats were testing my patience for years! Now their day of reckoning is finally near.”

     

    “No, wait!” - Kara ordered, still looking at the edges of the tear, despite Alex’s erratic movements that made her job harder. Something was wrong - The Sawflies never used any explosives, their lock picking was always silent, despite the horrible mess that they left behind - unconscious bodies, graffiti, scattered goods that fell out of their hands while they took it to their ship - as if they had a technically advanced person on their team who did the best hacking jobs she ever saw. And they never took anything like Kryptonite, always choosing useful and long lasting items, like canned food, tech for spaceships, occasionally some raw materials, but only the simplest ones, that showed their lack of the resources to actually work with them. If Kara was completely honest with herself - even the name “raiders” was inaccurate for scoundrels like these. She would rather call them “scavengers” - junkers who live by stealing useful scrap and building spaceships that look more like rusty tin cans, and basic weaponry that looks like it could explode in their own dirty faces. - “You are coming back to the base and then we will go after them together.”

     

    “What about Kal?” - Alex whispered, trying not to attract Kara’s cousin’s attention.

     

    “Bring him back, I will find a job for him.” - Kara sighed, watching Kal’s body cam solemnly. 

     

    Kara could not risk his life, as he was the last hope for the House of El as the heir and future ruler. Kara believed that at this point everyone thought that she should abdicate all right to be the heir and just give it up to Kal. It wasn't a terrible idea, she is a disappointment anyway, but Kal wasn’t thrilled about it. Growing up around Kara, he soaked in a lot of her personality traits and followed her everywhere, just like when he was a little kid. 

     

    That’s why he followed her to become a member of the Space Guard, despite his and Kara’s parents being absolutely against it and even pressuring Kara to dissuade him from following in her footsteps. He never listened though, so while he was happily following Kara and rushing into all the dangerous situations he could find, Kara was pestered by both sets of their parents and forced to watch him like a baby.

The Space Guard’s station was located at the very edge of it like a silent guardian, standing in the way of any evil that could enter their home. They were slowly orbiting a small yellow sun. It illuminated their quarters day and night through the wide windows and filled the Kryptonian Guards with the unimaginable powers of flight, super strength and a few more. Kara squeezed and opened her fists a few times, stretching her long fingers, as if feeling the strength flowing through her body. 

     

    She crossed arms behind her back and looked out into space until the small shuttle became visible in the distance, its blue and red lights blinking rhythmically. Kara never knew why their shuttles have this feature, but it was an idea suggested by their numerous human members. They said that Guards, or “cops” on their planet, used it as an identification mark on their vehicles. Kara thought that the big “Guard’s” logos on both sides were enough but the blue-red lights were implemented and used widely, and now every Guard shuttle had it, blinking cheerfully in the dark void of space or on the dirty street of some planet, riddled with thieving scavengers.

After she was promoted to Commander, Alex allowed herself a little bit of rebellious behaviour, just to keep Kara’s ego grounded. Kal entered behind her, his helmet under his armpit, spine rigid and hand in a salute gesture - right fist over his heart. He thumped his chest with it slightly and looked into Kara’s eyes fawningly, looking for any commendation from her.

He really deserved it today, and after their short weekend trip home, when they both were picked on by their parents, who mostly ignored Kara, but treated Kal like a baby boy who threw a tantrum, it was this kind of encouragement he was probably sorely seeking. They both hated it and they both took steadying, deep breaths only when they were back in the barracks.

    First they saw an explosion, then a smoke grenade was thrown into the room and then a few obscure figures moved through the destroyed door, looking around, wary and tense. Two of them dragged a weighty box, probably loaded with the explosive device. Last one who entered was a tall woman with regal posture and confident body language. She walked into the warehouse, slowly looking around, like the place belonged to her. Kara paused the video, squinting and trying to discern her features. Something struck her as very familiar, but the face of this stranger was, of course, under the makeshift gas mask with dark vision goggles. She clearly had long dark hair, and Kara made a mental note to herself. This woman looked like she was in charge here, ordering people around and yelling something, gesturing wildly. 

     

    The sound on the recording was completely corrupted, but Kara could see how her goons scattered and dragged the bomb out of the camera’s field of view. The other camera, closer to the Kryptonite cache was destroyed by a single shot from a blaster. The bomb exploded after a short moment and Kara saw them dragging another box to the exit  - now it was that box filled with Kryptonite  - the doom of her race. She cursed quietly under her breath and watched the gang leaving the warehouse, however, on their way out they did something strange; they deliberately made a pointless mess. They threw stuff on the floor and crushed crates, then sprayed crude “The Sawfly” graffiti on the walls and left in a haste. 

     

    Kara closed her eyes and rubbed them with her fingers, sighing deeply. There was enough evidence implicating The Sawflies, but she still didn’t understand who they were led by and why they needed Kryptonite. Looks like they went directly after it and only made a mess to cover their main objective, to throw the Guards off and slow them down while they concentrated on the petty robbery. Kara opened her eyes and looked at the screen again in silent realisation. Where are the Guards?

     

    She fast-forwarded the video until she saw a dozen Kryptonian Guards going into the warehouse. Seems like they were distracted by someone or something and all left their posts. Kara moved so close to the screen the silhouettes of moving people mirrored in her pupils. On the screen one Guard waved his hand and yelped something, before being pinned to the wall by the familiar long nail. It only pierced his clothes over the shoulder and he trashed against it, trying to tear himself off the surface, but then a few more nails pinned him tightly and immobilised him. He yelled something to his teammates and they scattered out of the warehouse only to be thrown back by the entering Sawflies. Kara blinked a few times, surprised. Alex stood on her feet and came to the screen to have a better look.

     

    “Reinforcements?”- she mumbled, chewing the inside of her cheek.

     

    “Not sure… They look kinda different.” - Kara paused the video and poked the screen with her finger. - “Look at the clothes and gear - they seem greatly inferior to those who were here before them. Their weapons are made of junk and their clothes are battered by the weather and excessive wear and tear.”

     

    “These are just the bottom feeders who pick up the scraps after their big bosses took the biggest score.” - Alex wasn’t really impressed, seeing only what she always saw - criminals who needed to be punished. 

     

    Kara turned her attention to the screen again, where the second group entered the warehouse again, looking around timidly, their weapons at the ready. 

     

    They were too wary to be part of the first group, Kara thought, looking at how careful they were, almost crawling around the place, not touching anything unnecessary and gesturing to each other with short, specific signs. They finally relaxed enough to scatter around the warehouse and take some things that Kara expected them to take - canned food, capsules with nuclear waste and electronics; the stuff they could actually use, like all junkers, in repairing their battered weaponry and vehicles and feeding their team. 

     

    “Kara?” - Alex called out to Kara with a satisfied scoff. Kara frowned, looking over her shoulder at her sister. She was always bothered by Alex’s eagerness to dispense justice before she brought evidence to the table. It wasn’t right, but it was, unfortunately, the usual way the Space Guards operated - “ kick first, ask questions after ”. Kara has fought against this mindset for years to no real avail, because it was rooted so deeply. She was unable to change the attitude of thousands of subordinates alone. - “Our bug arrived at the Megastructure.”

Kara dropped herself into the shuttle seat heavily and looked through the windshield. She still wasn’t sure they were on the right track and she wouldn't even bother with The Sawflies if not for stolen Kryptonite, but they had also plagued the galaxy for years and appear to have finally stepped into territory so egregious they angered the Cerberus. If they are responsible for taking the Kryptonite then maybe the time to finally answer for their crimes has come.

She tried to argue with Kara, focusing on how many humans there were in Megastructure and how she could more easily find common ground with any local dwellers. Kara, however, wasn’t having it, knowing how easily Alex is triggered and ends up throwing hands. - “And I expect you to follow my lead. Please, just for once don’t make me order you to stay in the shuttle. Megastructure is not The Station, it is a dangerous place and the last thing I want is to stir the toxic pot.”

It is a harsh, gritty and dark planet, populated by all kinds of criminals, scavengers and vagabonds. Its narrow multi-layered streets provide easy hideouts which made it a real haven for all kinds of criminals and runaway slaves.

     

    Thousand years ago Megastructure was a different planet with a name that was already long forgotten, populated by aliens now mostly extinct or mutated and living in the depths of the planet, hundreds of thousands feet deep. They were technologically advanced and wanted to expand their planet by building on its surface. For this they created roboticized constructors that they called “builders”. As these robots were mindless creatures, created only to do one job - expand the planet and build, they created an AI to control and guide them. 

     

    For years everything was fine, the planet was built upon, and civilization thrived. What they didn’t take into account was the AI was slowly learning and becoming ambitious. Eventually the builders started to build other builders and expanded not only the planet but also its population of robots. As old buildings were becoming unlivable, new structures were built on top of old, blocking the people inside obsolete concrete and steel walls. 

     

    Soon builders took over the planet, and without sunlight or food its civilization mutated into small, pale and barren creatures that soon died out almost fully, leaving only a few tribes buried deep inside the structure. One day alien ships received a distress signal, sent by the original Megastructure population, but delayed by years by the rogue AI. Swiftly a dozen spaceships came and retook the planet from the builders and critically damaged the AI, but its lifeless husk still exists somewhere in the middle of the Megastructure. Most builders were scrapped, only a few remained, their long steel arms still sticking out of the walls and edges and slowly building up the structure, reverting to their original purpose and expanding the planet ever further. Over time the planet was repopulated with raiders, scavengers, fugitives and adventurers, making it a hub for petty criminals.

Flashy LED lights, in all kinds of toxic colours, blinded Kara and it took her a few seconds to adapt to its flamboyant garishness. The streets below were filled with people of all kinds - tall Almeraci, grey skinned Criq, Tsaurons that resembled a reptile, green skinned Halla’s and many more of all skin colours and shapes.

Street urchins sneaked around the densest crowds and Kara could swear she saw a few purses being cut from the owners’ belts. They flew over the expansive bazaar that sold all kinds of goods, and Kara could swear again that most of it, if not all, was stolen. Alex pulled over in a crowded alley and they got out of their shuttle, leaving it and hoping that they will find it at least relatively intact when they return. She urged Kara to follow and confidently walked towards the door, knocking on it and waiting for the screen to slide to the side. At least four eyes looked at them, evaluating their affinity and worth. Kara was never happier for the grey cloak, covering her blue Space Guard uniform.

The stench made Kara’s eyes tear up and she suppressed a cough that was ready to escape her throat. She looked around, trying to keep her breath shallow. The bar was packed densely, and though the crowd was not the fishiest she had ever seen, it was still quite unique, with all kinds of aliens hanging out behind the numerous tables. 

     

    Her super hearing caught a few conversations which could be incriminating if she bothered to pay attention, and she noticed that some people traded suspicious packets under the tables. She smirked, catching the gaze of a small, thin man with a pointy nose, who took a small jar with a bluish sparkling substance that he hid up his torn up sleeve. He visibly shivered and hid from her eyes in the shadows of the room, and a new person appeared in his place in an instant - a Havanian of the upper class, her angelic features sharpened by the shadows and half obscured by the hood of her dark cloak.

    “There is no other place to get some information like Al’s, and believe me, it will be easier and faster to spend some credits or throw some hands than aimlessly wander all over the whole big ass Megastructure… that could take us years.” - Alex looked at Kara like she was a child and slammed her fist on the counter with a loud “ thud ”. She ordered some Krenon Rum and scrunched her face when some of the fiery, spicy liquid went down her throat. She scanned the area carefully, looking for something. - “Look!”

     

    Kara followed her eyeline and saw a kid who sneaked over to the Naltorian selling drugs to the ever growing crowd. It was a human child, well fed and in relatively good clothes of neutral colours, that would help her to blend in with the crowd. The kid left a little box with another batch of drugs on the lap of Naltorian and snuck back into the shadows.

    “It’s no use, kids are too sneaky. Let’s wait for the drug dealer to leave, looks like they’re one of The Sawflies.” - Alex hissed, gripping Kara’s bicep as hard as she could, but Kara was on the hunt already. She tore herself off Alex and gestured to her to stay put. Using her X-ray vision, she located the kid who had already ran away down a narrow street, her super hearing catching the sound of the child's small feet making quiet splashing sounds when she stepped into the puddles on the ground. Like a hound she was off to catch her prey.

     

    She squeezed through the dense crowd of Al’s and slipped through the doorway onto the street, finally inhaling clearer air. Megastructure was in a yellow sun’s system, and despite the little amount of sunshine it got inside its structures, it was still more than enough to give Kara super powers. She launched into the air, scaring a drunkard who tried not to fall into the dirt, desperately clinging to the wall of Al's bar. 

     

    She followed the kid through the intricate narrow streets that looked more like a labyrinth and would drive a person unprepared for it crazy. The girl dived under the fence and snuck past the Space Guard’s cordon easily, making her way to a hole in the wall that led to a steel ladder that brought her to an empty building with crumbling walls covered in familiar graffiti. She straightened up and ran past the guards who lazily greeted her, one pulled down her hood and ruffled her short, shaggy mane of hair.

As soon as she heard the girl open the door and close it, she landed just before the two sleepy guards who grabbed their weapons in an  instant and aimed them at Kara. She expected them to start shooting but they didn’t, their eyes scanning her from head to toe and back. One of them, an Orinoca who was a bit taller than Kara and much bulkier, stepped forward, lowering her weapon and squinted her eyes at Kara, reading her mind.

    “House of El?” - the other guard stepped up, looking at Kara, not scared but puzzled. - “What do the gods want from the worms?”

The Orinoca gestured for Kara to follow her through the dark corridor where the girl disappeared moments ago. They entered a big chamber filled with boxes of various sizes. Kara saw a few from their own warehouses, from Almerac, Quaria and even N’cron. The Orinoca urged Kara to go faster and led her further into The Sawfly’s headquarters.

     

    “Why did you let me in?” - Kara broke the silence first, looking at the broad back of the Orinoca.

     

    “I read your mind, you don’t want to harm us. You want justice and I can respect that.” - the warrior woman answered, shrugging. - “Feeh’a Amarii will see you and will decide if she will help you or order us to kill you.”

     

    “Feeh’a? Is it a title?” - Kara asked, curious. 

     

    “In Kwai tongue it means ‘mother’.” - the woman stopped before the plain grey door and looked into Kara’s face with sharp white eyes. Kara felt her mind being probed again and after a few moments, when she found nothing suspicious she opened the door and nudged Kara inside. 

    LOG.2: run, girl, run!

    Chapter Summary

    Kara gives certain someone a chase and tries to bring her to justice.

    Chapter Notes

    P.S Alex is being a bit of an asshole here but bear with her, she will grow as a person in the future.

    Thank you for reading<3

    Kara stumbled over her own feet stepping into the dimly lit room. She managed not to fall and straightened her spine, raising her head and looking around. The room was big and cosy with its walls covered in carpets weaved by skilled hands, lit by the warm, flickering light of the fireplace and filled with furniture of a nice quality. In the corner Kara noticed a crib and baby changing table with some items that clearly belonged to an infant. A small blue-skinned Aloi girl played with a plump human baby on the floor but as soon as she saw Kara she grabbed the baby and ran away, hiding behind a heavy curtain. Kara's sharp hearing caught her running down the stairs deeper into the hideout. 

     

    “You scared them.” - she turned in the direction of the calm voice. It has a clicking sound to it but was pleasant to the ears, silky, quiet and even reassuring. Behind a wide desk sat a Kwai woman, her ebony chitinous skin glistening in the light of the fireplace. Her huge black eyes held an unreadable expression and her face was like a mask, expressionless and calm. Near her stood a human man, short in stature and completely bald, covered in tattoos from head to toe, with metal spikes coming out of his head, that made it look like he had horns.

     

    “I beg your pardon, Feeh’a Amarii.”- remembering her name. Kara bowed with respect, after all, no matter how big or small, she was the boss of The Sawflies. - “I didn’t mean to interrupt.”

     

    “Why did you come, Commander?” - she stood up looking first at the House of El crest on her broad chest and then straight into the eyes as she walked towards her, carefully manoeuvring around the fluffy carpet where the kids played before. - “What interest could your kind have in people like us?”

     

    “There was an attack on our warehouse earlier today.” - Kara started carefully, not to accuse them or provoke the gang leader. She sensed her calmness to be superficial, the Kwai sounded too composed and too self-confident for someone who was standing before the Kryptonian Commander of the Space Guards, and it made Kara uneasy. She heard people moving behind the walls and coming up the stairs, where that little girl with the baby just ran away.  - “Our surveillance footage led us to believe it was your troops.”

     

    “Isn’t the first time, Commander.” - she stood uncomfortably close to Kara and looked into her eyes. The Commander held her ground and looked back into the black eyes of the Kwai - they were enormous for her head, protruding from the sockets significantly, thus making blinking impossible. She was much shorter than Kara, her thin, fragile body covered in a shiny, ebony coloured chitinous shell. She had four humanoid-like arms, two legs and four small wings that were folded on her back and looked like a delicate transparent cloak. She was quite attractive, but Kara chased that thought away. Her lack of a personal life really took a toll on her and made her mind too skittish for her tastes. - “And you never came here before, despite us raiding your stock on the regular basis. What is so different this time?”

     

    “This time your people stole Kryptonite.” - Kara said, her face hardening and voice steady and strong. She dropped the friendly expression and stood tall, squaring her shoulders. It was time to make a statement that this is a serious business, nothing to play about if you don’t want to make The Exodus mad. The almost monotonous voice of this woman, and the fact that she didn’t even bother to hide that they stole from Kryptonians, was irritating and Kara decided to drop the subtlety and just go for it. It didn’t have the desired effect on the people in the room though, the Kwai just shrugged and the man at the back of the room scoffed loudly.

     

    “Why would we? Kryptonite is useless to us. You cannot do anything useful with it anyway - you cannot use it in a weapon or build explosives with it. The only thing it does is harm to your race.” - the man at the back said with a lot of disdain in his voice. - “You just have these crazy laws about it because you are scared of it.”

     

    “Kryptonite is a deadly substance that can exterminate my people so yes, I am concerned and I would really like to know who stole it and what they are planning to do with it.” - Kara tensed and looked into the man’s eyes. He watched her with a smirk. It wasn’t vile or hateful, he was just mocking her and as bad as Kara hated it and wanted to wipe that smirk off his face, he wasn’t wrong - the only reason for stealing the Kryptonite was to harm her people.

     

    “Lex, darling, we should be respectful of the concerns of Kryptonians.” - the Kwai woman said with the fakest concern Kara ever heard, then turned to him and walked back to her chair. It made Kara’s eyes twitch in anger -  at this moment she knew she was also being played by Amarii. - “Answer the Commander, please. Did you steal it?”

     

    “No, we didn’t.” - Lex said firmly. He could have fooled Kara, but the Kryptonite was missing and all the evidence pointed to this gang.

     

    “You heard him.” - the Kwai woman sat on her chair and peered into Kara’s eyes again with her black unblinking ones.

     

    “I'm afraid I will need more than the word of a human I’m meeting for the first time in my life.” - Kara pressed, looking back at her sternly. She would have believed them if not for the mocking tone and all the snarkiness  from both Amarii and Lex. 

     

    She was a bit lost here - on the one hand Lex was right. Kryptonite was useless to  this gang, who was a practical bunch and rarely killed anyone during their raids. So the idea that they could have been a danger to the Kryptonian race was unbelievable, but they also could have done it for someone else, and only they knew who the client was, if there was one. On the other hand - they sounded too secretive, which again could indicate that they were hiding something. Kara opened her mouth to ask more questions, but Kwai lifted her hand to shut her down. 

     

    “He is my child, as are all The Sawflies. I believe him and so should you.” - Amarii said with the most monotonous voice Kara ever heard. It sounded like the end of the conversation and Kara didn’t like how it went at all. She achieved literally nothing and it left her frustrated and angry. She was sure they lied and hid something, but she couldn’t put her finger on what exactly they were covering up - their own crimes or their ties to  a powerful client. Their whole organisation was just a criminal gang full of hackers and thieves; even if they aren’t lying and are really innocent of stealing Kryptonite, they still commit plenty of other crimes and are deserving of punishment. Kara decided it was best for now to take her leave of them, but she will return and next time she will bring backup. - “Off you go.”

     

    There wasn’t much she could do now, alone, in a hideout full of criminals and children without harming the latter and killing most of the gang members, so she calmly watched the Kwai wave her off and turned away, letting the man she called Lex move towards Kara to escort her out. He looked at her with a mix of suspicion and badly disguised disdain, hissed “let’s go, pig” and urged her to turn around and leave the room. The way back was much longer than her way in with all The Sawflies lurking in all darkest corners of the hallways and throwing hostile gazes at her. She managed to get out without anyone shooting at her or making any other serious moves, only two people spat on the ground and whispered derogatory terms aimed at her job and social position. 

     

    Kara was pushed out of the entrance to the hideout and the doors closed behind her with a loud clang. She sighed heavily, deeply disappointed, and flew into the air to get back to her shuttle faster. The city was still busy, which was not a surprise - the concept of time didn’t exist here like it did on other planets. Most of the planet’s cities were inside the structure, thus locals couldn’t distinguish days from nights, counting their time in hours, and the activity inside the living quarters and markets never ceased. Kara flew over the bazaar past the rows with fruits and vegetables of all sizes and shapes from all over the galaxy, filled with shiny signs, advertisements, a cacophony of music and hundreds of people chatting, arguing and haggling. She was close to her shuttle already and she could hear the muffled sounds of music and voices spilling out of Al’s bar.

     

    “Stop right there, scum!” - she heard Alex yelling and shot through the air towards her voice. 

     

    Kara saw her sister running towards their shuttle, unholstering her gun on the way. She followed the direction Alex was looking and saw a young human woman jumping off their vehicle. She retracted two long wires that were coming out of her head and put on a helmet with night vision goggles on it. She was wearing all black clothes and had long black hair. It looked too familiar, Kara huffed in realisation. It looked like this was that same woman that they saw on a tape and Kara felt the thrill of the hunt coming on, making her stomach flutter and adrenaline spike. 

     

    She landed on her feet, willing to give chase to this woman on the ground, like a predator would chase their prey before feasting on it. Kara felt like she was close to knowing who stole the Kryptonite and wanted to prolong the pleasure of the chase, even if just for a little bit. The woman ran towards a narrow street, jumping over the bodies of the drunk patrons and especially deep puddles. She wasn’t the fastest runner but she knew the streets which would give her an advantage if she was being chased by another human, but it was Kara who was after her. Alex also followed behind, but wasn’t willing to run past Kara, understanding that she wanted to do it herself. 

     

    The young woman ran like a rabbit, zig zagging between the houses and choosing the darkest, narrowest alleys in a vain hope of escape. She looked back, saw Kara running only a few feet behind her and accelerated. Kara heard her laboured breath and saw her knees buckling a few times. This girl didn’t have the best stamina and she wasn’t built for a fight or a run - a big head, very narrow shoulders and weak arms and legs. She had a sniper rifle behind her back, seemingly self made from junk, and a little bag on her belt with something metallic rattling inside of it. 

     

    After just a few minutes she slowed down significantly, her weak lungs making a wheezing sound as she exhaled, like all untrained humans made when they were absolutely exhausted. She turned her head around again and mewled weakly, horrified. She tried to gain some more speed, but failed to do so, as the initial adrenaline rush of the chase was failing her already. She jumped over a wide crevice in a structure, but her legs were too short and she was too tired, so she didn't make it to the other side, falling off the edge with a squeal. 

     

    “Caught you, little beast.” - Kara said calmly, catching her mid air and holding her by the arm, watching her legs helplessly dangling. She pulled her up and briskly threw her on the ground right before Alex, who managed to catch up with them without any problem. The girl yelped quietly from the impact, tried to get up but was kicked back down by Alex. 

     

    “Stop, don’t.” - Kara abruptly stopped her sister from kicking the runaway again. Alex scoffed at her but moved away, allowing Kara to lift the girl up onto her feet. She waited until the runaway found her balance, stood solidly on the ground and then removed her helmet. 

     

    Unexpectedly, under her helmet, this woman was breathtakingly beautiful and Kara couldn't help but stare at her face, taking in her every feature. She indeed had long black hair shaved on one side, with a few thin implants going from her temple to the back of her head. The implant pattern formed three straight lines, covering her shaved side and ending together almost on the back of her neck, where Kara saw three small slots, where the woman hid those wires she saw before. 

     

    Despite being from the lowest social class of thieves and scum, she had regal facial features with a high forehead, straight nose and the sharpest jawline Kara ever saw on a human. Huge green eyes and puffy rosy lips, that reminded Kara of a makeshift “hearts” that Alex taught her to draw, completed the ethereal image. Right now, though, she was scared; her chest rippling with the heavy breaths from the excruciating exertion of running, and pale green eyes darting from Kara to Alex and back again. She looked at one cop, then the other, seemingly deciding if she would be able to convince at least one of them of her total innocence. She met Alex's angry gaze and decided that she would have more luck with Kara.

     

    "Listen, officers, I was just going by, meaning no harm. I am not a criminal,  I am just a law abiding citizen, like we all are."  - the lie was so blatant Kara couldn't contain a smile and masked it by clearing her throat. 

     

    "But you ran."  - Kara said, her voice calm but her intent blue eyes drilling two little holes in the lying girl's skull.

     

    "I was scared. I…"  - she started but was rudely interrupted by Alex. 

     

    "That’s enough bullshit! You are under arrest for tampering with a Space Guard's shuttle, for breaking into a warehouse that belongs to The Exodus, for stealing Kryptonite.”  - Alex listed accusations at the same time twisting and locking the wrists of the young woman into handcuffs and switching on their power suppressor. The woman winced from discomfort, when the dampening signal made contact with her implants, and put them on stand by. - “And for resisting arrest.”

     

    “What? I didn’t resist!”- the young woman squeaked, while being turned around and pushed by Alex to go forward in front of them. - “And about the other stuff…”

     

    “You can explain it to the Kryptonian Council. You stole Kryptonite, and that falls under the jurisdiction of The Exodus. They will judge you and will determine the punishment they see fit for scum like you.” - Alex scoffed, watching the girl almost fall after tripping over her own feet and looking back at them in horror.

     

    “Wait, The Exodus? Why? I am a citizen of the Megastructure, you cannot just take me wherever and do whatever pops into your head!” - she protested, wriggling in her cuffs like a worm.

     

    “Actually we can!” - Alex spat and pushed her hard. The girl fell against the hood of their shuttle and rolled over, looking at Alex angrily.

     

    “Stop pushing me, pig. You fucking cowards can only kick a person when they are cuffed and on the ground.” - she flashed an angry gaze on Alex and lifted her leg trying to kick her in the shin. Alex grabbed the holster with her laser gun on her belt and snarled at the young woman.

     

    “Stop, both of you. Alex, holster your gun. You -” - Kara growled, her eyes starting to glow with a subtle buzzing sound, startling them both and looked at the girl, pinning her to the hood of their shuttle with her gaze alone. - “Get inside, and stop resisting if you don’t want your punishment to be even harsher than it could be now, with all the charges against you.”

     

    “Listen. I don’t know what you saw and who framed me but I didn’t do any of the things you accused me of.” - the young woman started as soon as they took off into the air and Kara directed their shuttle to return to The Exodus. She shuffled on her seat, moving closer to the front seats, struggling in her cuffs behind her back. She was visibly uncomfortable but managed to keep her balance and pressed her forehead with a loud “thump” to the glass dividing the front seats with Kara and Alex from the back. 

     

    “What is your name?” - Kara asked, looking into the front mirror trying to catch sight of the girl, but seeing only the back of her head.

     

    “Lena.” - the young woman answered, her voice muffled by her uncomfortable pose.

     

    “So, Lena, we happen to have a videotape of your gang robbing the warehouse that belongs to The Exodus and thus, Kryptonians. As you already know, some Kryptonite was stolen.” - Kara explained calmly, switching on the hyperlight engine and shooting into the void of space. - “And we happened to see you on that tape.”

     

    “It cannot be.” - Lena laughed, lifting her head from the screen and looking at Kara. She tried to find something in her face, looking at her stern side profile. Sympathy maybe? Or at least a smallest piece of trust? - “I never went into that warehouse.”

     

    “You all say that, scum.” - Alex scoffed, crossing arms on her chest and looking behind her, meeting Lena’s big green eyes. 

     

    “But I never did, believe it or not!” - she yelped, angry and helpless. She laughed bitterly, fully understanding that she was royally fucked and no one would ever believe her, but continued anyway. - “I mean yes, we broke into the warehouse and stole some goods, but it was not Kryptonite. Actually we saw that someone had already been there before us. And I never go inside when we go out on business. I am a lookout. A sniper! What is the point of me in close combat? You saw my weapon, damn, you confiscated it! You know what it is fit for!”

     

    “Save it for the interrogation room.” - Alex rudely interrupted her and hit the button that dimmed the screen and made it sound proof. 

     

    Lena opened her mouth to argue further and closed it when she saw that she was cut off from the front seats. She suddenly felt the urge to scream and cry, helpless, trapped in the narrow shuttle with two people that were sure that she was guilty of a crime she never committed. She felt the space closing in on her like dirt over a lonely grave, and it filled her with anxiety. There was not enough oxygen around her and she started to hyperventilate there on the back seat of the cop’s shuttle, alone and terrified. 

     

    She whined weakly, trying to get up and knock on the dimmed screen with her legs or her head, or whatever, to at least get them to yell at her and switch the screen back on. But her body seemed to shut down, her legs went limp and her arms numb from the twisted position. She felt nauseous suddenly, her head spinning and stomach too light, her breath quick and pulse racing. She thought that it would be poetic justice to puke all over the cop’s shuttle, somehow vindicating herself. She chuckled at the thought, feeling even more light headed. 

     

    “Get out.” - she suddenly heard Alex’s voice. It sounded muffled, as if she spoke from under water. Lena turned her head towards it and squinted when the clear white lights blinded her. It took her a few moments to adjust her sight, calm her erratic breaths and swallow down a clump of nausea still lingering in her throat. - “I said, get out!”

     

    “Okay, okay!” - Lena mumbled, her voice coarse as if she screamed all the way here. She wiggled her numb body to the edge of the shuttle seat and jumped down, her legs still weak and trembling.

     

    “Are you okay?” - she heard a concerned voice beside her and turned towards it, catching the sight of the Kryptonian Guard’s blue eyes, who looked at her with concern. She couldn’t say why, but it was weirdly comforting, calming her racing heart completely and giving her strength to at least stand on her own feet. - “You look pale and your heart… It is beating too fast.”

     

    “I am fine.” - Lena scoffed, pushing down her panic and fear and holding her head high. Sudden movement made her dizzy but she ignored it, looking the tall blonde cop straight in her blue eyes and slightly pouting. She caught the cop looking at her chest and narrowed her eyes, scoffing and whispering “ creep ”. The cop was lost for a moment but then the realisation hit her and she panicked, her eyes darting from side to side and cheeks flushing. Lena smiled triumphantly - she hadn’t puked all over the cop's shuttle, but she caught her creeping and made her flustered.

     

    “I was just looking at your heart.”- Kara mumbled, feeling bad that she made the human girl uncomfortable. Lena scoffed and shook her head in distrust which made Kara even more confused.

     

    “If you are fine then move your feet.” - Alex shouted and jerked Lena by the shoulder, making her walk in front of them towards the exit of the landing platform. She didn’t seem to catch their short and awkward conversation and stubbornly continued with the arrest. 

     

    They went through the wide automatic doors that lead into a spacious compartment, its walls filled with rows of pods and power suits that could survive the harsh environment of space and inhospitable planets. Lena gasped, looking at them - none of these suits were like any she ever saw before. Kryptonians were always technologically advanced and had rare and precious resources both in their own spaceship and stored on other planets. 

     

    Their influence was widespread and affected the political situation on other planets. They were a rich, strong race of scientists, militarists and traders, and the most terrifying part of it was the fact that under a yellow sun they had superpowers and were practically unkillable. In comparison to them, humans were small, fragile and sickly creatures that could barely survive off world, and whose defining trait was the ability to rapidly procreate. If Lena compared a human to a Kryptonian it was like comparing a bug to a deity.

     

    She was broken out of her thoughts by the clanking sound of the other doors opening. It led to a smaller room on the walls of which she could see big yellow letters. Lena had limited knowledge of the Kryptonian language but she managed to translate a sign that read “ Decontamination ”. A smaller door opened before them opening into a narrow space, closing after them with a gentle hiss. She felt humid jets of air blowing all over her, covering her in the thinnest layer of antiseptic, killing all bacteria and neutralising any radiation. After they were processed through the decontamination chamber, the smaller door with bright yellow stripes opened, leading them into The Exodus itself. It was grandiose, the sight of the huge ship's interior unfolding before her. 

     

    Lena had never been inside of a livable spaceship of this size. It looked like a fantasy city which she saw in a torn up paper book that someone brought from Earth. She always thought that the inside rooms of a spaceship that big should be dark, it is a ship after all. But it was light and airy, its high ceiling covered in a myriad of small light sources that illuminated the area with a cold white light, akin to what she saw on other planets when the sun went up in the morning. The architecture of the ship was simple yet sophisticated, the floors covered in delicate pattern and ceiling held by the high thin incrusted pillars. The walls and houses were made of light coloured material and the lights softly reflected onto its surface, lighting up the wide inner space even more than it could be achieved with the ceiling light sources alone. 

     

    They only passed a few people on their way in, but the closer they were to, as Lena assumed, the centre of the ship, the denser the crowd of people passing them, most bowing to the blonde Kryptonian that accompanied her. She put a heavy hand on Lena’s shoulder as they passed through, making sure she would not dart away. As if there would be any point in doing that without being caught and maybe even executed on the spot by the numerous Guards in their blue uniforms, who circled the square like a pack of hungry wolves. Lena looked around counting them but gave up after the first dozen and switched her attention to her surroundings. 

     

    The ship square was impressive. Of course there was some vegetation in Megastructure, if one considered tufts of withered grass, greyish in colour, or rare and ugly leafless trees that held onto the concrete walls of the Megastructure with the last of their strength, vegetation. In comparison the green fields of The Exodus were exquisite. The delicate growth of the ivy entwined the high thin pillars that surrounded the park with a mini-mountain in the middle and a murmuring waterfall. It pooled on the bottom of the mountain, flowing into a few thin streamlets, nourishing numerous trees growing all around the park, throwing soft shadows over the white benches that were set in neat rows all over the park.

     

    There were people all around the ship’s square, either walking around or chilling on the benches, enjoying the warm artificial weather and synthetic sunlight. Lena turned her head a few times, believing that there was something missing and trying to put her finger on it. Then it hit her  - there were no children in the park, no strollers pushed by caring parents, no toddlers playing on blankets under the gentle shadow of the silvery trees, no kids running around and chasing each other. It was a vast contrast with the Megastructure and many other planets that Lena travelled to. She was stopped from moving too much by the blonde Guard’s steady hand that urged her to go forward. Her touch was weirdly gentle, she never pushed or pulled Lena, only guiding her the right direction with the warm hand on her shoulder. 

     

    They crossed the bridge that led them to the entrance of a structure that looked more solid than the ones surrounding it which were light, elegant and airy. This one was dark grey, with thicker pillars framing its entrance and high heavy doors that opened before them slowly, uncovering a breathtaking look at the huge hall. Giant statues supported its ceiling and the walls were covered in plates with blue glowing writing on them. Lena squinted, trying to read the biggest one right on top of the arc that led them to the second compartment of this huge space. “ The Hall of Justice ”  was written on a plate above the entrance and Lena cringed slightly, expecting a bunch of old Kryptonian hags deciding her fate. 

     

    To her surprise they took a turn to the left and the blonde Kryptonian Guard led her down the stairs into a spacious, but much darker room. There were a few sentries that saluted the blonde, banging their chests with their right fist and standing at attention. The Guard nodded in acknowledgement of their greeting and led Lena to a smaller compartment. Lena saw a bunch of solid doors and sighed heavily. Here is where she gets thrown into jail, like a dirty thug for a crime she never committed and without hope that she, at least, will get some kind of fair trial.

     

    Kryptonians were always a very secluded race and their customs were mostly unknown to the people outside The Exodus, but even a fool knew that they were not fond of other races. They weren’t openly xenophobic, maintaining the visage of a fair, just race of demi-god-like people, but they also weren’t the most accepting of people from other worlds. Rare non-Kryptonians were accepted and cherished, but only if they were special to someone and followed all Kryptonian customs, laws and accepted their own lower place in society. Lena couldn't possibly know what punishment she was facing. Most of what she heard about Kryptonians and their justice system was whispers and myth, passed on by other inhabitants of the Andromeda galaxy, which varied from “unimaginable torture and horror” to “a slap on the wrist”.

     

    To her surprise, she wasn’t led into one of the cells, but to another room with only one table and two chairs. “ Interrogation room” - Lena guessed correctly and felt the blonde Guard’s hand pressing her down onto one of the chairs. She dropped onto it heavily, feeling her legs aching from the run and long walk to this place. She was exhausted, sad and hungry; she didn't have any mental or physical strength left to fight these cops. 

     

    Lena was chained to the table, which provided her cramped arms a bit of relief and she stretched her fingers to restore blood flow. Both cops took their seats across her, watching her with different expressions. The red headed one looked at her with disdain and anger. The blonde one was watching her with calm thoughtfulness and concern, and Lena wondered why. They thought she stole their precious Kryptonite and endangered their race, but the blonde was still kind to her. Either she was just a good natured woman, or they planned to play a hackneyed game of good cop, bad cop. 

     

    “So, Lena.” - the blonde started patiently, looking straight into Lena’s eyes. She braced herself for a long game of wits and rolled her eyes defiantly. She heard the redhead choking silently on an angry sigh and saw that the blonde was slightly disappointed by her reaction. She remained calm though, and made a subtle  gesture to her partner to stay put. - “What were you doing at the warehouse that belongs to The Exodus?” 

     

    “I told you already and I will repeat. I. Was. Never. In. The warehouse.” - Lena emphasised every word, looking the blonde Kryptonian in the eyes. She seemed like the more reasonable one, and Lena chose her as her interlocutor and mostly ignored the redhead. 

     

    “Okay, allegedly you never were there, but we still have you on tape, so please, explain how that is possible?” - she asked calmly, looking at Lena like she was a bratty child. 

     

    “I am saying - I was never in there, believe it or not. I am a lookout, I was outside, I never go inside. You can see me? What would be the use of me in close combat? Do you really think I would hit people with my big head?” - Lena saw the blonde’s eyes darting down and evaluating her physique, lingering a moment too long on her chest. It made her chuckle bitterly. - “I really don’t know who you saw, but it was not me.”

     

    “There was a woman around your height and build in the warehouse. She had black hair, black clothes and night vision goggles, which is quite an accurate description of you.” - the blonde pressed her fingertip on her wrist guard and a picture jumped out, showing Lena the grainy picture of a group of people she never saw. One of them was indeed wearing black clothes and had goggles, but that was it.

     

    “Are you fucking blind or something?” - Lena sighed, looking at the staggered blonde with tiredness in her eyes.

     

    “Watch your tongue, brat!” - the redhead growled, jumping up onto her feet. The blonde raised her hand, calming her down and making her drop back to her seat. She still threw angry gazes in Lena’s direction, but remained quiet.

     

    “Do you see my armour?” - Lena sighed and tilted her head to the side, looking at the blonde like she was a dimwit. - “Does it look anything like the dress on that woman?’ 

     

    “Do you have only one set of clothes?” - the blonde retorted, although looking a bit uncertain. 

     

    Her eyes darted from Lena, examining her armour, to the picture and back. The woman in the picture wore a dress of a dense texture, with a long skirt that brushed the floor and a light cuirass that covered only the top half of her body. The helmet and goggles looked slightly off on her, and clashed with the overall look, like it was taken from someone and put on just to throw someone off. Lena watched the blonde Guard shift her eyes off the picture and look at her clothes, slightly dumbfounded. Lena stood up to give her a better look, causing the redhead to also jump to her feet but she was again stopped by the blonde. 

     

    The blonde stood up slowly and walked around to look at Lena from all sides. It was clear as day their clothes were different - if that woman on the videotape wore an elegant dress, Lena wore something that more resembled armour. It was ebony in colour and reflected light off its surface. The material resembled Kwai chitinous plates but were metallic, most likely made from rare Obsidian ore and was coated with gloss black paint. It covered Lena's whole body in thin, fine plates up to her fingers. The thick refined leather of an unknown animal was fastened to her lower back, making it look like she wore a  knee-high skirt. Thigh-high boots finished the look. It showed signs of being well worn and lovingly restored a few times, but the material seemed very sturdy and of a high quality. 

     

    “No, I do have other clothes, but these are the ones I wear when I go to raid your freaking warehouses!” - Lena laughed, looking at the uncertain expression on the blonde’s face. 

     

    “So you admit you robbed The Exodus. And even though you, as you say, did not do it directly, you still did stand as lookout and shoot at our guards.” - the redhead deadpanned, looking at Lena with a dangerous smirk, like a fox that just caught her prey and was ready to feast.

     

    “I…I never stole any Kryptonite, and you brought me here because of it.” - Lena stammered a little but recovered quickly, finding her ground.  - “If you accuse me of stealing other stuff you should bring me back to the Megastructure and have me judged there, because it is an intergalaxian matter, not a Kryptonian one.”  - she held her head high, looking at the redhead as haughtily as she could in her position as a felon.

     

    “If you didn’t do it, then who?” - the blonde asked sincerely.

     

    “She is lying! Look at her, wiggling like an eel in a frying pan.” - the redhead spat angrily, looking at the blonde in disbelief. - “I can’t believe you just dismissed all the shit she already admitted and fell for her lies?”

     

    “I did not say I believe her, I am just looking at all the perspectives of this case.” - the admission that the blonde Guard didn't believe hurt Lena, but she was already prepared to be disappointed, so she just sighed heavily and looked at her handcuffed hands, fidgeting with her fingers. - “We don’t have any solid proof it was her, but we also do not have enough to prove her innocence. We should consider all options, Alex. You cannot dismiss her words just because you think she is a criminal.”

     

    “She is, though.” - Alex deadpanned and looked at Lena expectantly, awaiting for her response. 

     

    “The warehouse was robbed before we arrived there. We didn’t see them but they blew up the wall and made a mess there.” - Lena said, looking at the incredulous faces of the both cops. She knew they were sure she was a criminal, whether she stole Kryptonite or not, and she didn’t have anything to lose anymore so she just decided to tell them everything she knew without any secrets. - “We didn’t know why one of the walls was blown out so we just took some stuff and left. No one saw your precious Kryptonite.”

     

    “You saw no traces of them? No ships taking off? You arrived just a few minutes after them.” - the blonde Guard asked, searching Lena’s eyes with hers. 

     

    “In just a few minutes they were probably already a few systems away from the warehouse, if they had a good spaceship.” - Lena signed, wondering where they found people this dumb to be Guards.

     

    “We also found typical ‘The Sawflies’ graffiti everywhere.” - the blonde pressed her thumb on her wrist again and brought up another picture. The crudest graffitis Lena ever saw were spray painted all around the warehouse in random places. It somewhat resembled “The Sawfly” logo, but was drawn so badly it was laughable that someone would ever believe it was something that came from the real gang itself. 

     

    “Have you seen our signs before?” - Lena asked, now completely sure they chose Guards by taking the lowest IQ ones from the bunch of applicants. 

     

    “Yes, a lot of times.” - the blonde answered sincerely. 

     

    “So if you saw it you probably saw that they are more neatly drawn?” - Lena sighed, almost defeated. She felt a headache coming on from dealing with these two dimwits alone.

     

    “Well I guess not everyone in your gang is Picasso.” - the redhead spat, leaning back in her chair.

     

    “Okay, but maybe, you happen to see that we don’t just throw them all across the room like prepubescent street brats, but mark important crates that we would want to take next time we will raid the place?” - Lena was growing tired. She looked into the eyes of her captors and saw nothing, not a spark of intelligence. - “The paint is bioluminescent, even if you wash it off or cover it with other paint our sensors will pick it up anyway.”

     

    “That’s bullshit! You are just a bunch of thieving rats and you deserve to rot in jail, Kryptonite or not!” - the redhead visibly lost it. Lena smirked slightly, lowering her head to hide it and not to provoke her further. Alex knew she was right and felt that the case was slipping through her fingers and it made her angry. 

     

    The blonde Guard stood up and unchained Lena from the table before Alex could jump her. She didn’t chain her arms behind her, but still left her handcuffed, just making the electric string longer so she could move more freely. She put her warm hand on Lena’s shoulder again and carefully led her out of the room, hissing something in Kryptonian to her colleague. 

     

    She seemed to know how to handle humans but still was very careful, trying really hard not to harm one with a sudden movement or accidental squeeze. Lena was grateful for it, she wouldn’t wish to meet a rude Kryptonian, knowing their god-like strength they would be able to snap her in half with just two of their fingers. The blonde ordered another Guard to open one of the doors that Lena saw before, and she was pushed inside, into a cell. She turned around and met the blonde's kind blue eyes.

     

    “It doesn’t look good for you, Lena. Even if you didn’t steal the Kryptonite yourself, it could be your gang or you could know who did it and why. If neither you nor your gang did it, you are still a raider and thus, a criminal. We will continue tomorrow, but now rest.” - the blonde looked at her once again with sympathy, then sighed and closed the heavy door, leaving Lena alone in the small dark room.

    LOG.3: It’s a match

    Chapter Summary

    Kara runs her genome through the matching matrix again, like she do every year under the pressure of her parents. It takes different turn from what she had anticipated and changes her life forever.

    Chapter Notes

    I made Kara's parents (especially her mom) horrible in this fic for the plot. I know that in the show they aren't as bad, more like.... questionable. But it was necessary change =)

    Thank you for reading! <3

    Kara stood in the doorway to the holding cells for a few moments, before turning around and facing Alex, who was visibly angry. She didn’t pay too much attention to her, walking past her and nodding to the sentry at the entrance before going up the stairs into the Hall of Justice. She breathed in the fresh air, finally able to relax after feeling caged in the small suffocating interrogation room, but even that didn’t bring her any relief. She still felt uneasy for some reason, like something might be terribly wrong. 

     

    The initial ecstasy of catching the felon faded long ago. Now Kara couldn’t help but think that she had brought the wrong person here, and that she would be torn to pieces by the Council who didn’t truly care about most Kryptonians and thought even less about other species. She walked past the other high ranking Space Guardians and Kryptonian Military, nodding politely while they stood at attention out of respect for her higher rank. She exited the building and went out onto the streets, called for a pod and turned to Alex who was following her all this time. 

     

    Despite calling Alex her chosen sister, they both understood that in the eyes of Kryptonian society  they will never be equals, Alex being human and neither born on The Exodus nor married to a Kryptonian. They met thirteen years ago, when Kara, back then still only a Special Forces Captain of The Space Guards, was head of an anti-slavery operation. Kara’s unit followed a trail of clues about a huge human slave trade through Maaldoria after Earth’s civilisation collapsed when her aunt, Astra, used Myriad to control its population and ruled the planet with her husband Non.

     

    It was just another of her family’s sins - an estranged relative who had been a prisoner of Fort Rozz when it was thrown out of orbit by the explosion of Krypton and drifted into the Phantom Zone, before finding its way out twenty five years ago. In their crazed fear of another planet dying from the exploitation of natural resources, Astra and Non took matters into their own hands and used technology from Krypton, Myriad, to control the minds of its population, making most of them mindless husks. Kara saw it as her personal responsibility to help as many people as she could, given that she was helpless to do anything for the Earth itself.

     

    The planet became an easy target for predators of all kinds, especially slavers that took helpless humans who, under the influence of Myriad, behaved like a flock of sheep, and brought them to Maaldoria to sell to other planets. Some humans were less affected by Myriad. In time they regained some of their consciousness and became smugglers, helping other humans to escape the hell that unfolded on Earth to other planets. They formed a resistance movement called “Free State” and even created some semblance of a new society with a small council that navigated the smuggling operations and a tiny army that fought especially brash slavers and tried to take people back. Alex was a part of the Free State sentinels at that time.

     

    During one of their smuggling operations they were overrun by the Maaldoria slavers and most of the Free Staters were killed or taken to the slave pens of Maaldoria and prepared to be sold. That’s where they met - the Space Guards raining down on the planet and overtaking most of the slave pens in minutes with a highly effective and expertly executed operation led by Captain Zor-El. They freed the slaves and recruited a few people on the spot, mostly sentinels from the Free State and a few mercenaries.

     

    They were preparing to leave the planet, after loading freed slaves into the shuttles that would transport them to other planets as refugees, when Kara heard a noise behind her and turned around only to see a gun pointed at her head. Maaldoria was a planet in a system that was orbiting a red sun, thus making her powerless, more akin to a fragile human. Before she could blink, she heard a gunshot and saw the slaver falling to the ground dead. Alex was the one that pulled the trigger and was standing before her now, trembling from the adrenaline rush and covered in a mix of her own blood and the blood of her enemies.

     

    Since then they grew closer throughout the years, saving each other’s lives countless times. Alex was brought to the Space Guard’s station and became a recruit and then Kara’s Lieutenant. Soon after that, Kara started to call her ‘sister’ out of deep respect, admiration and as gratitude for saving her life repeatedly. Alex accepted it, despite the El family’s history of enslaving her home planet. She was gracious enough to understand that those are not Kara’s crimes and she shouldn’t have been answering for things she hasn't done. Unfortunately that is where her understanding ended. 

     

    After she was freed, she became obsessed with catching as many criminals as she could. She even dug through cold cases and found a few felons that nobody cared to look for anymore after so many years had passed. She was easily triggered and quite ruthless in her pursuit, and despite Kara believing that her desire will cease with the passage of time, she proved her to be wrong and still retained her anger towards intergalactic criminals.

     

    Despite their sisterly bond, Kara was still the only daughter of the House of El and Alex was still a human, so they said their goodbyes and went their separate ways. Alex walked to the barracks, where she chose to live instead of the small apartment she was offered after she obtained the rank of Lieutenant. Kara watched Alex, while she walked away to her place, until she heard the soft beep of the pod that came to take her home. She hopped into it, entering the House of El’s Palace location and relaxed into the soft seat, enjoying her fast ride through The Exodus. 

     

    On the inside the enormous spaceship looked like a prosperous city, with parks, buildings and wide roads. It was fully provisioned to host more than three million people inside without straining its resources. Kara passed living quarters surrounded by little gardens where the inhabitants grew flowers and fruit trees. Two teenagers trimmed bushes around their apartment block, giggling with each other. Kara sighed, looking at them. The regulations regarding having children were tightened again last year, allowing only one new born per thousand inhabitants per year. 

     

    The prolonged life span of the Kryptonian race necessitated the population of The Exodus remain static, with only rare children being made throughout all these years. Of course, sometimes they gathered a group of people to settle somewhere on distant planets in an attempt to re-colonize and maybe make a new liveable planet for the Kryptonian race, but their inability to procreate naturally made their futile attempts at repopulation pointless. Disappointed settlers often came back empty handed, simply wasting countless credits on building a new civilization that could not be maintained. 

     

    The pod stopped in front of the Palace and Kara jumped out of it, stopping for a moment to admire the view. The Palace was the most grandiose building in The Exodus, making it stand out. It served as living quarters for the El family, an office for the rulers of the Kryptonians, and a workplace for the members of The Council. It was a huge building, made of blue solid crystal that glowed from within. It retained the spiky architecture of buildings that one could have found on Krypton, with its tall sharp towers and steep arches, going up to the high ceiling of the ship itself. It was built in the centre of the ship, on the shore of an artificial pond with clear blue water and colourful fishes swimming around it. Several other buildings surrounded the El Palace, but none of them was as grand.

     

    Kara walked up the stairs and proceeded through the doors that opened before her, letting her inside the Palace’s main hall. The interior was decorated in all light colours, mostly whites and blues, with crystal statues of the finest work standing guard throughout the entire length of the corridor that led her to a large room with several people in it. She came through, nodding greetings to some of them and just passing by others before stopping before a pair of Kryptonians who turned around as soon as they heard her footsteps approaching. 

     

    “Kara.” - the woman greeted her, giving her a tight lipped smile.

     

    “Mom.” - Kara bowed before her mother, showing respect to her status. She was disappointed that mother was still mad at her after last year's argument, when Kara was again unmatched and didn’t look sad enough about it. Kara was sure that today’s meeting is destined to end in a similar manner and she will go spend the night in the barracks with Alex and her officers, choosing the calmness of the military base over enjoying the comfort of the Palace.

     

    “Hello, daughter.” - at least her father’s smile was more or less sincere, when he stepped forward and put his hand on her shoulder, rubbing it affectionately. - “We are heading off to dinner. Join us.” 

     

    “If you don’t mind I will, dad.” - Kara nodded, smiling back at her father. Despite all their differences they were still her parents and Kara was still their daughter. - “I need to catch up with everything that has happened during my absence.”

     

    “Of course, you could hardly be expected to know what happens here when you are out there in space, performing your Guard duties.” - Alura said with resentment in her voice. Kara always knew her mother hated her job and would prefer to have her close to teach her how to rule The Exodus and be a proper member of The House of El, and not the vagabond she was in the eyes of her parents. Kara brushed it off though, it wasn’t something new to her and she just remained silent, looking at her mother expectantly. 

     

    “Don’t be so harsh, Alura. She has a job that is important to her, and like all young people, she needs a bit of space.” - her father, Zor-El, said calmly, patting his daughter on the back while they came down the stairs to the dining room.

     

    They sat around the big table, at a significant distance from each other and Kara watched how the servants started to move quickly around it, putting utensils before them and bringing them drinks and appetisers. For some reason Kara hoped that Lena, that young woman they arrested a few hours ago, also got her dinner already, because she complained that she was hungry. Kara made a mental note to check up on that girl and make sure she is being treated well. She just bit into the aryc’hock - a starchy vegetable from Angor and savoured its tangy taste, when Alura moved uncomfortably in her seat and mumbled something under her nose.

     

    “What are you saying, my dear?” - Zor-El asked, tilting his head in the direction of his wife.

     

    “I said that she is not so young anymore!’ - Alura repeated loudly. A servant that poured Kara’s drink jumped a little and spilled a few drops of water on the table. Kara waved him away, smiling at him kindly when he started to quietly apologise to her. She sighed heavily and continued eating, hoping that the sound of chewing would muffle her mother’s voice. It has been happening every year for the last twenty years, and her mother never failed to disappoint Kara by bringing up this matter in their first conversation of the year. 

     

    “Come on, Alura, she is! We live a long life, and there is no point in rushing forward to find a mate and produce an offspring nowadays, when young people want to live free for a longer time.” - Zor-El tried to dissipate the tension but Alura was still focused on it, looking at Kara, who seemed not to pay any attention to her and calmly dug into the tender ribs of a bisuu’n. 

     

    “It is not about a lifespan, it is about prestige! We are the ruling family of The Exodus and Kara is our daughter. And believe it or not, the rumours about her involuntary celibacy are spreading to other planets, too.” - Alura started to sniffle angrily, growing even more irritated by Kara’s complete lack of emotion about her rant. She just enjoyed her food, sucking the bone clean and gulping down a delicious vegetable stew that was brought to her as a side dish. - “Are you finished?” 

     

    “Yes.” - Kara answered abruptly and got up, wiping her hands and feeling at least a little bit less miserable than she was on an empty stomach. She sighed and walked towards the high double doors that opened before her, letting her inside the room with a big screen on the wall. On both sides of it were rows of small pods, filled with transparent liquid. Two of them hummed softly and were lit up by a warm yellow light. Kara squinted her eyes looking into them and saw an embryo inside each. The first one was just a few weeks old, the other seemed like it was just a few days from its birth and would be making someone happy. She heard her mother sighing heavily behind her back.

     

    “Hu-Ul family will get their baby soon.” - Alura cooed, walking towards the pod with a bigger embryo and looking at it tenderly. - “There are some settlers that have returned from Braal. There are some young people with them.”

     

    “They are the same people that lived there while I ran this matrix repeatedly year-after-year, mom.” - Kara mumbled, trying to prepare her mother for another disappointment.

     

    “The matrix will find a match for you not only based on your genotype but also on your personality. People can change, maybe there will be someone who is now a better match to you personality-wise. Just try it again.” - Alura looked stressed.

     

    She was horribly anxious every time Kara ran the matrix, and Kara just felt bad for her mother and was disappointed in herself again. Why couldn’t she be a decent daughter and make her parents happy, instead of being a renegade and a loser that cannot get laid even with this huge ass machine looking for someone for her? Kara grew angry. She hated this machine passionately, wishing that it never existed along with the strict Kryptonian customs and rules that made not only her life a hell but also her family’s. 

     

    She huffed angrily, sounding so much like her mother and squeezed her fists looking at the huge screen of the matrix. She cursed it in her head, but despite all her ill wishes it was still standing there, huge, cold and indifferent to her emotions. Her tense and angry silhouette reflected back at her from  the dark surface of its screen. Kara looked at it and sighed again, gathering her courage before she would disappoint her parents and run away in shame from here once again.

     

    She dropped her palm on the hand-shaped hollow on the control panel of the matrix and watched red Kryptonian letters appear on it, sorting her genome through the countless people that were on The Exodus right now. Three and a half million - it will take some time, Kara thought and took her hand off the panel, rubbing on the place where the needle that took a drop of her blood penetrated her skin.

     

    “Rao!” - she heard her mother exclaim in almost ecstatic happiness. - “There is a match! We got a match! Oh thank you, Rao!” 

     

    “What?” - Kara blinked dumbfoundedly a few times, looking at her parents who hugged each other, Alura wiping a tear of happiness from her eyes.
