She turned around slowly, afraid that she was being played by the device and the words “ match found… retrieving mate’s information… ” would disappear as fast as it appeared, but they didn’t, the matrix was still going through the database and looking for the full profile of her future spouse. Kara felt dizzy all of a sudden, cold sweat forming on her brow. She honestly never thought that she would be matched with someone and, if she had held the faintest hope it would happen at all, she never would have thought this day would come so soon.

     

    “INFORMATION FOUND” - Kara’s breath hitched. 

     

    “KARA ZOR-EL MATCHED WITH…

     

    LENA LUTHOR.

    SPECIES: HUMAN

    FELON NUMBER: 16975

    BIRTH PLACE: EARTH, 201480 HOURS AGO

    RESIDENCE: MEGASTRUCTURE

    AFFILIATION: THE SAWFLIES

    HEIGHT: 5’51/2”

    WEIGHT: 135 LBS

    SOLD AS A SLAVE TO AMARII KEF’ARII 196224 HOURS AGO.”

     

    The matrix was ruthless, spewing out words, each of which were like nails in Kara’s coffin. She thought she had been prepared for everything, but not this. There were Kryptonians that were matched with the other species throughout the years, but it was a rare occurrence, and it usually shocked the society deeply. She heard the happy cries of her parents stopping abruptly when they finally looked at the screen. 

     

    They stood in silence, Kara holding her breath, afraid to turn around and see the faces of her parents. After a few long moments she finally tore her eyes off the cruel screen and looked at Alura and Zor-El, who stood behind her, silently clutching each other in an anxious embrace. They both looked baffled, shocked and horrified at the same time, both still clinging to each other as if it would ground them somehow. Her mother was first to come to her senses. She tore herself off Zor-El and walked towards the matrix, never taking her eyes off the screen. 

     

    “Who is she?” - Alura asked tensely, pressing her lips in a thin line and looking at Kara with an unreadable expression.

     

    “We brought her on The Exodus today for interrogation.” - Kara mumbled, still shocked by everything that was happening.

     

    “She is a criminal?” - Alura watched Kara’s face like it might give her the answer of why her daughter was matched with a human from Megastructure.

     

    “We don’t know for sure, mom. There was a robbery at the warehouse and Kryptonite was stolen, but it seems like she doesn't know anything about it and I tend to believe her.” - Kara answered sincerely, looking at her mother who seemed to go through all the stages of grief within a few seconds, though it looks like she paused at “the acceptance”. 

     

    Kara exhaled in relief when she saw her mother’s face relaxing. Maybe everything happening is not too bad, Lena seems like a nice person and maybe if she is agreeable, Kara could try to work things out with her after she serves her term for her other petty robberies - maybe just a dozen years or so.

     

    “So she is a criminal.” - Alura deadpanned suddenly, her face hardening and eyes becoming cold and ruthless. Kara had already seen her like this back on Krypton, when her mother had to choose who would leave the exploding planet and who would stay to die.

     

    Alura walked towards the exit of the room and pushed the doors open. Kara ran behind her, trying to understand what she was up to. She was scared, maybe for the first time in her life she was terrified of her own mother. She knew her her whole life and at the same time she could say that she didn’t really know her at all. Alura was unpredictable when angered and it was terrifying. She ran around her mother and stepped in front of her, trying to stop her, but Alura just walked past her daughter and strolled forward like an unstoppable force. Kara had to almost run full speed at her side to keep up with her. 

     

    “Mom, what are you going to do?” - Kara asked, her heart beating too fast and her breath uneven, but not from running, she could run for miles and never get tired. She was scared for that poor girl from the Megastructure, who was now dragged into their family drama against her will, a fact that made her even more stressed out.

     

    “I want to see her, that’s all.” - Alura cut Kara off and called the guard. He appeared suddenly right before them, took Alura’s quiet order and disappeared quickly and quietly. 

     

    Not even an hour had passed since Kara’s matrix disaster, and Lena was already brought to the Palace and rudely thrown before the two chairs that stood on the high pedestal where her parents sat, awaiting her arrival. She winced when Lena fell down with a thud and yelped in pain from the harsh landing. Kara looked at her mother and saw her looking back, watching her closely. She smiled when Kara didn’t move to help Lena up, and it made her feel like the worst person in the whole universe. She felt like a coward - too stunned to move and too scared to step up for someone. She was so consumed by her own misery and so absorbed in the fact that her whole life had just turned upside down that she didn’t help the person who was unwillingly dragged into the weird customs of an alien race. 

     

    “She is quite beautiful.” - she heard her mother’s voice and turned to her swiftly, horrified at how detached she sounded.  - “But a human and a criminal too? This is your match, Kara Zor-El?” 

     

    “Maybe she is not such a bad person?”- Zor-El mumbled, uncertain.

     

    “Mom, stop.” - Kara tried to argue but her mother didn't even turn to her when she spoke.

     

    Lena tried to get up, looking disoriented for a moment. The guard jerked her up abruptly and pulled her up, to kneel before Alura’s seat. Her arms were once again twisted behind her back, now tighter than when Alex had chained her up. She looked battered, tired and still hungry and it filled Kara with deep regret. She still wasn’t fed after all the hours in jail. Her big green eyes sank and lips were cracked from dehydration. Kara cursed herself again, for letting her mother get into her head and run her damned genome through the matrix, condemning this poor girl to her. 

     

    “What the fuck is going on?” - Lena’s voice was raspy from both thirst and staying silent for hours in jail.

     

    “And potty mouthed, who would have guessed.” - Alura said, even more disappointed. - “Not only is she a crook but also a brute. How could it be, dear daughter, that you got matched with a dirty little animal like her?” 

     

    “She is not an animal, mother.” - Kara snarled, growing angry at last. She was a very patient and kind person, but when she reached her limit she could become a monster. She caught Lena’s questioning, desperate gaze, and felt anger coming up from the depths of her whole being. 

     

    “Well, Kara, I am just trying to understand and failing.” - Alura said, shaking her head. Kara never saw her mother behaving this fake, this vile and malicious before. Or maybe she did, but she always buried it deep inside, under all the feelings she had for them, all the love and admiration. The sudden realisation hit her like a truck - she always tried to justify the actions of her parents, but if she peeled all the feelings away, she would understand that her mother is petty and malicious and her father is weak and controllable. - “Why would the mating matrix match you with… this?”

     

    “What?” - Lena yelped, still lost about what was going on around her.

     

    “Do not involve her in this!” - Kara growled dangerously, staring her mother straight in her eyes.

     

    “Oh, do not worry, I will not. I don’t want her involved in your life at all.” - Alura said slowly, and Kara felt dread coming up from her stomach again. - “You, girl. You are guilty of raiding our warehouses and stealing Kryptonite.”

     

    “I didn’t do it!” - Lena yelled, angry and helpless, standing on her knees before the ruthless ruler of a half-god people.

     

    “I sentence you to be exiled into the Phantom Zone.” - Alura deadpanned, her face straight and eyes cold. 

     

    “Mother, no!” - Kara shouted in horror. She looked at her mother like she saw her for the first time in her life.

     

    “Be grateful I did not sentence her to be disintegrated.” - Alura said, her tone even but her eyes were vicious. - “I will wipe her from your record whether you want it or not.”

     

    “What is going on? I didn’t do it, you fuckers!” - Lena yelled hysterically, her eyes teary. She was terrified, and Kara wanted to protect her, even if she was a petty criminal. Now she was suffering for the archaic customs of an alien race and Kara was the one to blame. 

     

    “She will be exiled tomorrow at dawn. You can say your goodbyes if you so wish.” - Alura said, her tone too calm and eyes too cold.

     

    “We still don’t have all the evidence that she did it, mother! Let me investigate!” - Kara begged like a coward, unable to confront her parents. She felt weak and pathetic. The mighty Commander of the Space Guards, the ultimate protector of the whole galaxy, one of the most powerful creatures in the known world, she now stood before her mother, weak, whiny and trembling and begged for the life of an innocent girl. If she could she would punch herself straight in her lofty mug. She turned to Lena who was still there on her knees with her hands tied behind her back. Tears flowed down Lena’s face, her lower lip trembling and spine hunching under the weight of all the charges that were laid against her. - “I will find the truth and then, if she is innocent, we will let her go and will never see her again. Please!”

     

    “No, my dear. The verdict is final. I don’t want her to stain your perfect image, Kara. I will not be shamed before our council members just because you happen to be a match with a dirty little animal like her.” - Aura pressed her lips in a thin line and waved to the guard who jerked Lena to stand up and almost dragged her out of the Palace. 

     

    “It is not fair, mother.” - Kara said slowly, but in a tone that clearly indicated how she now viewed her mother - petty, prideful and vile. 

     

    “It’s a shame, really. I want you to be happy, Kara, and this girl just happens to be in the way. But do not worry, dear, she will not be a problem anymore.”- Alura looked her daughter in the eyes and Kara saw nothing but emptiness in them, and it terrified her. 

     

    Kara turned around and walked out of the palace, her legs stiff. She forgot to breathe this whole time and, when finally outside, she wheezed in the cool artificial breeze that blew straight into her face, making her choke on it. She looked at the road and caught sight of the pod that was already on its way back to the Hall of Justice. Ironic. There was no justice in her mother’s verdict, only petty malice aimed at the person who deserved it the least. She shouldn’t be involved in this in any way, it is only Kara’s problem. It is all her family’s business and nobody should ever suffer because of her inability to cope with her own personal life and relationship with her parents. 

     

    She gritted her teeth so hard she heard her jaw creak and ran down the street in the direction of the jail. It was a long way down the wide streets, but she didn’t care, she should clear her head before she did anything rash and stupid. People stopped and watched the Commander running like she was chased by demons, her face red and eyes teary. She didn’t pay any attention to them, looking at the grey Hall of Justice building in the distance with determination. She felt her muscles straining, showing the lack of sun radiation that gave her strength like on the planets that were illuminated by a yellow sun. The Exodus free floated in the void of the cosmos, in between several solar systems and didn’t have its own sun. It was probably for the best, because no universe would need a super powered nation, especially with people like her mother. Kara’s breath hitched again from the thought but she swallowed it bitterly and ran even faster, despite pain rippling through her muscles and lungs.

     

    She stopped in front of the Hall of Justice, her wide chest heaving from deep, rapid breathing. The gloomy grey building looked like a concrete block and the mere sight of it suffocated her. Kara stood there for a couple of minutes and walked further down the street, towards the barracks. It wasn’t too far away from the Hall of Justice and housed a lot of Space Guards as well as the sentries of The Exodus that formed a local police force. It had a Police Department right near it, a small unsightly building with a large parking lot for police shuttles and pods for the quick assessment of a crime scene. 

     

    She passed a few bored sentries who jumped to their feet to greet her. Kara wanted to bow her head down low and hide from them, because the last thing she deserved right now is the respect that they gave her. She winced, fighting with those feelings but nodded in acknowledgement anyway, remembering that no one is at fault for the mess of emotions she is enduring right now and no one deserves to be treated badly just because she feels bad. 

     

    Kara passed through several doors until she reached the living quarters of the higher-ranking officers of the Guards. Not a lot of them still stayed in the barracks, having better salaries and families, but those who stayed here were accommodated with all the needs they could have. The rooms were spacy and cosy, furnished nicely and even had separate showers in each apartment. She walked towards the door that led to Alex's apartment and stopped here, unsure what to do, her hand on the doorknob and heart racing. She chewed her lower lip, remembering that Alex is also dead sure that Lena was guilty. But Alex was her sister, even if not by blood but by choice, and she deserved to know that Kara still loves her and values their bond no matter what she does next. She held her breath and knocked.

     

    “Come in!” - Kara heard Alex shouting from somewhere within her living quarters. She pushed the door open and walked into the cosy room. Alex waved at her from the kitchen and turned back to cooking something that smelled nice. - “Steak kabobs for dinner today. Wanna join? I can share!”

     

    “No thanks, I already ate.” - Kara answered quickly, stopping Alex’s hand from taking another plate. 

     

    “Are you sure? That’s not normal, knowing your appetite.” - Alex laughed and looked at Kara’s face. Seems like she noticed something that Kara couldn’t hide despite all the effort she poured into keeping her face less sour. - “You look off. Oooh! Is it dinner with the parents? Went like usual?”

     

    “Yeah. Went like usual.” - Kara lied, hiding her eyes by looking at the shelf with the collection of spices like she saw it for the first time in her life. 

     

    “I hope they will understand someday that you are your own person and will stop pestering you with this matrix shit.” - Alex loaded her plate with kabobs and walked towards the dinner table in the middle of the guest room. She waved to Kara to join her at the table and dug into her food. - “As if you don’t have your own feelings.” 

     

    “It’s the custom of our race.” - Kara mumbled, sighing deeply.

     

    “I know. I just want you to be happy.” - Alex said, continuing to chew on her kabobs. She didn’t see how Kara winced. Her parents just said the same words and they meant nothing. People who want Kara to be happy for some reason also want to hurt others to do it, and it is not the way Kara would like to live her life. She sighed and looked at her sister, who was finishing her meal and pouring herself another cup of sparkly water. - “And don’t get me wrong, I respect the customs of your people, I live here after all. And I am grateful, I…”

     

    “Alex.” - Kara stopped her and put her hand on Alex's wrist. Alex stopped chewing and looked at Kara’s hand first, lifting her gaze to watch Kara’s eyes after. - “I just want you to know that whatever happens next - you will always be my sister and I will always love you.”

     

    “What are you talking about, Kara?” - Alex turned to her, puzzled. 

     

    “Things just became more complicated.” - Kara said, evasively. She already saw the distrust in Alex’s eyes and couldn’t blame her. - “I am just asking you, if something would happen, if one day you wake up and see me in a different light just… just try to trust in me. I just ask you to believe that everything I will do is for a reason. And I know you follow the letter of the law, but there is more to life than that. Life is so much more complicated and sometimes you understand that the rules that people follow are unjust and pernicious, but you are unable to change anything so you just go with the flow or try to do something.”

     

    “And what are you trying to do?” - Alex squinted her eyes, looking at Kara as if trying to read her mind. - “Kara?” 

     

    “Nothing. I’m not doing anything, don’t worry. I just... I was sad and came to you to whine. I am sorry, Alex.” - Kara smiled at Alex and got up, patting her sister on the back. - “See you tomorrow.”

     

    She stood up and walked to the door and touched the doorknob but stopped for a moment and turned back to look at Alex. Her sister still sat at the table, looking at her with a weird expression. Kara couldn't read it, was it fear? Disappointment? Irritation? Sadness? Maybe even all at once. She gave her one last sad smile and left.

    LOG.4: prison break

    Chapter Summary

    Kara goes against her parents and their unjust decision about Lena.

    Chapter Notes

    The evidence repository was dark and empty when Kara went in, unlocking it with her universal key. She knew she was leaving traces behind but couldn’t care less, only focusing on what is ahead of her, and pushing the fact that she will be forced to answer for her actions to the very back of her mind. She quietly prowled between the rows of boxes filled with stuff from both current and older cases, neatly marked and sorted by years. 

     

    Kara pondered why the newest cases got their stuff stacked in the back of the repository, when she stumbled over a box with something rattling inside of it. She jumped over it, landing on her feet without a sound and crouched, afraid that she would awaken a sleepy sentry at the door. She wriggled behind the farthest racks and sat there for a few moments listening to their heartbeat outside the door. She started to move again only when she hadn't heard the sentry moving. 

     

    Kara finally found what she wanted in a corner, between the boxes. A black sniper rifle, made of scrap and lovingly refined by skilled hands. It had a long rectangular barrel, thick and heavy, made from some kind of compound metal pipes. It also had wide grips, finished with leather from an unknown animal, gritty to the touch, which made it non-slippery if the shooter's hands got damp. It had a weird box-like magazine, not too big in size, but with enough capacity to hold a few dozen of the nails that served as the ammo of this weapon. The stock, with adjustable cheek, was wrapped with softer leather strips for a better grip. The night vision scope was the only thing that looked like it was bought instead of being made from scrap. 

     

    It took Kara a few minutes to get out of the repository and sneak out of the Police Department, carrying a stolen sniper rifle. It helped that she also got her hands on two dark cloaks and covered the bulky weapon under it when she passed a few sentries that gaped at her while she walked by, holding her head high. Kara only prayed that she would have enough time to do what she had decided to do. She walked towards the Hall of Justice building and stopped there again, holding her breath and trying to calm her racing heart. 

     

    What she will do next will be a felony, but if she won’t do it she will never forgive herself. Swallowing down her fear and anxiety, Kara stepped into the building, her face stony and her steps confident. She passed a few doors without meeting anyone, but there were a few sentries in front of the jail entrance. Kara ordered them to stand down and leave her so she could further interrogate their latest detainee alone, and only return when she’s finished.

     

    She didn’t have much time now and prayed that Lena will not be overly stubborn and agree to her proposal quickly. The inside of the jail was dark, only one weak light source was throwing shadows on the furniture inside. Kara pressed her palm to the sensor and opened the door, hoping that nobody moved Lena to another place. Luckily the human girl was still in that cell, and Kara exhaled in relief, thanking Rao that her biggest fear didn’t happen and she was still here and in one piece.

     

    Lena sat in complete darkness, her back pressed to the cold concrete wall and her knees pulled to her chest. She audibly sobbed when Kara opened the door and moved deeper inside, scared that she would be abused again. Kara squeezed her fist in helpless anger against her mother and everyone who was eager to take her orders and use them against an innocent person, just to curry her favour. She stepped inside the cell, looking at the girl who curled up in a ball on the cold dirty floor. Her heart squeezed painfully, watching a helpless woman being so scared from the mere sight of the Kryptonian. What have they become? Kara was always so eager to help everyone, to be a beacon of hope, to be a hero. But all she mustered to do was to let the abuse happen and to helplessly watch the unjust sentence being dropped on the woman who wasn’t even fully aware of what was happening.

     

    “I am not gonna hurt you.” - Kara stepped forward and crouched before Lena, looking at her with the most sincere eyes she could muster. She made the girl suffer and she will make it right, whatever it will cost her.

     

    “Good, because you did enough damage already.” - Lena spat, her voice trembling. It sounded like she had cried for a long time before Kara came and it made her feel even more of a piece of shit than a second ago. 

     

    “I know, and I want to make it right. I’m gonna get you out of here.” - Kara moved closer to Lena but the latter moved away from her, looking at her with angry eyes, reddened from prolonged crying.

     

    “How can I trust you? You are a cop, you live to destroy the lives of people like me.” - Lena hissed, wiping her damp nose with the sleeve of her armour and frowning at Kara. Her green eyes searched the face of the Kryptonian for any traces of malice.

     

    “I am your only chance now, Lena. I dragged you into my problems and my mother sentenced you to certain death just because you were in the wrong place at the wrong time. I am not letting you suffer for it. You don’t deserve any of it.” - Kara assured, taking Lena’s rifle from under her cloak and set it at her feet with the second cloak she took for her. - “We don’t have much time. Are you with me?” 

     

    “Okay, but if you betray me, you will find yourself with a nail in your eye.” - Lena hissed, taking the cloak and wrapping it around her shoulders and holstering the rifle behind her back.

     

    “It’s a deal.” - Kara agreed and urged her out of the cell, closing the door and walking towards the exit. She crouched at the exit, waving to Lena to do the same. They passed a few sentries, who seemed bored while patrolling and scanning the place lazily with their eyes. Kara didn’t want to hurt anyone on their way out but was still ready for anything, if the circumstances became dire. 

     

    They crouched behind the police station, following the path of the sentry who went to pour herself a drink. It was easy and familiar for Lena, who was smaller and much more lightweight than Kara, sneaking around with the familiar movements, quiet like a mouse. Kara really tried to not fuck it up, cursing the lack of yellow sunlight at The Exodus that would make it so much easier to run away; she would just take Lena in her arms and fly away.

     

    They finally crept to the entrance of the Hall of Justice and Kara stood up, seeing that they passed all sentries and there was not anyone in their way. She pulled Lena behind her and moved quickly to the landing zone, where a bunch of Space Guard’s shuttles were parked. Kara opened one of them and jumped inside, waiting for Lena to throw her rifle inside and do the same. Once Lena was safely inside, she flew them up and to the departure point of The Exodus, passing a bunch of shuttles on the way out. Every time she saw a police vehicle she held her breath and prayed to Rao they would not be stopped at any point. She asked Lena to pull up the hood of her cloak before stopping in front of a checkpoint and passed her biometrics through the system onto the border guard’s computer.

     

    “There is someone with you, Commander?” - she heard the uncertain voice of the border guard.

     

    “Yes, I am transporting a felon to another department.” - she said, trying to keep her voice strict and steady.

     

    “By yourself, ma’am?” - the guard sounded even less sure now.

     

    “Yes, it is an important matter, so I would like to avoid any further questions.” - Kara lied, making her voice harder. 

     

    “Yes, ma’am! Sorry, ma’am!” - the soldier saluted, leaving her alone. She thanked Rao for her status and growled “at ease”, before taking off into space. 

     

    She heard Lena letting out the breath she held for Rao knows how long and squeezed the wheel of her shuttle. She will take her far away from any problems she could have, maybe on some safe, far away planet where they don’t have jurisdiction and she could live her life peacefully. Kara looked at Lena’s regal profile, taking in how beautiful this girl was. She was paler than before and looked absolutely spent, dark circles around her tired eyes and reddened nose from prolonged crying. Kara wanted to reach for her, to touch her and at least show that she is here for her and she will not go anywhere. She took her hand off the steering wheel to reach out when she heard the crackling sound of the communication device on the front panel of the shuttle.

     

    “Kara Zor-El! The administration of The Exodus has ordered you to turn back and return a felon number 16975.” - the robotic voice declared with an indifferent tone. Kara felt the cold sweat dripping down her spine. She looked at Lena who was no less terrified, unsure what Kara would do next - betray her and give her back to The Exodus, or ignore them and go further with this little illegal mission. 

     

    Kara remained silent and flew to the jumping point, switching on the hyperlight engines, taking off into space. The black void welcomed them, cutting them off from any pursuers that could try to chase them. Kara wanted to take an easy breath, but she knew that they were still in danger. She wondered if she would be demoted, seeing how she basically broke a felon out of prison and kidnapped her. Maybe a few years of desk duty and she will get back to the field. She looked at Lena, who curled up in the seat near her. Was it worth it? Yes, absolutely, an innocent life is worth any trouble, even if she is demoted she will at least sleep better, knowing that she saved the girl from a fate she never deserved. Kara smiled slightly, looking back out of  the windshield, watching the stars passing fast before her eyes.

     

    “Kara Zor-El, administration of The Exodus has ordered you to turn back and return felon number 16975, or we will have to treat you like a fugitive.” - crackling voice returned and filled the small cabin again. It made them both jump and Kara looked at the shuttle panel in disbelief like it could explain what this voice meant. - “Kara Zor-El, you broke the law and The Space Guards and The Exodus will now treat you like a hostile.”

     

    “What the heck?” - Kara was sincerely shocked by this revelation. She stammered and looked at Lena with pure horror in her eyes. - “I am a Commander, how can they treat me like a hostile?”

     

    “What did you expect? You broke me out of prison!” - Lena laughed out loud. It sounded slightly hysterical. - “I am a convicted criminal. Commander or not, you still can be seen as my accomplice, especially by the stupid laws of your stupid fucking ship!”

     

    “I didn’t know they would just cut me off like this!” - Kara was still baffled, searching for a way to rectify this when she returns from taking Lena to safety. There should be a way, she is a Commander and a member of the House of El after all.

     

    “Stop whining! They can and they already did it, you dumbfuck. Now land us somewhere!” - Lena spat, looking at Kara angrily. Her eyes flicked from Kara’s face to the shuttle's control panel. Kara wanted to tell Lena off for calling her names but she felt the steering wheel resisting in her hands. The shuttle buckled to the side and she jerked the wheel up, to straighten it up but she was unable to. 

     

    “They remotely deactivated the shuttle.” - Lena sounded grim, looking at the dimmed lights of the vehicle's control panel. Kara squeezed her teeth together and jerked the wheel like it could help them somehow. 

     

    “They cannot do that, it is illegal!” - Kara growled, growing angry. She felt betrayed by her own people, by her own parents and by her own co-workers who just gave up on her so quickly, not letting her even defend herself before the unjust law and repent for the crimes they accuse her of committing.

     

    “Well they can.” - Lena shrugged. The shuttle jerked violently again, making her move in her seat like a ragdoll. She clung to her seat like a cat, looking at Kara with pity. - “Fuck it.”

     

    Kara watched Lena who suddenly crawled into her lap, squeezing between the ex-Commander and the steering wheel. She bent over it, tore the plastic panel from under the wheel and fumbled with the wires for a few seconds. She hummed, satisfied and jerked out the wire from her cyber implants, almost hitting Kara in the face with her elbow in the process. She slotted it into the electronics of the control panel and stopped, suddenly.

     

    “Does it have some kind of AI?” - she turned around in Kara’s lap, almost brushing her nose over Kara’s forehead.

     

    “This shuttle? N… no.” - Kara mumbled, stunned by the sudden closeness of this woman. 

     

    “Awesome.” - Lena turned around and froze for a few seconds. Kara saw the shuttle’s control panel lighting up again. - “I hacked it, but we don’t have a lot of options and it is not a permanent solution. I will direct it to the Megastructure, where we will be able to find some help.” 

     

    “Okay but there are Space Guards in Megastructure too. I doubt it will be safe.” - Kara said, thinking about their possibilities.

     

    “It is still better than just drifting in space. And I am not a navigator, I don’t know  any other place to go, so this will have to do for now.” - Lena answered discontentedly. 

     

    She fell quiet again and Kara watched her zoning out, seemingly diving deep into the electronics of the shuttle. She felt the vehicle straightening and taking off into deep space. It took Lena a few direction adjustments to find the right way and a few tries to switch on the hyperlight engine. Seemed like she got lost on the way to them, switching on and off a night vision screen, a kinetic barrier and even starting up a laser machine gun, but switched it off quickly with a quiet curse when she heard it whirling outside. Finally she seemed to be able to probe the controls of the engine and the ship abruptly jumped forward. Kara felt Lena’s back pressing into her chest, it suddenly felt comforting. She tried not to touch Lena and not to make her uncomfortable so she just let her arms hang freely at her sides, which wasn’t the most comfortable position but at least the human girl should feel safe. Soon they saw an ugly planet in the distance. From space, Megastructure looked like an unformed piece of dung that was overflowing with the flies, with its uneven surface and countless spacecraft that flew in and out its many ports. Kara felt the shuttle rattling and looked at Lena with a question in her eyes.

     

    “I fried this tin can from the inside. I cannot control it anymore!” - Lena sounded panicky.

     

    “Can you at least make the engines run for a bit?” - Kara asked, all her insides doing cartwheels. She saw Lena zoning out again, her head nodding in tact with the vehicle rattle like she was a bobble head figurine. The panel was still lit up, despite the numerous “danger” icons beeping and flashing red at her face. 

     

    Kara spread her legs, letting Lena slide from her lap onto the cushion of her seat between her tights. This position felt inappropriate and made Kara flustered, having another person so intimately close but she chased these thoughts away. Indecent or not, this position gave her a possibility to grab the steering wheel and direct the shuttle to the surface of Megastructure. The planet grew so big, its higher levels were already out of its ozone layer and there were zero gravity zones in some open areas. Kara flew the shuttle to the surface, praying to Rao to find an opening, and she did, flying straight into the narrow entrance, far from any port. They were slowed down by the gravity but still flew faster than Kara would like for her comfort. Luckily, she was a good navigator and manoeuvred through the structure with relative ease. They dropped down at a dazzling speed, passing level after level, going miles and miles into the depths of the Megastructure. The deeper they flew, the steeper, narrower and more dangerous this opening had become. Kara pulled the wheel to herself, squeezing it into Lena’s chest and apologising quietly.

     

    She managed to align the shuttle with the surface of the planet finally, finding another opening in the structure, this time parallel to the ground. But it was very narrow, and caused her to go in a tunnel between the ugly grey concrete walls of the deep levels. She saw the tunnel warping at the end of the road and, after understanding that she would not be able to do anything, wrapped her arms around Lena and pressed her face into the dark hair.

     

    “I am sorry.” - she managed to say quietly, feeling how Lena pulled her wires back inside her headpiece. Then they crashed, the impact throwing both into the windshield and knocking Lena out instantly. Kara tried to shield Lena from the hardest blows, when their shuttle tumbled down like a broken toy. She wrapped the fragile human in her arms like in a cocoon and thanked Rao for the fact that Lena was significantly smaller and curled in her lap like a kitten, allowing Kara to keep her body mostly unharmed, not counting a concussion and a few bruises here and there.

     

    The shuttle managed to land much softer than Kara expected, leaving her a way to crawl out of the wreckage without even using her laser vision. She wiggled out from under Lena’s limp, unconscious body, kicked the door off the hinges and crawled up and outside. The ship crashed on its side and Kara thanked Rao again for the fact that Lena sat on her lap when they went down. The side where the passenger seat was supposed to be looked smashed completely, the seat deformed  and buried under the collapsed roof. Kara plunged into the broken remains of the shuttle and grabbed Lena by the hands, then taking her under the armpits and pulling up. 

     

    She never heard someone coming from behind, concealed in an invisibility shield. She felt a sharp pain in her spine and let Lena go, unable to hold her grip on her, her arms and legs convulsing uncontrollably. She managed to turn on her back, between the attacks and saw a creature she was never even aware existed anywhere. Part alien and part machine, it held a forked staff, charged with electricity, its empty, glassy eyes looking at Kara impassionately. Kara tried to get up but was attacked again, feeling all muscles in her body tensing up and leaving her unable to move or even breathe. She screamed out in pain, fear and anger, trying to get back at least some of the control over her body but failed, falling into the comforting darkness of unconsciousness.



    Chapter End Notes

    And there their adventures together begin =D

    LOG.5: silicon life

    Chapter Summary

    Kara and Lena are captured by the cyborgs and try to get their way out of it, and also understand their feelings about each other.

    First thing that Lena saw, when she finally came back to her senses, was two metallic sticks that moved right in front of her eyes, making a “cling-clang” sound when they hit the concrete walkway. She winced from the pulsing pain that dwelled just behind her eyes. She must have hit her head when they crash landed. Lena moved her fingers and wiggled her toes, feeling relief that she isn’t paralysed or in any pain other than that rippling headache. She was still in a horribly uncomfortable position - someone held her by the arm and just dragged her on the ground through the structure like a broken doll. She tried to wiggle out of their grip but they squeezed her arm tighter, making her yelp in pain. She tilted her head, straining all her neck muscles and looked at her captor. Apparently, they felt her moving again because they turned their head to her and stared back at Lena.

     

    It was a cyborg. Lena only knew about them from the tales of those people who saw them occasionally, in the deepest places of the Megastructure, in its darkest corners. They were the remains of the ancient race that lived on this planet. When the robots built level upon level, cutting the old race out of the surface and burying it under tons of concrete, they survived, without sunlight, without food or water. Abandoned by everyone on the lowest levels of Megastructure, they were forced to endure by any means necessary. Some of them formed tribes that survived on the remains of the civilisation, some turned to technology, implanting themselves with tech that helped them to live on, but, in the end, becoming less and less of a person. 

     

    These silent, weird creatures rarely interacted with people and, even more rarely,  were seen anywhere outside the depths of the structure. They were self-sufficient enough to ignore the outer level world and could make their own tech easily. The only stuff they were after, when they came up, were body parts of living beings. They ignored the black market, filled with cloned or original organs though, and resorted to kidnappings and torture, which made them despicable creatures that were hunted and killed on sight by the living. 

     

    The cyborg that, at this moment was dragging Lena, was tall, its bald, translucent skull showing parts of its wired, pulsing brain. It had a bulky body, with long arms and thick legs. One of its arms had a built-in laser machine-gun, so big it almost dragged behind the creature and made it look like it had one arm longer than the other. Its companion was small and lanky, its legs looked like sticks that didn’t have knees and it looked like it walked on stilts. Its arms were also as thin as sticks but both ended in human-like hands with fingers. 

     

    Lanky one turned its head and looked at Lena too, its big round eyes rotating wildly. Its wide mouth, filled with at least two rows of needle-like teeth that almost divided its face in two, formed some kind of expression that looked like a smile. Lena couldn’t decide which one of them was uglier - the first one wasn’t a beauty either. It had some resemblance of a face on its translucent head. It was mostly covered in metal, small red dots in place of its eyes, glowing between grids and slots of an unknown functionality. Instead of a mouth it had a gritted filter that puffed in and out rare, loud breaths. 

     

    It seemed like they finally arrived, because Lena was abruptly and unceremoniously thrown into the closest wall. She managed to fall on her side and roll, avoiding taking any damage. She saw Kara being thrown beside her like a sack of potatoes, still unconscious and poked her with her leg, trying to wake her up, to no avail. She saw one of the cyborgs playing with her rifle, but then it got bored with a simple makeshift weapon and threw it away, into the pile of goo that pulsed in the corner of the room. Lena winced, watching her treasured rifle sinking into the goo, the dirty liquid filling all its crevices and staining the leather. She followed the direction of the growth with her eyes and gasped.

     

    From the wall of the structure hung a huge, blob-like creature, its malformed body covered in blisters and growths, its thick guts spilling out on the streets and pulsating, running something thick and nasty through them. It had distinct body parts - a huge head with goggled eyes and a thick, tumorous torso. It didn’t have any limbs, instead a thick growth spreading out of it and going through the wall, keeping in hanging on it like a disgusting, bloody tinder fungus. It rolled its eyes, trying to catch a glimpse of the newcomers and to Lena’s surprise opened the hole in its head that looked like a mouth with sporadic, but very pointy teeth. 

     

     “Well, well, well, what have we here?” - its voice was hoarse and something bubbled in its throat, making it sound like the creature was drowning in its own phlegm. - “If it isn’t Kara Zor-El, the runaway Commander?”

     

    “Leave her alone, you freak.” - Lena stood up, but not without battling sudden dizziness that was still present after her concussion. 

     

    “How dare you speak like this with The Governor?’ - the creature sounded hysterical, which would be hilarious if Lena wasn’t concerned that they would be killed within minutes. Well, if she goes down now, she will go with dignity, at least trying to remain as sassy as she could. Her only hope was that when the cyborgs dismember her, they at least do it after she is dead.

     

    The Governor was more of a mythical figure to most of the Megastructure’s population, a boogeyman from children’s lullabies. Local Naltorians had a rhyme, it went something like…

     

    Ding-dong The Governor appears

    Cut off your arm, tear off your leg

    Don’t stick around and try to beg

    Uh-oh little baby, scram away 

    before he takes your head as a souvenir.

     

    Standing before the creature now, Lena  understood that the tales and myths were true. According to them, The Governor was the remnant of the old race, just like the cyborgs. Long ago he was the governor of the old race, not a huge one, just some kind of Mayor of one part of Megastructure, when it was still separated by sectors that formed different cities. Then they were built upon by the Builders and left to die, buried under the thick layers of concrete. With the technological advances of these times, they managed to stay alive, enhancing themselves with tech.

     

    First they used cyber implants to just survive longer, not so different from those Lena had. Then hunger, lack of other resources and even a simple sunlight, turned them into a more exclusively cybernetic race. The Governor, though, chose to try other kinds of enhancement - genetic ones. The cyborgs gathered genetic material from other races and integrated it with his body, making him taller, stronger, disease resistant and altering his metabolism. In the past they actively hunted people, just to get the genetic material for him, making every new arriving race at the Megastructure a target.

     

    Humans were one of the rare species that were not used as a genetic material, their bodies unable to handle mutations like the old race of Megastructure could. Every mutation in humans, with the rarest exceptions that could make one metahuman, led to disability or tumorous growths and, thus, death in another. They weren’t safe from being kidnapped anyway, them being a good resource of flesh for the cyborgs, or silicon life, as they called themselves, pointing to their non-carbon life form origins. Countless humans were abducted from Megastructure’s upper levels throughout the years and were never seen again.

     

    “God, you are ugly.” - Lena mumbled under her nose, hoping that they would end her and Kara fast. She wasn’t sure it would work, judging by the glassy indifference that she saw in surrounding cyborgs’ eyes. She continued to try her luck because, even if she is not able to talk their way out of it, maybe she can prolong their conversation enough for the Kryptonian to wake up  - “What do you want from us?”  

     

    “From you? Nothing!” - he laughed out, hysterically, his entire tumorous body shaking and leaking goo. - “From her, however… I can do a lot of things with her. Do you know that The Exodus offers three million credits for her, dead or alive?”

     

    “Why do you need credits?” - Lena scoffed. The cyborgs never went to the upper levels, and having credits for them was like buying a ton of toilet paper when they didn’t even have an ass to wipe.

     

    “You are right, we don’t need credits, but we can trade her for cybernetics or human parts.” - The Governor suddenly fell silent, its googly eyes bobbing from side to side. It looked so ugly yet kind of hilarious. - “Or I can use her genetic material and throw the rest away for the cyborgs to have. I’ve never had a Kryptonian in my genotype, I heard they are really strong.” 

     

    “You heard wrong, they are a sickly, dying race.” - Lena lied, looking him straight in the eyes. 

     

    “Tsk, tsk, such a little liar.” - The Governor wouldn’t have it. To Lena’s horror he also seemed to get bored with her, his bulbous eyes closing lazily. - “Get lost from my sight, bug. You have annoyed me enough. Now you will serve your purpose as  spare parts for my people. Maybe your pretty face will look better on someone who is less talkative.” 

     

    “No wait! I know powerful people! If I say the word they will trade with you!” - she was cut short by the bulky cyborg who jerked her upright and led away from the Governor and Kara. The lanky one followed them, her thin legs clanking on the ground. Lena still managed to turn around and catch a glimpse of an unconscious Kara, who was dragged away in a different direction by several cyborgs.

     

    They walked for a few minutes, before entering a dark tunnel, leading down, one level lower. Lena never thought about how deep the Megastructure was, having only a vague idea of how huge this planet was and how many secrets it held in its depths. Judging by their current situation some secrets shouldn’t be revealed, because it can lead to dismemberment, abuse and death. They finally came out of the tunnel onto the wide street. A few buildings there still had doors, and the cyborgs headed for one of them, the least rusty one, its handle displaying signs of use. Lena felt like this is it - there is no coming back out of this door and she needed to think quickly. She looked at the bulky cyborg, who walked in front of her. It was tall, taller than she is by at least a foot, but he had a slot in the base of his skull. 

     

    Lena swallowed down thick saliva that formed into her mouth from a sudden wave of anxiety. There it was - her only chance. If she is quick and agile enough she will be able to connect to it and maybe, if she is lucky enough, even hack it. Luckily, the bulky one had a built in weapon which may come in handy. She subtly looked at the lanky one. It was weaker, smaller, seemingly broken and glitchy, its limbs twitching and knees buckling. It walked at some distance to the right, at the most convenient position she could think of. Lena held her breath - they underestimated her, and she will use it in her favour, or die trying.

     

    With a quick movement she pulled out her implant wire and pushed it into the taller cyborg’s slot. The connection was successful and Lena went into its consciousness like a hot knife through butter. This cyborg didn’t have a lot of living parts left, only some of its brain, as far as Lena could tell. Thanks to that it was quite easy to overcome its consciousness to force it to do her bidding. She grabbed onto the cyborg's non-weaponized hand to stay up and zoned out, her eyes rolling back, entering the mind of the cyborg and leaving her own body helpless, hanging from the cyborg’s body back to back. She looked through its eyes, turning it around so that it was protecting her own body, too. She saw the lanky one, looking at its companion, with big, round, surprised eyes. It giggled, barring all its teeth, its eyes rotating wildly. Lena lifted the cyborg's weaponized arm and fired, its laser weapon tearing through the metal of the lanky one’s body. It fell to the ground, twitching and screeching. 

     

    Now it was the bulky one’s turn. Lena searched for traces of some kind of self destruction protocol in its brain, but found none. Silicon life was made to survive and there was no other way to kill it but to fry its brain. It was extremely dangerous to interfere with the consciousness of a sentient being, be it a living creature or an AI. But it was her only chance to survive and to save that dumb Kryptonian. It was either death by being disassembled for organs and parts, or dying trying to survive and she took her chances with the latter, breaking into the cyborg's mind. She felt only minor resistance, going through the firewalls and hacking safety protocols, baring the mind of the cyborg and cutting into it, severing the connections and overloading its system. It went smooth so far, its body jerking slightly, while Lena destroyed its brain matter, making it bleed and slowly die.

     

    Just before its death, with the last drops of its conscience the cyborg retaliated, hot pain flashing before Lena’s eyes and making her squeal. She saw bright white, feeling the strongest wave of nausea and falling on her knees, the wires popping out of the cyborg's head. She didn’t hear its lifeless body falling behind her back, her insides turning and her head thumping with unimaginable pain. She choked on her own scream and fell, her body cramping in a seizure, nails scraping on dirty concrete. 

     

    Lena groaned and opened her eyes, rolling onto her back. Her entire brain pulsated painfully, she felt dizzy and disoriented. She felt a new wave of nausea and quickly turned to her side, not to choke on her vomit, but thankfully only dry heaved a few times, her stomach empty. She laid down, weak and angry at herself. She should have thought better before interfering with the sentient creature’s brain. It backfired and she was lucky to still be alive, though the extent of her own brain damage was still unknown to her. She tried to get up on all fours and failed, falling face down on the ground and battling nausea. She stayed down and relaxed her body, giving up on her life.

     

    She opened her eyes again, and felt that her dizziness had subsided significantly. She must have fallen asleep, exhausted from the pain, nausea and hunger. She felt only a little bit better, but it gave her hope to at least try to get up. She crawled to the wall and propped herself up, trying out the new position. Lena still wasn’t feeling good but at least she could stand upright and walk. She saw the lanky cyborg lying on the ground, looking at her with its round eyes, still with that stupid smile on its face. Lena tore herself off the wall, walking towards it’s broken body and kneeled before it. 

     

    The lanky one followed her movements with its eyes, screeching quietly, with an electronic trill. Lena tilted her head and looked at it, meeting its eyes briefly. She crouched before it, put her finger into the hole of the laser bullet in its head and pulled the plate up, baring its brain. Her eyes met the round cyborg ones, looking at her with a puzzled expression, following her every movement. She pushed her fingers deeper, rummaging through its brain matter until she felt something solid under her fingertips. She pulled out a chip, shook intracranial liquid off it and hid it in her pocket. Then she stood up on shaky legs and walked away, but then turned back and looked at the broken cyborg, torn apart by the laser bullets, meeting its eyes. It was still alive when she walked away.

     

    She walked back through the tunnel and onto the street, crouching in the darkest corners as soon as she exited the dark alley. She tried to be quick and silent, freezing behind walls and merging with shadows when cyborgs passed her by. There weren't a lot of them, but meeting even one meant the end of her plan and also her life. She circled the building with The Governor through the surrounding alleys, hiding in the rubble and sliding under the weird bridges and ladders that were scattered over the whole level. 

     

    Seemed like the Builder that made this place glitched at some point, making a surreal landscape within the concrete walls, creating a house with many doors but without one window, putting countless staircases that ended in solid walls. It looked like the feverish dream of a crazed architect, and Lena would never find her way around if she hadn’t been quick enough to download a map from the bulky cyborg’s mind. Now at least she had a vague idea where Kara could be held, and also how to enter the seemingly impenetrable structure. 

     

    She was nearing the door that should lead her to the living quarters, if that’s what you would call the place that most cyborgs hung out, according to the downloaded map and some half-corrupted memories when she saw her rifle laying on the ground. It must have been carelessly thrown on the ground by one of the cyborgs who lost interest in the crude weapon. Lena looked around, searching for any danger with keen eyes, but it was quiet, not counting the huge bulbous mass of The Governor, hanging from a wall like a big, ugly adornment. His eyes rotated widely and Lena couldn’t tell if he was scanning the streets or it was just his mutated body acting up. 

     

    She crawled closer to her treasured weapon, trying to pass the twitching and pulsing guts that spilled out of The Governor’s body without touching them and attracting his attention. She managed to grab her rifle and hide in the shadows just quick enough to evade the sight of two cyborgs, that walked past her, dragging the thick tube, with thick brownish goo stuck to it. The Governor turned his head with a satisfied groan and watched these two sticking the tube into the opening in his mess of a guts. Something poured through the tube right into his body with disgusting slurping sounds. Lena grimaced and, using his moment of his nasty pleasure as a distraction, crawled back to the door. 

     

    The insides of the building were empty, not counting a few overgrown roaches that ran away from her path with skittering sounds. Lena managed to wipe most of the goo off the most vital parts of her rifle and checked its magazine, which was intact and still filled with two dozen long nails. It was not a lot and Lena was pretty useless in a close fight with her heavy rifle, but it was a weapon with the ammo nonetheless. She placed it behind her back and tightened the strap on her chest, securing it on her back and quickening her steps. She still wasn’t sure if Kara would still be alive, perhaps already arrested by The Exodus, or taken apart to be the new ‘glorious’ mutation for The Governor. 

     

    She still couldn’t understand why she wants to help the Kryptonian, who just fucked up her life so royally that there was no coming back from it. She had everything  - a family, friends, warm house and food in her belly every day. One stupid raid, one stupid mistake and she was arrested and sentenced in a span of a few days, and then on top of it almost killed. She was also dangerously close to waking up in a vegetative state just a few hours ago. 

     

    Still, she was drawn to this woman, and ran to her rescue like a faithful dog. They hadn’t even spoken enough for Lena to feel at least a modicum of sympathy towards the Kryptonian, or even like her whatsoever. Lena shrugged and froze when she heard a quiet ruffle in the corner. It was just a roach again. She hummed in relief and walked further, looking at the doors and trying to find the lock that could be fitting for the chip she took from the lanky cyborg earlier. Maybe she doesn't like the Kryptonian with her stiff manners and stupid love for justice, but she will have a greater chance of survival with her.

     

    Lena finally found a few doors that looked like they could contain important stuff for the cyborgs and tried to unlock them with the chip. To her satisfaction, most of the doors opened with it and she searched through the rooms, finding nothing of interest to her. Mostly these rooms served as storage for the rusty parts that were too broken to be used somewhere, or nutrition paste that was already expired and smelly, overflowing from the rusty buckets that were already too bloated to contain the stuff inside. Lena heard a faint noise from one of the doors and unholstered her rifle, loading a nail into its barrel. It was too heavy for her to shoot from her arms, but it was still better than nothing. She pushed the chip into the small slot on the surface of the door, unlocked it with a quiet click and pushed it with her leg, coming inside, slowly, her weapon on the ready.

     

    “You came!” - the Kryptonian was restrained by chains with power dampening chipsets on every cuff. She had quite a derpy look on her face, grinning at Lena happily, her eyes sparkling with joy.

     

    “Fuck you!” - Lena snapped, coming closer. Kara seemed not to be bothered by Lena’s rudeness, and smiled at her softly. It made Lena scoff in disbelief. She turned her attention to the power dampening device and mused about how she could disable it. She aimed her rifle to the blue dot on one of the cuffs and shot it without further ado. Its light flickered and the cuff broke, freeing the Kryptonian’s leg. Lena repeated it with another and Kara broke off the arm’s restraints, feeling stronger with half of the devices dead. 

     

    “I never thought you would come. I… I am really happy you did!” - the Kryptonian rubbed her wrists shaking off the last pieces of her restraints and smiled at Lena widely. - “And I was worried about you. They could do something bad and I was too far and unable to help you.”

     

    “Well they wanted to do ‘something bad’, but I managed to escape.” - Lena scoffed, not even looking at Kara. She rubbed the nail she used to free the Kryptonian against the concrete wall, sharpening it and putting it back into the magazine. It was harder than she expected, her vision blurring for a moment and hands shaking. She fidgeted with the nail, cursing quietly, but fell quiet when she heard herself slurring her words. 

     

    “Lena? Lena! Are you okay?” - Lena finally heard Kara’s voice. It sounded weird, like through the water, a little bit distorted and muffled. Lena looked at Kara, disoriented slightly. She frowned, trying to remember what she wanted to do and looked at the rifle in her arms, confused. The magazine was open and the nail she was trying to put inside was on the floor. It seems like she had lost a few seconds of her life. She crouched and took the nail off the floor, putting it into the magazine with a fast movement and clapping the lid shut. - “Are you feeling alright? What did they do to you?” 

     

    “Yes. Yes! Nothing.” - Lena lifted her chin up and tried to stare Kara down. Her shorter stature made the attempt look pathetic but at least she tried. She threw the rifle over her shoulders and tightened the straps. - “Now move your hooves and let’s get out of here before they come and fuck you up again.” 

     

    “Your eyes were twitching and it looked like you were seizing a little. Is it normal for you?” - Kara pressed on, unsure what to do and how to behave around the girl that wanted to push her away, which wasn’t a surprise, considering their short but intense history. She didn’t want to let it go, despite Lena’s attempts to downplay it. Something changed in her, her face was incredibly tired looking, eyes sunken in and framed in dark circles, her lips pale, almost indistinct from the rest of her skin and cracked from dryness, and on top of that she also had the micro seizures. Kara’s pupils narrowed while she scanned Lena’s body for injuries. There was nothing she could see, no traumas, no internal bleeding.

     

    “No, it is not! I almost fried my brain on the way out of here. Now move!” - Lena snapped again, gritting her teeth. Last thing she needs now is the care and pity of this disgraced Goldilocks. She walked away fast, as if trying to run away from the prying eyes of the Kryptonian. Kara frowned but followed her closely, still watching her like a hawk. 

     

    “Before we go…” - Kara started again and Lena abruptly turned around. The look on her face was extremely angry, green eyes throwing thunder. Kara held her gaze and continued.- “I would like to deal with The Governor.”

     

    “Why? Why do you care what this creature does here in the depths of the shit planet you all, cops, hate so much?” - Lena asked, irritated.

     

    “Despite what you may think of me, I don’t hate anyone. The Governor is dangerous to those on the higher levels. You saw it with your own eyes, they kidnap people, they torture them. But we can end it now. Me and you." -  Kara looked into Lena's eyes with such sincerity, her clear blue eyes sparking with hope. Hope that Lena will be a decent enough person to follow whatever delusional quest she just created in her blonde head. 

     

    "There is no me and you, Kara ." - Lena laughed out, exaggerating the Kryptonian’s name mockingly. - "There is me and my will to get the fuck our of here alive. I understand that I have more chances with you around, and no matter how much I hate this fact, or you, I want to go home and preferably never see you again!"

     

    "I know that, but I also know that you are a good person…"  - Kara started, but was interrupted by Lena, who grew angrier with every word that came out of Kara’s mouth.

     

    "No! You don’t get to say who I am! You don't know me! Everything you have done is screwing over my life and dragging me through the dirt straight into hell! I risked my life for you, to get you out of this room, and that's enough for today!" - Lena yelled at the Kryptonian at the top of her lungs. 

     

    All the stress, all the anger and resentment poured out in a short but powerful fit. She gritted her teeth and looked at Kara as angry as she could, wishing that she could have that heat vision thing like Kryptonians. She had a faint understanding that she was yelling at a creature with god-like powers, the strongest alien in the galaxy that can just snap her in half with one finger, but it also brought her a great amount of angry satisfaction. She took in that silly facial expression of the Kryptonian who seemed to be torn between wanting to cry or to reach out for Lena, her arms already extending towards the crying human. Lena jumped away from her, huffing angrily and throwing a devastating gaze upon the alien.

     

    She suddenly felt dizziness and muscle spasms again, her eyes rolling up and her body becoming limp. She stepped back and, fortunately, felt the wall behind her, making it easier to stay upright while it lasted. She saw Kara jumping closer to her and extending her arms in her direction again, though respecting her previous wish not to be touched by her and only made sure she would be close enough to catch her if she would fall down.  

     

    “Shhh, calm down, calm down.” - she heard Kara cooing gently, like she was trying to calm down a kid who had a temper tantrum in the middle of a candy shop. - “We will go right now, Lena. I will take you home, just breathe.”

     

    Lena choked on the words that she wanted to spit out, and after a few vain attempts to speak just threw an angry gaze at Kara. No matter how angry it made her, she followed Kara’s advice and breathed deeply in and out for a few minutes, until she felt strong enough to tear herself off the wall and take a few careful steps around the room, to try out how it would go for her. She saw Kara following her every movement with her eyes closely, like she was afraid that Lena would go into seizure again. She now knew for sure that she damaged her brain, which messed her up slightly. But she still could stand on her own two feet, speak coherently and it didn’t affect her cognitive abilities, so Lena just shrugged it off, hoping that someday she will be able to fix the seizures with some additional implants. 

     

    Lena opened the door and tiptoed out of the room, looking around carefully. She felt the Kryptonian following her, her footsteps so quiet that if it wasn’t for her clothes ruffling and her loud breathing, she wouldn't even know she was there. Lena walked down the corridor onto the metal rack that conjoined two buildings together. If the first one was mostly empty, full of expired items and rusty parts, this one seemed to be used more often. Its rooms were full of supplies and the newest electronics, none of which looked used before or torn from the broken body of another cyborg or android. Kara moved in front of her, opening the doors and looking around the room, before letting Lena in. She wasn’t against it, feeling dead tired. She found a crate of cyborg food, taking a few protein bars out of it and biting into one instantly. She was ravenous. Of course, cyborg bars tasted like toilet paper and had the texture of dried out chalk, but it was nutritious food and Lena was not picky right now.

     

    One of the rooms was long and lit by red lights, which seemed weird. Cyborgs haven’t cared enough about their surroundings to change the interior or lightning anywhere else. Lena walked forward slowly, looking around. There was nothing of interest, only a few big jars with some non-transparent liquid in them. She rubbed the dull glass of one of the jars and tried to look inside, but couldn’t see anything through the thick yellow liquid. It looked disgusting enough for her to make a face and leave it alone, walking towards Kara, who suddenly stopped in her tracks. 

     

    She wanted to push the Kryptonian to walk further as rudely as she could, but saw what Kara saw and stopped abruptly. Under the red lights the whole room looked ominous, huge jars as high as a human stood near the walls, yellowish liquid bubbling inside. It was more translucent than at the entrance of this room so Lena could see clearly what was inside of them. There were human bodies in these, floating in liquid, some of them still intact, some already mutilated, their limbs and organs taken as spare parts for cyborgs. 

     

    There were also smaller jars, with humans carefully grown from embryo to young adult. Lena felt nausea coming up and tore her eyes off the horror that unfolded before her eyes. She stared at Kara's broad back covered with a long greyish-blue cloak, trying to swallow down the lump in her throat. Kara turned around and came closer, cutting off the scene from Lena’s eyes with her body. Lena didn’t know if it was intentional but was thankful anyway, carefully lifting her gaze to look into the sad blue eyes of the Kryptonian.

     

    “Now you understand why I want to destroy the Governor?” - Kara asked softly, looking into Lena’s eyes with strange tenderness.

     

    “You knew about it?” - Lena asked, her voice quivering and eyes watering. Nobody deserved a fate like this, not these people. They could be someone’s family or friend, they could grow free and happy. Instead they were just thrown into jars for organ harvesting, to be the spare parts of beings that lost their humanity long ago.

     

    “No, but I suspected as much.” - Kara answered simply and looked round, wincing, like the mere sight of it pained her. - “If they kidnap people for parts, then of course they would also have an organ farm somewhere. It just confirmed my theory.” 

     

    “I…” - Lena started but her voice betrayed her again and she took a deep breath before continuing. - “How will we do it?” 

     

    “From what I know, the cyborgs aren’t answering directly to The Governor, they answer to the AI.” - Kara said, thoughtfully, leading Lena out of the room by touching her shoulder lightly and guiding her, allowing her not to look around. 

     

    “The AI is long dead.” - Lena chewed her lip and looked at Kara quizzically.

     

    “Yeah, it is, but it seems like The Governor's body is bigger than we saw. I managed to X-Ray vision through the structure. I haven’t seen all of it, but what I managed to see is interesting - The Governor grew into it, spreading some kind of neuron-like growth inside all structures of this layer. And if my theory is right - he innervated the AI with his neurons and made it semi-sentient, so he could give orders to the cyborgs. We need to sneak closer to the AI’s body.” - Kara tapped on her chin, thinking about what would be the best plan for them right now.

     

    “I doubt it is far away, he needs to maintain constant connection to the AI and it is not easy to do in remote mode, no matter how many tentacles he’s sprouted over the years. I saw weird growths on the wall when they tried to escort me out of the level to kill. If we go further and find a way out of the building, we will be quite close to the place I’m talking about.” - Lena suddenly quickened her step and shot forward with Kara following her closely.

     

    She jumped out of the room swiftly but Kara, who first walked just a step ahead, lingered at the entrance to the room. She watched Lena with a thoughtful expression for a few moments and then turned back, looking into the red darkness of the room of horrors. She activated her heat vision, burning jars with the bodies, making the glass pop with the sickly sound. She worked through the room thoroughly, never missing a body, destroying them, giving them peace at last, as they all deserved. She turned to Lena finally, the whole red room engulfed in flames behind her back. Lena saw tears in her eyes and turned around fast, not to let the Krypronian see her own emotions. 

     

    It seemed like a long walk through endless corridors. They met a few cyborgs but made quick work of them, Lena shooting them through the skulls with her nail-ammo and Kara finishing the job melting them with her heat vision. To their surprise and relief they were right about the direction of the AI’s body - the thin meaty tumours started to appear on the floor and on the walls, growing thicker and covering more area the closer they came to their goal. Finally, they reached the opening in the structure and came out of the building.

     

    The lifeless body of the AI was right in front of them, its enormous, round, eye-like body hanging from the structures above it. Its lens was dull and four appendages that long ago worked as its hands were frozen in cramped positions, its joints rusty and light paint peeled off almost completely. Its backside, which was closer to the wall, was overgrown with sprouts of The Governors neurons. They pulsed erratically, causing the AI’s body to creak and slightly swing on its handles that fastened it’s body to the higher level. 

     

    It seemed like the cyborgs vaguely understood the concept of worshipping. The floor before the AI’s body was littered with human remains. Coming closer Kara and Lena could distinguish a pattern that the bodies were laid out in. It appeared to form some kind of code, the bodies arranged to form the unfamiliar signs. Wires and electronic spare parts were laid right before the AI, in a weird attempt to decorate the area. A few cyborgs were around it, some on their knees, praying, and others just wandering around, aimlessly. 

     

    Lena placed her rifle on the edge of a crumbled wall and rested her elbows on it, looking into the scope. She still had seventeen unbroken nails. She tried to recover most of her ammo after shooting other cyborgs, but a few fell victim to the Kryptonian’s heat vision anyway. She held her breath and pulled the trigger, watching two cyborgs fall to the ground, their heads pierced by a single nail. Other cyborgs panicked and started to scatter for their hideout. It didn’t take too long, and she saw a few of them aiming their weapons at her. 

     

    Lena shot two more before they understood what was happening, but then needed to retreat behind the wall, hearing hundreds of laser bullets impacting it. She saw Kara flying up and heard her heat vision buzzing, melting a few bodies away. She poked her head out and saw the cyborgs occupied with her temporary partner. She took a few out with her nails, to help Kara out, seeing her working relentlessly, circling in the air and shooting them one by one. Through her scope she saw that one of the cyborgs aimed a small pistol at her. It whirled softly, gathering power. 

     

    “Watch out!” - Kara yelled and shot towards this cyborg, knocking the pistol out of its hand before it could shoot. She melted the cyborg with her heat vision and threw its remains away with one hand, smashing it against the AI’s body. She turned around, looking carefully in all the crevices and dark corners but it seemed like no one was around anymore. She landed heavily on her feet and took the pistol in her hands, looking at it with mixed emotions. 

     

    “What is that?” - Lena had poked from over Kara’s shoulder, her chin resting on it. Kara looked at her, surprised by the sudden contact but her eyes softened instantly and she froze in place, trying not to distract Lena and prolong it. 

     

    “A Gravitational Beam Emitter.” - Kara answered thoughtfully and turned the pistol in her hand, inspecting it. - “And in great condition. I wonder how they got their hands on it. It is extremely rare and exceptionally powerful.” 

     

    “Can I have it?” - Lena begged, almost salivating over a better weapon. She loved her rifle, and even named her “Hope”, but the old girl was in a desperate need of maintenance and now they were in need of better equipment.

     

    “You can carry it but I doubt you will survive the recoil.” - Kara huffed, turning the weapon in the direction of the body of the AI and aiming right behind it, into the wall.

     

    “Do you think I am so weak?” - Lena took offence to her words and took her chin off Kara’s shoulder. She was too irritated to see a slight disappointment on the Kryptonian’s face. Instead of answering Lena’s question Kara pulled on the trigger slowly. The pistol made a quiet squeaky sound, which became louder with time. She saw its barrel lighting up and then a thick beam of energy shooting from it, making a deep hole into the structure and exploding somewhere deep inside, miles away. Kara’s shoulder yanked from the recoil and Lena gulped down all arguments she was ready to spit out. If the Kryptonian jerked under the force of the powerful recoil, Lena’s arm would be torn off by it. She looked into the hole, made by the Beam Emitter, its edges still smouldering and smoking and then on the meaty growths of The Governor’s body. They jerked erratically, pulsing and leaking brownish, bloody goo. 

     

    “You managed to hit The Governor?” - Lena gasped in awe.

     

    “Yes.” - Kara watched the growths pulse irregularly, before all the movements of the bloody gore of The Governor’s body stopped. - “It is dead. Brace yourself.” 

     

    Lena frowned, looking at the Kryptonian. Everything was silent at this moment,  and she wondered why Kara was so tense. The danger seemed to pass but the Kryptonian stood still, her head slightly tilted to the side as if she was listening to something. Lena opened her mouth to say something quirky but then closed it, suddenly hearing clunking sounds from everywhere, including the still smouldering hole made by Emitter, the erratic clatter filling the area. She turned around, terrified and felt her breath hitching somewhere deep in her throat. There were cyborgs, hundreds of them, coming from all the holes and openings, crawling down the walls, coming out of the huge hole in the wall, their weapons ready. Lena saw their rolling eyes and the terror from the loss of both their ruler and deity in a one shot of a gun. 

     

    They all shot their weapons at the same time and Lena closed her eyes, ready to die. She felt Kara landing in front of her and covering her with her body, deflecting all shots. She shrugged off the bullets and turned around, melting cyborgs that came too close  with her heat vision. Lena managed to run away and hide behind the fallen rubble. She opened her magazine and suppressed a disappointed whine. It was empty.

     

    Kara shot into the air, destroying cyborgs with her vision and landed only to punch someone who came too close. She grabbed one of them and tore its upper body off, swinging it like a mace and hitting a bunch of silicone life with it, throwing them away from her and showing them farther from Lena’s hideout. She growled and smacked it against the big cyborg who tried to punch her, knocking it on one knee and shooting into the air just to heavily land on top of it and break its chest, making it wheeze. She was jumped on by at least five smaller ones and Lena held her breath when she saw Kara being buried under the metallic bodies of these creatures. She exhaled only when Kara emerged from under it, with someone’s head in her hand, which she smacked with a sickening crack over someone’s skull. She roared like a beast and shot through the bunch of the assailants with a single blast of her heat vision. 

     

    “Kara, look out!” - Lena yelped, when a huge cyborg emerged from the shadows of the structure and shot Kara with his built-in machine guns which were fastened to him as hands. Kara staggered under the force of bullets but managed to stay upright, her legs sliding back against the ground when the force of the shots pushed her. She waited until his weapons overheated and he would need to take a break to cool down and shot forward, landing punches on him. She was strong, pushing him back with every punch and leaving dents on his metallic body, but Lena saw her breath heaving and her knuckles started to bleed. Even Kryptonians had limits to their unimaginable power and now Kara was at her limit. The cyborg landed a heavy punch on her and it made her fall to one knee. She shot her arm up and caught his other arm and stopped him from hitting her again. 

     

    Almost a dozen smaller cyborgs attacked her again, landing punches on her and making her fold and drop to both knees. Lena now saw blood pooling out of Kara’s nose and staining the blue uniform. The Kryptonian was spent, and if she is spent it means that they both are dead. Lena jumped from her hideout and yelled loudly, waving her hands to attract the attention of the cyborgs. She succeeded, all of them suddenly stopping mid punch, turning their heads in her direction. Lena stepped back, ready to run.
