She started off already, when she felt herself being grabbed into a bridal carry and pressed against someone's chest. She turned her head and saw Kara, her nose and mouth bloody and left eye half closed, a bruise already forming around it. The Kryptonian, with Lena in her arms, shot into the air like a speeding bullet, leaving the cyborgs behind.

    LOG.6: into the depths

    Chapter Summary

    Kara and Lena finally have their alone time and have a conversation.

Lena jumped off her before Kara could fall on top of her and incapacitate them both. She was in a sad state, her face and knuckles bloody and bruised. But right now we will be lucky just to survive out there and not to be caught by the cops. Especially with you on board. No offence!” - he put his hands up and smiled apologetically.

When it went out of commission, it was separated from the main station and jettisoned into outer space to rot and fall apart by itself.

    “Oh, nice! I will have my own room!” - Lena smiled happily, interrupting Kara’s stammering.

    ‘It’s a hideout, Kryptonian, not a hotel.” - James laughed out, walking past her and smirking slyly. - “Be happy you have a roof over your head and no cops on your ass.”

Lex was still watching her like a hawk, tensing when she touched Lena and James refused to call her by her name, instead calling her “Kryptonian”. But it was more of a minor inconvenience than a real threat, and even they visibly warmed up to her after a filling dinner and a cold can of  nice beer.

Turned out that the Naltorian girl’s name was Nia and the Coluan’s was Querl, but everyone called him Brainy. Kara knew about Braniacs, who were used by the Kryptonians as supercomputers on Krypton, and ran day-to-day operations. She asked about his story, curious how he ended up here, so far from his kin. He stiffly told her how he left his home planet and his 31st century’s timeline, running from his cruel parents. Nia explained away his quietness with the fact that he knew about the future but couldn’t tell anything because he feared a time-space paradox happening and irreparably damaging this timeline.

     

    The Martian fled from Earth after many years of being a smuggler and saving thousands of people, until he couldn’t bear it anymore. Now he assumed human form, justifying it with the fact that he fit better in a soft chair in his smaller visage. Esme, who played with her toys, finally curled up on the blanket thrown on the floor and fell asleep, lulled by the quiet conversation of the adults around her. Lex also dozed off in his sofa corner, finally letting all the stress and anxiety of the day go. Lena cradled a beer in her hands, curled up on the old soft chair, her feet tucked under herself. She shedded her armour beforehand and now wore only her under armour that looked like a black dress. It felt like home so much more than on Krypton. Of course Kara had family there, and she missed Alex and Kal-El dearly, but only now she felt free to be herself, without a weight of expectations on her shoulders. 



    LOG.9: stronger together

    Chapter Summary

    There was only one bed @_@

She heard the noise of falling water in the bathroom, where Lena took her shower and finally took step forward, after a long consideration. There wasn’t a lot of furniture, only a small round table with two chairs, a closet and a bed. It was, indeed, only one bed. A double one, not too big, but enough for two people to sleep comfortably, if they would lay close to each other. Kara’s brain switched off as soon as she imagined herself intertwined with Lena on this small, dusty piece of furniture.

It was surprisingly easy to talk to Lena, even the fact that she was playful and drove Kara into the flustering fit wasn’t uncomfortable. Kara just wasn’t used to the intercourses like these but the fact that Lena praised her look and watched her with obvious admiration was flattering. -”I am still somewhere in the “young people'' age group for my race.”

    “I will be 24 in a few months.” - Lena smiled at Kara but then fell silent and frowned for a moment. It was comically exaggerated and made Kara smile. - “Oh my god, I hope I am at least considered as an adult on The Exodus?”

    “Of course!” - Kara laughed, watching Lena’s face relax and eyes lit up with a cunning spark again.

    “Then I can throw my leg on you.” - Lena dropped back onto the cushion and threw her knee over Kara’s stomach, exhaling in content. - “Sorry, I almost got cramps from laying still.”

    “Feel free.” - Kara stiffened again slightly, feeling her face heating up, but the feel of Lena’s body wrapping around hers so perfectly made her melt into her embrace. She closed her eyes, feeling Lena's breath on her neck and her body relaxing around her, warming up fast, taking in Kara’s heat.

    “I wonder, do you miss being a Space Guard?” - Lena’s quiet voice made her open her eyes and look at the human. She bumped into her forehead with her face, feeling her lips brushing against Lena’s forehead.

    “I don’t know.” - Kara admired honestly. She felt Lena signing into her shoulder and felt her thick eyebrows brushing over her neck to form a frown. - “I may miss the days when I know what to expect tomorrow. But I don’t miss the person I was back then. I was blind to injustice, I was too scared to stand up for the things I saw were wrong, I was afraid to speak against my own people in the fear of losing them. But I am a different person now, or at least I hope I am.”

    “You are. You believed in me and that means a lot. You stepped over your prejudices to stand up to things you found wrong and I appreciate it.” - Lena mumbled, her voice muffled by Kara’s shoulder. It made her smile and she found Lena’s hand under the sheets to squeeze it gently.

    “You know what? I think maybe it was time for me to change. I was a coward for so long that it’s now time for me to grow, to start my life anew. Like a clean slate.” - Kara looked at the ceiling again, to find the stars of the Almerac once again. She found some kind of grounding in watching these uneven, phosphorised accessories. - “Maybe now I will finally find the strength to be the hero I always wanted to be.”

    “You became a Space Guard to be a hero?” - Lena smiled and lifted her face from Kara’s shoulder to look at her profile.

    “I guess I did. Is it stupid?” - Kara suddenly felt uncertain. Her parents always said that this is a childish dream, and she was above that as a member of House of El.

    “No!” - Lena interrupted the train of her thoughts, pulling her out of the misery that suddenly overflowed her. - “It is actually amazing. Well, being a cop is not the right way to be a hero, but the thought of helping people is a noble one. Maybe now when you aren’t limited by the stiff rules of The Exodus you will be able to do things you always dreamed of.”

    “My first heroic deed will be to clear your name!” - Kara hummed, determined.

    “Okay, my hero.” - Lena chuckled. She lifted herself up and leaned on her elbow just to place a firm kiss on Kara’s cheek and dropped back down, wrapping her arm around Kara’s biceps. - “You are my knight in shining armour. Now go to sleep, Sir Kara, tomorrow will be a big day.”

    -

    “Rise up, sleepyheads!” - Kara jumped awake and stared at Kelly, who stood in the doorway, looking at them with a slight disappointment for some reason. Lena yawned and turned on her stomach, pressing her face into the pillow. - “I made pancakes and James is already up. I can’t promise there will be something left if you don’t not move your asses into the kitchen in the next five minutes.”

    “Mommy? Can I drink the rest of the sweet brown thingie from the bottle?” - Esme’s squeaky voice sounded from the depths of the kitchen.

    “No! That’s chocolate syrup, don’t you dare!” - Kelly dashed out to save the remains of the syrup from her daughter’s sweet tooth. - “I knew that a few minutes of silence were too suspicious!”

    Kara watched Lena rolling out of the bed with a sleepy groan and walking towards the bathroom. She blinked the last traces of sleep from her eyes and turned to her back. She never felt this warm inside before, like she finally found her place and its here, among these people. She could live like this - start her mornings wrapped into Lena’s arms, listening to bickering of James and Lex. Watching Kelly bustling about, yelling at her brother to be useful and help her and shooing off Esme, who was ready to put her fingers into everything on the table.

    “Kara? You okay?” - Lena’s gentle voice brought her back from her thoughts. Their eyes met and Lena smiled, seeing that Kara’s face lit up with newly found determination. - “Let’s go or there will not be any food left.”

    Kara ate her breakfast quietly, observing the people around her. The warm, fuzzy feeling still lingered when she saw this big, noisy family around the table, consuming fluffy pancakes of different shapes. Kelly was everywhere, loading plates with pancakes, wiping the dirty face of her daughter with her hand and stopping her from drowning her plate in a syrup. James loudly argued with his sister about the amount of food he can eat in one meal. Winn was trying to survive the morning by sipping from his coffee mug and jumping every time his noisy boyfriend yelled and laughed too close to his ear. J’onn tried to relax in his favourite soft chair with a beer, but was yelled at and sat down at the table with a plate and a mug of steaming black liquid. Brainy stiffly sat beside Nia and muttered something that made her laugh. Lex was already looking at his tablet, mumbling something under his nose and typing from time to time with determination on his face. Kara looked at Lena, who drew a smiley face on the pancakes with liquid chocolate to the amusement of Esme. The girl looked at Lena like she was a goddess, with her eyes sparkling with adoration and mouth agape.

    With the increasing amount of mouths to feed, they decided to go to the Hator to get provisions, not without some bickering of course. Hator was a ruined planet, overrun by the scavengers and raiders, who hid from the hands of justice. It was an unruled place, dangerous and barely habitable, with only parts of it remaining clean enough to have breathable air and drinkable water. But one could get any supplies from here and prices were usually determined by the status of the buyer and could always be negotiated with the use of weapons and strong words.

    They loaded supplies for the journey onto their ship and boarded it, occupying the seats on the sides. Kara still wondered how this tin can was staying afloat and moving so fast, but Kelly was really proud of her. Even under the better light the ship still looked like an over bloated dung fly. It was big, clunky and was painted black a long time ago. The paint peeled in places, especially on its nose and the only new addition on it was the red letters painted by hand which read “Black Betty”. Betty was an old imperial paratrooper carrier, made in the Citadel a long time ago. It was meant to have been destroyed along with other imperial spacecraft, gear and bots but was sold to the raiders instead and from there came into the possession of Kelly and her team. Betty was surprisingly fast, sturdy and most important - she was off the grid of the Space Guardian system.

    Kelly piloted it through the meteor storm with a steady hand, having time to answer the numerous questions asked by Esme. Kara just relaxed in her chair, when she heard the radar beeping and Kelly yelping “we’ve been followed”. She jumped to her feet and looked at the greenish screen, hoping to see the pursuer. Unfortunately the system was so old the alien ship only showed up as a small red dot, which came closer with every passing second. Kelly furrowed her brows and accelerated, evading the remaining rocks in her way carefully. She flicked her eyes on the radar screen and looked back through the frontal window, determining how long it would take for her to fly to the closest jumping point, to be able to use hyper light engines far enough from any planet, to not be disrupted by the solar system’s magnetic field. Unfortunately Betty was too old and not able to accelerate enough if there was any interference, and they needed to fly closer to the ancient jumping point to be able to fly between solar systems.

    Betty rattled dangerously when Kelly opened the metallic lids of three small switches and turned them on, accelerating the old ship and making it spiral forward a great distance, leaving the pursuer behind. The victory was short though, as the pursuing ship was coming dangerously close again. Kara looked around, searching for any external weapons. She wasn’t alone, James already pulling out the ship’s gun handles and aiming for the pursuers.

    “This is the Lieutenant of the Space Guards speaking. Lay down your arms and prepare the dock to be boarded!” - Kara heard a familiar voice through the crackling interference of Betty's old communicator.

    “Over my dead body!” - Kelly growled through gritted teeth and saw James pulling the trigger and showering the pursuing ship in laser bullets.

    “I said lay down your arms or I will be forced to call for a backup and bring you down.” - Kara heard the familiar voice again. The person behind it grew impatient.

    “The fuck I will!” - Kelly spat out and turned around, looking at James who nodded and was ready to fire again but Kara jumped to him and put a steady hand on the trigger, stopping him.

    “What are you doing, Kryptonian?” - James hissed, looking at her with poorly concealed anger. Kara braced herself to be patient. She saw it in their eyes - they were ready for her to betray them, to run back to the Space Guards like a loyal dog and drag them all up to the cops just to be swiftly judged and thrown into jail to rot.

    “The person in that ship - she is my sister.” - Kara said calmly. She tried to look collected despite feeling her heart beating somewhere in her throat.

    “She is a cop! Makes no difference if she is your sister or not, cops aren’t our friends, Kryptonian. Especially now, when they hunt us down like animals.” - James looked her straight in the eyes, squaring his jaw. He was angry, and it was justified, after all they were through thanks to the Space Guards and their over eagerness to catch Kara. But Alex was on that ship and Kara wasn’t ready to give up on her.

    “Let me try to reason with her. If I am not able to do it, then do whatever you feel necessary.” - Kara begged, looking around and meeting Lena’s eyes. She could see that Lena wasn’t a fan of this idea, remembering the abuse she survived from Alex. Kara watched her with pleading eyes, silently promising that she will try to make it work and that everyone will be safe, no matter what it will cost her. Lena sighed and nodded to her, in a silent agreement. Kara smiled. She suddenly felt elevated, the fact that Lena understood her from half a gaze was so heartwarming, like they didn’t meet only a few days ago but knew each other for decades. - “I will not allow any harm to come to any of you. Just let me try.”

    “Okay. I just hope you know what you are doing, Kara..” - Kelly finally agreed, after a few long moments of contemplating. Kara still saw the mistrust in the eyes of the people around her, but she was not ready to give up on her sister. Not when she was so close and where there was a real chance to try and convince her to drop the chase and maybe, if she is lucky, also win her over to her side.

    “But if you try to betray us, you have my word, I will destroy you and everything you love.” - Lex threatened, watching Kara’s face closely for any sign of doubt or malice, but the Kryptonian only nodded in agreement. Of course she wasn’t sure of the outcome but whatever it takes, Kara will try to be the bridge that will bring together her sister and The Sawflies. She didn’t want to think what would be if Alex wouldn’t be willing to let them go or join up, so she whispered a short prayer to Rao for her sister to be less of an ox head than she always was.

    There should be a way to reason with Alex, despite her being so certain that they are all irredeemable criminals. Kara came to the communicator and held her breath for a long moment to gather her thoughts. She has only one shot at it, her sister or not, but she will not allow her to drive them all into a Guard-controlled area to be arrested. Lena will be sent to the Phantom Zone, Esme will end up in an orphanage again and this family will be scattered across the galaxy, serving undeserved time in the different jails.

    “Alex, it’s me, Kara.” - she said after calming her thoughts and pressing a button with the peeled off paint from overuse.

    “Kara! You are alive! Did they hurt you? They captured you! Hold on a bit I…” - Alex rambled, growing angry.

    “Shut up, Alex and listen to me. I wasn’t captured and I for sure wasn’t harmed in any way. I have a proposition for you and I would like you to listen to me, and make the right decision right now.” - Kara asserted firmly, tensing her jaw. It wasn’t easy for her to be so harsh to a person she cared about, but a lot of lives depended on her. She said to Lena that she wanted to protect people, to be their pinnacle and hero, and there was her chance to follow her own words and make her dreams come true.

    “I am listening.” - Alex said after a few seconds of silence. She seemed as stunned by Kara’s rough response, as Kara herself.

    “I know you don’t wish any harm on these people, or me. You are a good person, Alex, I know you. You are kind and caring and just. You know what is the right thing to do when you see the injustice, and you should see how wrong was my mother when she condemned Lena for the crime she didn’t commit.” - Kara pleaded, hoping that she would stir something in the soul of her sister.

    “I am not sure I know you now, Kara! They are raiders, criminals. And you also broke the law when you broke the accused felon out of jail.” - Alex said bitterly but Kara heard a note of the uncertainty in her voice and decided to press further.

    “It’s still me, nothing has changed. But I decided to look at things from another perspective and it changed my attitude towards a lot of things that we thought were good and just. Just look around you, Alex. Everything we fought for, everything we knew was thrown away when my mother judged Lena for something she didn’t do and doomed her to die. Just because she wanted a petty revenge on her for destroying her perfect image of me. But I am not perfect. Nobody is. There is no one without sin, but we need to see deeper than that. Not everyone who steals is a thief, maybe they are just hungry! Not everyone who kills is a murderer, maybe they were just defending themselves or someone else. We are used to just throwing people in jail. No matter what they did, because they don't match this “perfect image”. Our judging system is deeply flawed and I couldn’t change it from within. But we still have a chance to do better, not to participate in this injustice.” - Kara paused to consider her words but then continued silently hoping that her words fell on a fertile ground.. - “Join us, Alex. I know you don’t trust anyone, but it’s not the way to live your life. There are a lot of good people around you, you just need to look around and let them in.”

    “You sound like you were brainwashed.” - Alex mumbled. She still hung on to her stubbornness, but sounded even less confident than before.

    “I was not, but the things I survived in this short time changed me for the better. These people aren’t your enemies, as they aren’t mine. So either you come in peace and see it for yourself or you leave and never come back.” - Kara deadpanned and held her breath to hear Alex’s answer.

    Alex fell silent for a long time and Kara started to stress out slowly, when she finally heard some rustling and muffled voices behind the microphone on board Alex’s shuttle. Her breath hitched when the realisation hit her. Alex wasn’t alone on her ship and there was at least one more person who’s voice she managed to hear. She held her breath in hopes that Alex acknowledges that she isn’t alone and doesn’t betray Kara. She looked back, watching the faces of those around her. They all heard another person through the comms and were watching Kara with different levels of unease.

    “You are asking me to betray The Exodus and all the people I know, plus the Space Guards. Despite all my reservations about the people you are with now, I am willing to risk everything and join you, but I am not alone.” - Alex said finally, with a bit of uncertainty in her voice.

    Kara didn’t know if she should feel relieved or not. On one hand, Alex agreed, which was great. On another - she did it so fast, without fighting back like Kara expected her to, that she felt uncertain of how sincere was her sudden shift to their side. She suspected that they discussed it with someone else, who was on board that shuttle and only hoped that it wasn’t the only thing that affected Alex’s decision after her speech, where she poured all her heart out. She held her breath in for a moment, and contemplated her next step. There was no coming back, after Alex agreed to her proposition but she needed to know who was in that shuttle. It could be friend or foe and because of that the safety of the people that surrounded her was in question.

    “Who is with you?” - Kara asked carefully, keeping her voice calm and steady.

    “It’s me, Commander Zor-El.” - she heard through the microphone and felt her heart sinking. - “Don’t try to run away now, or I will use all means to catch and destroy you.”

    “I will not run away from you, Reign.” - Kara said with a steady voice, despite her heart beating somewhere in her throat.

    “Good.” - she heard Reign saying and switching off the comms.

    The door slots outside whirled, letting the other ship dock to them, letting the police shuttle passengers inside. Alex came in first, her hands empty but fingers twitching near the holster of her blaster. She met Kara’s eyes and relaxed visibly, seeing that her sister wasn't harmed in any way. Reign came in after her, her dark eyes scanning the insides of the “Black Betty” sharply, her face stony and spine rigid. She stood at the entrance and looked around, her eyes lingering on Lena for a short moment, softening significantly, before meeting Kara’s eyes. She smirked dangerously, walked towards the Kryptonian and sized her up from head to toe.

    “You know I was sent to bring you both back?” - she asked, smirking and looking into Kara’s eyes unblinking.

    “I figured. It seems like my parents really want to bring me to justice if they send the Punisher herself to get me.” - Kara hummed bitterly.

    As much as she would like to deny this fact, it was obvious that her parents were ready for anything just to get her back and put her under her mother’s heel. Reign was the person who she would like to see last in this universe. Dangerous, not burdened by any morals of this world, ruthless, cruel even. She was usually sent to get the worst criminals to justice, dead or alive, depending on what The Exodus ordered her. She was their fist of justice, the thing that in their understanding stood for justice but in reality was just a tool of intimidation, a person who would break one and subdue them, making them a victim of the horrideous grinding machine of ‘justice’ how they understood it.

    “Your mommy was really sad that her baby girl disobeyed her and threw a tantrum. They promised to pay me nicely for dragging you back under mommy's skirt.” - Reign sarcastically bellowed as she narrowed her eyes and tilted her head, still drilling holes in Kara’s skull. Kara held her gaze and tried to stay calm, despite anger brewing deep inside. She still couldn’t understand what to expect from Reign. She wasn’t hostile, despite her cruel quirks but it was impossible to tell what was really on her mind.

    “Reign, leave her alone!” - Alex intervened suddenly. She was obviously scared of what could come up next and Kara could understand her, she was also on the edge. If Reign attacked, Kara could be forced to defend herself and two Kryptonians fighting in an area so small would be devastating.

    “I didn’t speak to you, Lieutenant.” - Reign turned to Alex and looked at her with a badly concealed dislike. Kara blinked, surprised. She hadn’t expected The Punisher to react like this to a person who should have been her ally. It seems like Alex was also surprised by this sudden lashing out but remained silent, not willing to escalate it.

    “What are you gonna do now, Reign? I am not sure what your intentions are. But I would like you to know that I will give my life to protect those people, be it from you, or from any other threat that comes their way.” - Kara stated calmly. Despite her stony exterior, she felt her palms becoming damp from sweat. Reign was not only a formidable opponent but also an entity of an unknown origin. She was a mysterious being, who just appeared on the Exodus along with her three aunts and two sisters. Nobody knew how they were chosen to enter it, and even Kara’s parents questioned the legitimacy of their being on the board of the starship, but in the end all three sisters grew up to be the best sentinels of The Exodus and the fiercest law enforcers.

    Or so Kara thought before she turned her head to look at her team and saw them relaxed and returning to their business as soon as they saw Reign boarding their ship. Lex looked like he was watching a funny movie, his eyes sparkling with amusement and mouth covered with his palm to conceal the wide smile. Kelly, who jumped to her feet when she heard the other ship docking and pulled Esme behind her, sat back into her chair and relaxed, switching off the now unnecessary accelerators. Others also seemed calm and content, going back to their business. Lena stood up and came closer, looking into Reign’s eyes. Kara held up her hand to pull Lena behind her like Kelly did with Esme before, but Lena stopped her with a soft hand on her bicep.

    “Sam, please, don’t be so rude.” - she said looking into Reign’s eyes.

    “My apologies.” - Reign mumbled, suddenly deflating. She looked at Lena apologetically and gave her a shy smile, looking like a big guilty puppy who just knocked a plate off the table.

    “You know each other?” - Kara asked, her voice slightly quivering from anger. She inhaled through her nose loudly, looking at Reign with the badly concealed hatred. She had no idea Lena knew her and this revelation stung her painfully. She felt the hot red anger creeping up, making her breath hitch. She squeezed her fists so hard her knuckles became white and inhaled and exhaled slowly, trying to battle the rage inside. At this very moment she hated Reign more than anything in this life but she still got a hold on herself and continued as calmly as she could. - “ You knew about it, Alex?”

    "No, I didn’t!" - Alex turned to Kara. She looked at her sister in shock, watching her squeezing her fists and huffing loudly. Kara flicked her eyes on her, meeting her gaze briefly but then peered back at Reign, who was watching Lena with the tenderness in her eyes. Fortunately, soon Reign finally stopped ogling the human and looked back at Kara, her mouth stretching in the nastiest smirk Kara had ever seen on the living being.

    "I would not go into details right now, but me and the Sawflies have met before." - Reign acknowledged evasively, amused by the sisters’ reactions. - "And since you already involved me in your little heist I will stay here and keep an eye on you both."

    "On us both?" - Alex narrowed her eyes, gazing at Reign, who wore a smug smirk, looking like a cat ready to eat a bird. - "You mean me and Kara?"

    "Of course, who else?" - Reign looked at Alex with an exaggerated surprise, which caused several people around them to chuckle.

    "I don't understand…" - Kara looked at Reign, trying to understand her motive and failing miserably. - "What is your motive for that? You are fanatically loyal to The Exodus, you serve my parents and they promised to pay you for bringing me in. What are you planning to do with those people?"

    "Oh, Lieutenant! You are here for the big surprise regarding me and my real loyalty, so buckle up, buttercup.” - she laughed out sarcastically, drilling Alex with her reddened eyes, making her step back, closer to Kara and look at her sister anxiously. - “ And what am I planning to do with these people, Kara, is none of your business. What I am planning to do with you is make sure you will not attract any unwanted attention. You, Commander, put targets on their backs, and I would really like you to not be the cause of their downfall."

    "Why do you care so much?" - Kara pressed on, watching Reign rolling her eyes and tensing her jaw, annoyed by her persistence.

    “Because I do! I have my own agenda here and it’s none of your business right now. Right now it’s up to you not to attract any unwanted attention and not to get them all killed!” - Reign grew impatient, looking at Kara with hatred.

    “Oh, don’t you worry, asshole! I will keep them safe, unlike you, the lapdog of The Exodus! You wiggled your tail before my mother for decades and now you think I will trust you to be the saviour of these humans? You?” - Kara stepped forward, pushing stunned Reign back. She didn't expect the reaction so strong from the usually calm and collected Kara and it startled her.

    “Girls, please! Calm down!” - Lena stepped between them, pushing them apart. Of course her arms were too weak for them to be really pushed apart but they both still stepped back obediently still watching each other with deep hatred. - “We all can see you have a beef with each other but if you will fight each other right here we all will go down.”

    "Can I talk to you for a second?" - Kara huffed angrily, urging Lena into Winn's server room. She saw Reign scoffing at her when she saw her touching the human, but she just gave her the hardest stare she could muster and pulled Lena deeper into the room. As soon as they were alone, Kara closed the door, listening carefully so no one would come to eavesdrop on them. - "You cannot seriously trust her!"

    "Why not? She’s been our ally for years. I trusted her before and I trust her now. I don't know what your problem is with her, but she is a good person and an amazing friend." - Lena crossed hands on her chest and looked Kara in the eyes. She watched Kara closely, not getting why she reacted so sharply. Kara struggled to understand it either, just feeling a red hot anger right in the middle of her chest. It wasn’t the fact that Reign was a dangerous person that made her so mad. It was the fact that Reign knew Lena and how she looked at her. It was infuriating.

    "She is literally an Exodus lapdog. I never saw a person more loyal to the Kryptonian government than her. She is lawful to a fault, she is The Punisher after all! My parents like her so much because she can hunt down anyone who wronged them wherever they are and whoever they are. She is dangerous!" - Kara took Lena by the shoulders and made her look into her eyes. She wanted to convince Lena that Reign is bad, but somewhere deep inside she understood that it wasn’t fair. These were her own feelings and her own jealousy, but she chased the thought away and continued to watch Lena’s green eyes for any spark of doubt about the other Kryptonian.

    "You were ready to give Alex a chance, give one to Sam, too. I know it's not easy to get over your past perception of her, but you will see that under the mask of a tough warrior is a soft and caring woman who is not indifferent to the people around her. " - Lena pleaded.

    "Okay, but I will watch her every step." - Kara growled. The fact that Lena called Reign by some other name stung her even more, but she pushed these feelings down hard. It hurt how she praised another woman and spoke about her affectionately, but Kara braced herself and finally relaxed, even just so slightly. It would be only fair to give Reign a chance like Lena asked. But when she inevitably betrays them, Kara will be ready to fight her.

    "Of course, if it will ease your mind." - Lena agreed calmly. It was slightly offensive to the Kryptonian, that Lena was so confident about someone that wasn't her, but she pushed these feelings down too.

    They finally landed on Hator, Betty hitting the sandy ground with the rattling sound and coughing out a cloud of dirty smoke. The landing platform dropped and they scattered out of the ship, happy to be finally out of the suffocating atmosphere created by two Kryptonians. The mutual dislike was palpable and the rivalry between two godlike creatures terrified the others.

    Kelly kicked everyone out of the ship and turned all her attention to the malfunctioning engine. Kara exited last, withstanding Kelly’s shouting and everyone’s quizzical gazes. She finally descended the steep stairs, her spine rigid and newly obtained armour clanking. She watched Lena who stood at the bottom of the platform, looking at her with a mix of admiration and something more. She watched Lena biting her lower lip when she looked at her, standing on the of the platform awkwardly. Kara’s body was still accommodating to the feeling of the new armour and she blushed under Lena’s gaze, stumbling over her own feet on the way down and barely making it without falling.

    “Aunty Lena!” - Esme hung on Lena’s skirt like a monkey on a palm tree and looked at her with big eyes, grabbing her attention. Kara exhaled in relief quietly. - “When we go to the bazaar will we buy candies? And chocolate, and a cornhog?”

    “Wouldn’t it be a little too much, munchkin?” - Lena ruffled her messy hair and took her by the hand. - “First of all we need normal food and then I will see what I can do about a little treat if you will behave and help us carry the groceries.”

    “But I want a big treat!” - Esme argued, pouting.

    “Looks like we have a lot of negotiations ahead.” - Lena laughed, walking towards the noisy bazaar. Kara walked behind them, dragging her feet, still embarrassed by her clumsy descent.

    She saw Alex, who stood at some distance and only came closer when she was sure that they were far away from the prying ears of other team members, especially Reign, who seemed to feel at home among the ex-Sawflies. She pulled Kara closer and waited until Lena and Esme walked further away. It seemed like she wanted to talk but the presence of other people stopped her. Alex was always an overly cautious human and now she seemed at her limit, surrounded by strangers.

    “Speak up, Alex.” - Kara urged, finally growing annoyed by her hesitancy. She saw Alex chewing her lips but she gave up finally.

    “You cannot trust Reign. She sang one song while we were chasing you but changed it as soon as we boarded the pirate ship. She is not who she seems to be and I cannot crack her for now.” - Alex flicked her eyes to Lena who seemed to listen to Esme's happy babbling but slowed her walk a bit when Kara and Alex fell silent, eavesdropping from afar.

    “She seems to be a trusted member of the team. Lena trusts her and I do, too.” - Kara answered loudly and stubbornly. Alex didn't seem too convinced and looked at Kara like she grew another head.

    “Listen. I know you grew fond of this one, but try to be reasonable.” - Alex flicked her gaze on Lena and back to Kara, showing that she knew what was going on between them. She still tried to press on but Kara stopped her with a steady hand on her shoulder.

    “It is me being reasonable, Alex. I gave you a chance and I will give Reign one, too. If she betrays us, I will destroy her. If she proves to be a great ally, I will cherish the day I accepted her.” - Kara pressed her lips together. Alex saw that this decision didn’t come easy but she also understood that there was nothing that could convince Kara otherwise so she just let it go for now.

    “I hope you are right, sister. I hope you are.” - Alex shook her head and walked forward, entering the noisy bazaar before her companions.

    Kara saw Lena stopping to put on a black gas mask that covered her face and threw on a black hood. It made her look surprisingly like a Qinoori raider. She clutched Esme’s hand desperately in hopes that locals would not see the little girl as a slave for trade, and would not make an attempt to snatch her. Lena fastened the straps of her rifle on her shoulders with a free hand and went into the crowded area with her head held high.

    They walked between the rows of fruits and vegetables that alternated with tables filled with spare parts of different quality, age and rust layers. Lena barked something rude in Qinoori to a few aliens, who wanted to trade their goods for Esme. A few people looked at Kara with a weird focus and she heard a few whispers aimed at her. She started to panic slightly, trying to hide in the shadows and conceal her face by covering it under the locks of her hair.

    She couldn’t hold back a yelp when someone pulled her inside one of the huts on the side of the road. She was ready to silently fight the assailant, feeling bad enough already for letting any sound out, but saw Reign who looked at her with pity. She poked her head out of the doorway and whistled, attracting the attention of Lena, who came inside, taking Esme with her. The girl seemed to understand the danger of this place and was as silent as a little mouse, clutching Lena’s hand desperately.

    “You attract too much attention, Kara.” - Reign spat, looking at her from head to toe. She seemed to be satisfied enough with Kara’s new armour, it was her face where Reign’s dark eyes lingered the longest. - “But I have some ideas. Sit.”

    “If you do anything odd to me, I will destroy you.” - Kara threatened, crossing arms on her chest.

    “Don’t worry, Kara, it’s just a little glow up. If I wanted to, I would have cut your head off long ago. Sit.” - Reign urged again, looking at the chair behind Kara’s back. Kara followed her gaze and sighed, sitting down and looking at Reign with distrust.

    “I hope you don’t value your hair too much.” - Reign mumbled after walking around Kara and evaluating her looks.

    “You want to cut my hair?” - Kara looked at Reign who watched her impassionately. She saw a tiny shadow of satisfaction on her insolent mug and wanted to wipe it off but held back just because she didn’t want to scare little Esme.

    “Hair isn't teeth, it will grow back. It will be easier to hide her hair under the helmet if it's short.” - Reign smirked at Lena, who watched them both with a slight worry, because they were arguing again.

    Kara looked at Lena too, but saw her smiling at her supportively and exhaled, relaxing her shoulder, sitting back into the chair and watching Reign moving around her menacingly. She seemed to contemplate how to cut into Kara’s hair better and gestured to her so she wouldn’t move. After a few long moments when Kara thought that the other Kryptonian would melt her face off instead of just cutting her hair, Reign finally activated her laser vision and cut through her locks. Kara watched Lena’s face drop slightly, her saddened eyes looking at Kara’s blonde hair dropping on the floor cut by the only thing that could do it - the powers of another Kryptonian. - “While we are at it we can discuss our next step, you know?”

    “Don’t you want to do it in the presence of our whole team?” - Kara mumbled, looking at Reign from under her brow.

    “No, I wanted to discuss it with you both first, before involving others.” - Reign answered calmly, still cutting Kara’s hair carefully. Kara winced slightly, feeling the heat of Reign’s vision too close to the tender skin of her temple. - “What is your next step?”

    “My plan was to find out who stole that Kryptonite, because it was obviously not Lena.” - Kara mumbled, still cautious of Reign lasering off the hair from her head.

    “And your guess is…?” - Reign asked, finishing her job with a final flick of her eyes and admiring her work from afar, nodding to herself when she liked the result.

    “Daxamites.” - Kara gave her short answer, finally gathering all her courage to look at Lena. She was afraid to see disappointment on her face, maybe even disgust. Instead Lena looked like she was standing before Rao himself, her wet lips parted and eyes glistening with the same desire Kara saw in a bathtub. She saw Reign also watching Lena, her face changing from amused to tensed and then disappointed. She sighed heavily and turned to Kara, looking at her with newly lit annoyance.

    “Your guess is as good as mine.” - Reign battled her feelings towards Kara for a bit, but then finally hummed in agreement and looked at Lena, who nodded.

    “I think I know a few people that can tell us where to find the Prince of Daxam.” - Lena smirked, looking at the faces of both Kryptonians.

    “Good! I, on the other hand, have an idea how to hide this smug face from being seen by everyone around us.” - Reign smiled at Lena tenderly and Kara hated it. Before she could say something, Reign took a helmet off the table and held it out to Kara. It was a simple piece of armour with a narrow darkened T-visor and round dome that made it look like an egg. It was painted royal blue, like her armour and looked like it was a part of the set, except for the helmet being made from another metal alloy. - “Now you will be a member of The Children of Juru. A nameless human, who took the creed and swore to never take it off in the name of the ancient goddess, Yuda Kal. Repeat after me - ‘And we will reign!’”

    “And we will reign.” - Kara repeated, not without some hesitation. But it was a perfect disguise and she couldn’t argue with that. As hard as it seemed for her to follow Reign’s advice, she will survive this, if not for herself, then for others who depended on her.

    LOG 10: the hideout

    Chapter Summary

    Kara and Reign finally sorted things out between them. And on the way, also finally found out who stole Kryptonite.

    It took them weeks to find the one who would point them to someone who knew about the Daxamites wearabouts. They were bounced from one informant to another and paid an excessive amount of credits just to keep hitting brick walls. Everyone was tired, and Kara and Lena were living in constant fear that the team would give up eventually, leaving only them and Reign to ferret out the thieves of the Kryptonite. 

     

    Lena adapted nicely to her new identity as a masked Qinoori Raider, recreating their howling language so fluently she talked her way out of two of their ambushes. Alex and Kelly grew close, bonding over Alex’s protectiveness and adoration towards Esme. Lex was still angry and willing to help them track down the Daxamites, but he was also burdened with the responsibility of leading their team, and being one of the providers for Amarii and the rest of their huge family. Almeraci gave Amarii shelter, but providing food and money was still the responsibility of her older children. 

     

    The problems weren’t ending with the overall mood of the team. The rivalry between Kara and Reign was palpable, suffocating, scary. Of course Lena knew that neither of them would harm her in any way, but they were dangerous to each other. When Kara went into Lena’s room for the night's rest, Reign became so angry they almost fought and, judging by the insults they threw at each other in Kryptonian, they were ready to fight to the death. After some short contemplation Lena decided to just kick them both out of her room and made them sleep on the couches in the kitchen. They took it differently. Kara was angry, but obeyed Lena’s request, and Reign just revelled in Kara’s misery, when they both made their beds. 

     

    But despite all of that, Lena needed to trust both of them. In her disguise as a Qinoori she was unable to communicate with most of their informants, relying on members of her team. Kara had a special way with words, and Reign, who looked and behaved intimidating enough, was able to get them to spill the information they needed. 

     

    A few days after Alex and Reign joined the team, they received the same message as Kara. They would be treated as fugitives if they did not come back and turn themselves in to The Exodus, preferably with Kara alive, on a leash and Lena’s head in a bag. After two increasingly more threatening messages, Reign crushed the communication beacon in her hand and threw the dust overboard of their ship. Lena watched the tiny metal bits mixing with the sand and being blown away with the wind. She heard soft humming behind her back and turned around, just to see Reign’s eyes glowing with a dangerous red light. 

     

    At first, Reign was against wearing any disguise to hide her identity, brushing off every one of Lena’s suggestions, even refusing to wear a simple black cloak with a hood. But after they were ambushed by the group of mercenaries the size of a small army, she finally gave in. She found a grim-looking mask somewhere and changed her Kryptonian uniform to black padded armour, and finally accepted a black cloak. Reign supplied herself and Kara with two malfunctioning jetpacks that she found in a Hator junkyard to be able to fly and not raise any questions about it. Kelly had a much easier time with Alex, who didn’t have to be told twice and accepted different clothes and cut her hair, at the same time deciding to mostly stay on board and protect the Captain and her kid.

     

    As soon as they started to become desperate, one of the slave traders finally pointed them to someone, who might have some information about the Daxamite wearabouts, so they took off for Sklar. It was a rocky planet, almost bare of any plant life, with more than two thirds of its surface  covered in an enormous ocean. The ship circled it a few times, until they managed to find a spot to land which wasn’t in a stormy area or in the middle of a raider territory. The radar beeped hysterically, showing numerous red dots in the absolute empty desert and Kelly tensed visibly, looking at the smooth sandy surface that seemingly had no end. There was nothing in the desert but the radar still beeped, picking up several living creatures around them. 

     

    Kara shot into the air first, followed closely by Reigh, who huffed angrily behind her back, mad at the thought that the blonde Kryptonian came up with a plan to scout the area first. She overtook Kara and flew around the place, x-raying the ground intensely. Kara just shrugged, seeing Reign’s cocky behaviour and deciding to just ignore her instead of bickering again. She was already tense after so many failures and disappointments and adding an argument with the other Kryptonian wasn’t in her plans today. 

     

    Kara redirected her attention to more important matters and started to scan the ground herself. To her surprise, the radar was right, and under the sandy surface she saw several sand worms. They slept in the depths of the desert sands, still unbothered by the arrival of a spaceship. Kara signed with relief, feeling lucky that they didn’t come when the mating season of these slimy gigantic beasts was ongoing. At least it significantly lowered the possibility of being eaten alive by one of them. She was still watching the ground when she felt Reigh’s heavy gaze on. Kara sighed heavily and looked up, meeting her eyes that were already getting a reddish tint - the clear sign that Reign was pissed.

     

    “Spit it out, already.” - Kara took off her helmet and looked at the other Kryptonian with tired eyes. It was obvious, Reign had something to say, she floated right in front of Kara, her jaw tense and deep frown between her brows.

     

    “You don’t deserve her.” - Reign growled suddenly, startling Kara.

     

    “Who? Lena?” - Kara asked, the realisation hitting her slowly. 

     

    How could she be so naíve? Her dislike towards Reign blinded her, making her think that the other Kryptonian was just angry at her for running away and settling with The Sawflies, who she saw as her friends. Of course she saw all the tender gazes thrown at Lena by Reign, and it angered her, but she dismissed it as the attraction to an obviously beautiful woman. But now she got it finally - Reign was in love with Lena, that’s why she was so annoying and lashed out so often and without any reason. 

     

    “You don’t deserve her.” - Reign repeated stubbornly, letting her eyes lit up with a quiet hum.

     

    “You are right.” - Kara agreed suddenly, startling Reign this time. - “I don’t deserve her and I let her down a lot, but I am trying to be the best me for her. And it doesn’t matter that it’s the matrix that decided to link us together, I would still like her regardless.”

     

    “Oh, yes, the matrix! That fucking thing that ruined everything!” - Reign growled, litting her eyes up again, red glow brighter this time. - “I wanted her to be mine! I… She is dear to me! I built up our relationship from scraps, I fought for her trust and admiration and you - you were just handed things that I worked on for free.”

     

    “It’s not true. You know nothing about us and what we went through together.” - Kara still tried to remain calm, despite her heart thumping in her chest and anger rising up. - “She is not a toy to be tossed around to whomever is most deserving to have her. She is a living being with free will and she has the right to choose herself.” 

     

    “What are you thinking? Would she choose you if you weren’t matched by the matrix? Look at you, you are pathetic.” - Reign spat, sizing up Kara from head to toe with a disgusted grimace. 

     

    “I don’t know.” - Kara admitted, hanging her head low. Would she have allowed Lena to rot in a cell if the matrix hadn’t matched them back then? Probably, yes. What is worse, Kara would not rule out the idea that she would have completely forgotten about Lena after being haunted by her beautiful face for a few nights. Yes, the fire in her heart was there the second Kara met the gaze of these big green eyes. But she was too used to suppressing her own feelings and disregarding her own sympathies. She wouldn’t be surprised if she would have just closed herself off from any feeling that would linger inside her, and just dug deep into her work to numb the pain. 

     

    “Well I would say that she would never. You are wea…” - Reign started, lifting her chin up and looking at Kara condescendingly before being swallowed by the giant worm.

     

    The huge beast emerged from the sands so quietly neither of them heard a thing until Kara saw the shadow of its split jaws closing on Reign’s body. She heard Reign’s yelp, which was cut off instantly when the heavy mandibles closed shut. She put her helmet on and dodged the bulging body of the beast, when it dived deep back into the sea of sand, cutting it easily like a hot knife cuts the butter. She followed it, x-raying the ground, hoping that it will not go deeper, burying the other Kryptonian for good. Luckily the worm was greedy and emerged again, its flat face turned to Kara. It screeched, throwing its head in the direction of the blonde Kryptonian but missing her, when she dodged to the side. 

     

    It staled for a moment, growling, screeching and shaking its head erratically. Kara saw its body glowing red from the inside, making it let out pained noises. It seemed like Reign was still alive and trying to break through the thick skin of the beast. As soon as the heat became too much to bear, the worm dipped into the sand again, crawling away and leaving a wavy path behind it. Kara followed it, taking her helmet off and searing the ground with her own heat vision hoping to anger it enough to get it outside again. 

     

    As strong as Kryptonians were, they still could drown or suffocate and, because of that, Reign was in real danger right now. Kara flew closer to the ground and seared the place where the worm dived deeper into the sand. Its head jumped up with its mouth opened right under Kara’s feet. She soared up as fast as she could, making the worm follow her and extend its body to the limit. She heard a searing sound again and the worm’s body jerked erratically, its head dangling from side to side. 

     

    Kara flew up and then dropped down, adding the power of momentum to the powerful blow she landed on a beast's jaw, stunning it for a few moments. She still heard the searing sound and saw the middle part of the worm’s body glowing red, where Reign still tried to cut herself out of it. She heard the creature roaring and getting high up. It slammed its heavy head on the ground in hopes to hit Kara, but the Kryptonian was faster, dodging this blow too and landing a few heavy blows on the creature's head. It only angered the worm and it jerked erratically, letting out screeching sounds and clicking its mandibles in front of the Kryptonian’s face. Kara flew up again, out of the creature’s reach and took off her helmet, lighting up her eyes and shooting at the beast. 

     

    She flew around it, shooting lasers out of her eyes and dodging its blows. Her attacks had little effect on the beast, its skin rock hard. Kara flew around it again, dropping on its head and punching it at the same time like she did before, as it seemed the only thing that could shake it. She watched a red glow sliding down its throat again and understood that she had little time left to help Reign, or she would suffocate in its sticky guts. Kara worked her heat vision again, this time searing the skin of the beast right where she saw a red glow from Reign’s laser eyes. She heard the skin breaking finally, but the joy was short lived, when the worm slammed its head on the ground again, causing Kara to jump up and jolt into the air. The worm didn’t get up though, only jerked on the ground, screeching. Kara dropped down, right on the creature's head, pinning it to the ground. Then she felt the creature stop moving, she jumped down and found the red glow again. She saw Reign finally cutting through the skin and worked her heat vision to widen the tear. 

     

    Kara pushed her hand into the warm, bloody and damp inside of the worm’s gut and searched for the other Kryptonian, cursing under her breath. She saw the glow so Reign should be here, not sure how unharmed but at least alive. She felt something hard under her fingers and grabbed it, pulling out the other Kryptonian, who gasped for air and coughed out a greenish goo. Kara helped her up and held her steady until she found her ground. 

     

    “Thank you.” - Reign mumbled shortly, not looking at Kara.

     

    “Don’t mention it.” - Kara shrugged and kneeled before the body of the worm to pull out Reign’s black cloak. It was in a sad state, the cloth already starting to get digested in the acid of the worm’s gut. She held the cloak to Reign, who scrunched her nose but took the cloak and unfolded it, evaluating the damage. 

     

    “I guess it will give me a little bit of a tattered look.” - she said, throwing it around her shoulders. It smelled terrible and they both coughed when she shook it in the air and the stench spread around them.

     

    “You look lit.” - Kara wrinkled her nose and looked at Reign. She looked just like she should - like she was chewed on and spit out. Her hair was slimy, her clothes were dirty and her face was covered in a mix of blood and greenish goo. Kara snorted, then apologised, then snorted again, unable to hold in the laugh.

     

    At first Reign looked offended, dusting off her clothes, but her hands stuck on the slime that covered her whole body and she struggled, looking at Kara with angry eyes. She huffed loudly and stopped struggling against her own clothes and just watched Kara, who was giggling louder and louder, finally giving in and folding in half, her body shaking from laughter. Reign smiled, biting her lips from inside not to show her amusement but Kara’s laughter was too contagious. In just a few minutes they were both howling and doubling up.

     

    Kara wiped the tears from her eyes and looked at Reign who was trying to breathe deeper to calm herself. Her unhinged look made her chuckle again and she opened her mouth to let out a quippy remark, but saw Reign looking at the distance, her eyes narrowed and lips pressed in a thin line. Kara turned around and followed her gaze. At first she saw nothing, just an endless sea of sand with rare dry vegetation sprouting here and there, but then she noticed the small hole in the ground. It looked like the den of some animal, who found a place to make its home in the middle of an unforgiving desert full of dangerous giant worms. But logically - no animal would survive there, especially in a den dug in the ground, where it could have been eaten by the predators in a few seconds.  

     

    “Do you see what I see?” - Reign asked, never taking her eyes off that little hole in the ground as if she could lose it if she did.

     

    Kara nodded and took off from the ground, flying closer and putting her helmet on, barely remembering that it was vital now. She felt like she was close to finally knowing what was going on and who was at fault for their troubles. The electronic visor of her helmet was long dead but it was made of a metal that wasn’t obstructing her x-ray vision. She scanned the area, hearing Reign catching up to her and doing the same. 

     

    “No lead.” - Kara affirmed briefly.

     

    “U-huh. And no security outside. No turrets, no cameras.” - Reign hummed, a dangerous smile stretching her lips.

     

    “Those cocky bastards.” - Kara mumbled, cracking her fists. She took off high into the air and dropped back down, her arms stretched in front of her and fists clenched. She crashed into the thick concrete lid that worked as a door that led into the hideout, crushing it into a million small pieces.

     

    “Who is the cocky one here?” - Reign laughed and jumped after Kara who slid inside never stopping to answer her quip. 

     

    The insides of this bunker were huge, full of hi-end tech and Daxamites in a white lab coats. Kara was never happier that they laid their nest here, on a planet that was warmed by the yellow sun. Here, Rao blessed her with unimaginable powers, and she will use them to defeat and finally bring these Daxamites to justice. She watched them cowardly scattering like rats, grabbing things that they saw the most important and running deeper into the building, hiding in the corners and under the furniture. She saw one running to a wall and heard the soft hum of Reign’s heat vision heating up. The beams hit the man a split second after he pressed a button. He yelped and grabbed his burned hand looking at them. Kara saw his intense gaze going from horrified to sinister and frowned under her helmet, puzzled. 

     

    She didn’t have a chance to poke Reign and discuss this weird behaviour. The insides of a bunker lit up with a sickly green light, making her drop down on the floor, writhing in pain. Kara heard Reign grunting besides her, and saw her trying to scramble to her feet but dropping down again, helpless. She saw Daxamites coming out of their hideouts and circling them, chuckling and discussing their Kryptonian impotence with each other. She saw weapons being drawn out -  knives with blades,  laced with Kryptonite, and guns with bullets loaded with it. She grunted, a feeling like hot lava coursing through her veins and blurring her vision. 

     

    Kara tried to fight through it, using all her strength to stand on her knees just to see a gun barrel aimed at her face, but this time it wasn’t Lena’s brother behind it, but a smirking Daxamite guard. Dying, standing on her knees before Daxamites, wasn’t on her bingo card, but there was nothing she could do. She clenched her fist and swung it, gathering all her strength but it was so weak she barely lifted her hand up just to drop it back on the floor, completely drained of any strength. She blinked heavily, watching the Daxamite who mockingly laughed, turning to his friends. He aimed his weapon at her again, his finger squeezing the trigger and Kara readied herself for the impact just to see the man dropping down, nail sticking out of his eye. 

     

    The second one dropped dead before Kara could blink, his body jerking on the floor in a pool of blood. She heard laser bullets whistling around her, laying their captors to rest one by one. James jumped in front of her, working his two shotguns and sending attacking Daxamites flying. J’onn worked his way to the control panel with his fists, shielding her and Reign from the flying kryptonite bullets with his body. Alex joined him, shooting her way forward and punching the unfortunate souls that were too slow to die or run away from her. Nia grabbed two Daxamite guards and slammed them one against another, knocking both out. It seemed like Brainy didn’t even move his finger to fight, making three guards surrounding him shoot each other with his smart moves. Lex jumped over the desks, shooting his small blaster, grabbing one of the scientists in a lab coat by the collar and dragging them to the control panel like a ragdoll. Guards dropped around him before they could move or shoot, a familiar nail sticking out of their eye or skull.

     

    They never wasted energy nor ammo, shooting every Daxamite once or twice. Laser bullets did little to wound them, with the yellow sun empowering them too, but the nails did their job without failure, and Kara suspected that Lena laced them with lead. By the looks of it, James also loaded his shotguns with new ammo, easing Lena’s job and stuffing Daxamites with lead. They cleared the place fast, hoping that their surprise attack would serve its purpose, or they would be overpowered by dozens of powerful aliens, who were only slightly weaker than Kryptonians at their full power. The attack ended shortly, leaving only a few scientists alive and a few guards wounded and groaning on the floor. 

     

    “Kara!” - she heard someone calling her name, but the voice was distant and it took a lot of her strength to turn her head around and see Lena who ran to her and wrapped her arms around her neck, resting Kara’s heavy head on her shoulder. - “Lex! Switch it off already, they are hurt!”

     

    “I am trying, sis.” - Lex yelled from afar and turned to the Daxamite he captured, holding him by the front of his lab coat and shaking him violently.  - “Switch it off, or I will fill your guts with lead like they did with your friends over here.”

     

    “I will do it, just don’t shoot!” - the Daxamite scientist begged after a short contemplation. He clearly saw his colleagues scattered on the ground either dead or dying and decided not to play with luck anymore. He entered the shutdown code into the control panel’s computer and the Kryptonite stacked in the walls was covered with thick metal plates again, cutting the radiation off. 

     

    Kara gasped for air finally, feeling instant relief when the Kryptonite poisoning subsided, leaving her body slowly and finally letting her heal and gather some strength to get up on her feet. She let Lena help her up, clasping her shoulders gently and looking into her eyes. Stupid helm covered her face and Kara pressed her forehead, covered in the cold metal to Lena’s, just to show her affection and gratitude. She saw Lena closing her eyes and swallowing down tears that formed in her eyes. She was missing her own mask, probably forgetting it in the haste. Kara tore herself off Lena just to take her hands into hers and caress the backs of her hands tenderly. 

     

    She caught a glimpse of Reign, who walked past them. There was sadness in her eyes but also understanding and much more. She was not a member of the Children of Juru for nothing after all, and as much as Kryptonians were people of science and logic, believing in destiny and fate was an important part of their sect. Kara saw the sorrow, the pain but also determination and knew that in this exact moment Reign tried to let it go because she truly loved Lena. 

     

    Kara herself didn’t feel worthy of Lena’s love, but she felt warm arms hugging her waist and the face of a human woman hiding in the crevice between her neck and shoulder and understood that she made her choice clear. She looked at Reign and saw her nodding. It was barely visible to anyone but them and Kara nodded in return, promising to do everything in her power to keep Lena safe without saying a word. She hugged Lena back, taking in the warmth of her body and rubbing her helmet against the human’s dark hair. 

     

    People around them raided the place, turning over every dead body and dragging the surviving Daxamite farther from the control panel for him not to do something stupid. Kara scanned the walls, using her x-ray vision and shivered. There were tons of Kryptonite ore stacked in the depths of the bunker, raw and glowing with a sickly green tint. The amount of it could kill everyone on The Exodus and leave the spaceship unlivable for hundreds of years. The refined kryptonite was all around them too, on the shelves behind the thick glass doors, in vials in its liquid form, in cans, split into atoms and made into poisonous gas, in bullets and grenades, in knives and sabres. Even some parts of the guards armour was laced with Kryptonite, its subtle green glow filling the room with a sickly shade. 

     

    “It’s a shit ton of Kryptonite!” - she heard Alex exclaiming, speaking the same thing she was thinking. - “Why do you need it all?” 

     

    “We are just scientists! We don’t have anything to do with it!” - the Daxamite in a lab coat begged from his place on the ground.

     

    “But you worked on it.” - Lena tore herself off Kara and walked towards the scientist, looking at him with a weird expression. Kara never saw her like this -  calm, calculating, cold even. She tilted her head to the side and watched the man with expressionless eyes. - “Tell us, and we will let you go.”

     

    “You will need to protect me then! If they find out that I told you anything, they will kill me!” - he trembled like a leaf under the weather and Lena’s eyes softened with fake concern.

     

    “But of course.” - she crouched before him and put her hand on his knee in a reassuring gesture. - “You were just doing your job, and we don’t judge you for it. My team will take you to the Almerac, where we have friends who will take care of you, darling.  Just tell us everything you know.” 

     

    “How do I know you aren’t lying?” - he yelped, desperate. He was alone here, looking at dozens of people who were ready to kill him on sight but still managed to be feisty before betraying his people. Kara cringed in disgust, looking at him, wiggling before them like an eel on a hot pan. 

     

    “I can assure you we don’t have anything against you. You are just a scientist, like you said and I believe that you have no political implications in this game. So please, tell us what you did here and who was the overseer of this operation, and I promise you we will keep you safe.” - Lena assured him, looking at the Daxamite with her soft, pale eyes. He seemed to be captivated by her beauty and his distrust dissipated slowly.

     

    “We were creating the Krypto-Bomb. His Highness, the Prince of Daxam, was meant to oversee the building of this bomb, but he left for Jambora a few weeks ago.” - he admitted, looking at them all with hope in his eyes. 

     

    “Krypto-Bomb? But why?” - Kara asked, baffled. 

     

    “Because Kryptonians should answer for their crimes against our race!” - he sneered at Kara but then remembered that he was surrounded by people who weren’t happy about their business and calmed down in an instant, hiding his eyes. - “Or so they said when there were briefings. They want to aim it at The Exodus and destroy it, and the people inside of it.”

She sighed and got up on her tiptoes to press her forehead against Kara’s helmet. - “Everything will be okay, trust me.”

     

    Kara sighed as loud as she could to express the depth of her irritation, and took the shackles to clasp them around Lena’s wrists. She saw that Lena liked it a tad too much for the situation they were going into. Her eyes glistened with desire and she caught her lower lip between her teeth. Kara tugged on the shackles, wrapping the thin chain around her fist and suddenly pulled her closer. She heard Lena’s breath hitching and rolled her eyes, flustering despite all the stress she was under right now.

     

    “You are unbelievable!” - she whispered, barely containing the nervous laughter. 

     

    “I need to get into the role you know.” - Lena winked at her and licked her lips, leaving a wet trail on her plump lower one. It made Kara feral.

     

    “Let’s go!” - she was brought back to reality by a heavy clap on her shoulder, Reign walking past her, and nearly knocking her over. She put her mask and cloak on and looked a bit like a big angry owl. Kara straightened her spine and exhaled, calming her emotions. She watched the door slowly opening before her, exposing the impressive view of the Coliseum.

     

    LOG.12: the prince of Daxam

    Chapter Summary

    They finally got to the Prince and questioned him. Kara founds out she is much more angry and dangerous than she thought she herself is.

    Chapter Notes

    Quite gory battle ahead, thread carefully if you are squeamish.
    Blood and gore, a mild ones but still can be too explicit for some people

     

    Happy reading! =D

    “Step right over here.” - one of the Savothian guards droned, looking exceptionally bored. He turned his long face to watch Lex and his entourage descend from the boarding platform and mumbled under his breath, urging them to proceed into the cargo retention area. - “Your reason for arrival?” 

     

    “A shipment for his majesty!” - Lex proclaimed ceremoniously, brushing invisible dust off his elegant suit before crossing his arms on his chest. -”He ordered the best slaves in the Galaxy and I delivered. Two beautiful bedside girls and two strong gladiators.”

     

    “Leave them here and we will have them delivered.” - the guard mumbled, gesturing somewhere in the vague direction of the back room. His partner turned around and looked at them with half closed eyes, chewing something slowly. He lost interest as soon as he saw the slaves, which wasn’t a surprise. They were overly accustomed to slave trading on Jambora and the camel-like Savothians saw humans as ugly, preferring their own race over anyone else.

     

    “No-no-no! I will not do business like this! I brought fresh, clean and healthy slaves specifically to be delivered  straight to the prince's apartments! I will not ‘leave them here’ to be starved and forgotten before you remember to ship them or even feed them and the men are expected to be in the pit today, not being stored here to wither away.” - Lex growled, looking extremely irritated. - “I will deliver them myself or I will never do business here again and your governor will hear about it!”

     

    “Okay man, chill!” - the guard backed off, even opening his eyes fully for a short moment before falling into a half-asleep mood again and going to get a tablet on which he scrolled through something for a long few minutes before humming again.. - “M-kay. So slaves - human?”

     

    “Yes, all four.” - Lex lied without batting an eye. The Savothian contemplated for a long moment again before going back to his desk and taking a small device into his hands. He walked towards the chained up Lena and Nia, pressing some buttons on it and shaking it, its screen blinking a few times before finally fully activating. 

     

    The closer he came the more tense Kara became. They just disembarked from their spaceship and made literally no progress just to be stopped by two bored guards. Kara didn’t know they had a device that could tell their races and the unexpected obstacle made her heart race like crazy. She felt cold creeping up her spine and her palms becoming sweaty. She squeezed her fist to stop the subtle tremble of her fingers and watched how the guard approached them slowly. Thankfully, Lena stood closer to him so he poked her in the neck, taking a blood and cell sample. Kara watched her squirm from the sudden pain. A big drop of blood formed over the little pick on her skin and the bigger this drop became the angrier Kara grew. 

     

    “Careful!” - Lex yelped suddenly, startling them all. - “What are you doing? You are messing up the goods! Oh my word, what are you thinking? Am I supposed to  bring the prince a bloodied slave? Are you absolutely crazy??”

     

    “Okay, chill man!” - Savothian lifted his hands in a calming gesture and threw a quick look on the screen of the device. It lit up green, confirming that Lena’s race is indeed human. He scratched his maned head slowly, looking at Lex with slight concern. It was clear he was torn between doing his duties and dealing with the fussy client. The inner battle was short and he finally let out the prolonged hum, convincing himself that today is the day when he doesn't want to bother. - “ The others are human too?” 

     

    “Of course! I already told you so!” - Lex rolled his eyes and took out the stack of fake papers that they cooked up earlier with the help of Miss Grant to shake them in front of Savothian’s face.

     

    “M-kay…. And the guards? Mmm.. human?”  - he looked at his device and then at Kara, Querl and Reign with a hint of worry. Kara held her breath for that long moment while the guard watched them with a mild interest. She realised how they all looked right now - herself, fully covered in armour, Reign in a battered black clothes and stupid mask, and Querl who was covered in a pale-human-skin coloured concealer from head to toe, hints of green skin showing in places where his clothes already rubbed it off. 

     

    “Do you think I would pay someone other than humans to guard my product?”  - Lex exclaimed dramatically, taking Lena by her thin wrist and shaking her arm in the air, showing how completely harmless his ‘product’ was.

     

    “M-kay, m-kay, I think we have clearance.” - the guard tore his gaze off the three guards and hummed looking at his partner who hadn't moved all this time, feigning a pile of clothes on a chair. Kara let out the breath she held in, slowly and carefully so no one would hear it. - “ Do you need to be escorted? I think we have people free down there…”

     

    “No! With poor service like this I don’t need or desire an escort! I am sure they will be as horrible and slow as you two are. Oh. My. Word!” - Lex squealed and jerked the thin chain and dragged Lena and Nia along. Both girls made exaggeratingly scared faces that looked extremely fake and stupid, but the guards were too occupied with their urge to be rid of the fussy client.

     

    Kara relaxed only when they were let out into the inner sanctum of the Coliseum and walked its wide corridors. Lex radiated confident and perky energy, passing numerous guards and attendants. They walked through the ground floor without any further interruptions. James moved quickly, showing them the way through the familiar grounds, until they came to a small door in the wall. It was so narrow Kara would totally miss it if James hadn’t abruptly stopped in front of it. He looked at Brainy discreetly, flicking his eyes to the small device on the wall near this door. The Coluan understood him without words and walked towards it, pulling out a small sticky chip that he placed on it, connecting to it through his own brain and unlocking it with ease.

     

    “Good luck.” - Lex patted J’onn’s shoulder and nodded to James who pulled the Martian inside.

There was still too much to do, too little time, and with only a handful of people against a whole army. She tried to suppress the anxiety but each time she relaxed another guard passed them by or a random attendant rested their eyes on her for too long and it creeped back again.

     

    “Don’t fuck it up.” - Kara heard Reign hissing and turned her head to look at her. She wore a grave expression and gave Kara an angry gaze. It was a sudden quip and Kara frowned under her helmet.

     

    “Why do you think I would fuck it up?” - she asked quietly, trying to remain calm. They just came to more or less neutral terms and Kara would hate to see them going back to being enemies.
