    “It’s not anger, I am just stressed out.” - Kara hissed, maybe too loud. Lex turned around and watched them for a long moment with a fearsome expression that promised them a good old ass kicking if they tried to argue in the middle of enemy territory. 

     

    “Hm.” - Reign hummed, still frowning.

     

    “Concentrate on the task, not on my feelings, Reign.” - Kara hissed, irritated.

     

    “I will concentrate on you both if you don’t stop this shit.” - Nia suddenly turned to them and whispered loudly, attracting a few weird gazes. 

     

    Kara panicked, looking around hoping that they didn’t attract too much attention. There were a few guards around who looked at them, bored out of their minds from idly standing around the corridors and watching rich guests arriving and leaving. A Graxion, wearing a rich golden robe that clashed with his emerald green skin, sized Nia up from head to toe and made a disgusted face, watching the “insolent slave” being disobedient like that. 

     

    “Shut up or I will tear you a new one, slut!” - Reign growled loudly and made a fearsome face. It made the guards chuckle but they lost interest as soon as Nia turned around and lowered her gaze coyly.

     

    “Wow, dude, take it down to like…. seven.” - she whispered, snorting under her breath. 

     

    “Sorry.” - Reign mumbled, pushing her forward slightly and looking at the nearest guard boldly, making him look away when her fierce gaze became too much for him to hold. 

     

    Lena turned her head and looked at Kara, winking slyly, which startled the Kryptonian. It was a mess of an operation but for some reason it was still working, like they were led on by some miracle. Kara thanked Rao quietly which again attracted the attention of Reign who looked at her like she grew a second head. Their acting was atrocious, their undercover clothes were lame and they behaved like they were at the park and not surrounded by at least a hundred enemies on a hostile planet. They were literally behind enemy lines with their asses bare and Kara felt like nobody took it seriously. She pressed her lips into a thin line and looked back at Lena. She just started to feel annoyed at Lena who didn’t react at her serious face at all, and then remembered she had a helmet on. She suppressed a pained groan.

     

    Lena seemed to be extremely satisfied with herself and walked forward, passing some guards and guests who ogled her with hungry eyes. Kara grew angry when she saw these oily gazes, directed at her lover, her mate. She looked back at them and perhaps it was sheer hatred that she emanated but they averted their gazes and went to their own business. It gave her a little bit of satisfaction, but also filled her with anxiety that creeped up her spine, cold and sticky. This building was filled with people who would wish them harm as soon as they knew who they were. Dangerous, cruel and immoral they would kill them on the spot without mercy, or would take them captive. and Kara didn’t even want to try to think about the things that would happen to them, and especially to Lena in that case.

     

    “I believe this might be the lift to the prince’s lounge.”  - Brainy mumbled under his breath and nodded in the vague direction of encrusted double doors. It was guarded by two bulky Daxamites who watched them with disdain, but let them in as soon as they saw ‘slave girls’. It was a relief, but Kara still had a feeling that it was all too easy. 

     

    “Ok, so once again - we go in, we mingle and when they all loosen up we neutralise the bodyguards and interrogate him. Like we planned earlier.” - Kara gabbled, fixing her armour and squaring her shoulders. 

     

    “Of course, it will all go according to plan, starting right now.” - Brainy folded hands behind his back stiffly. As always his emotions were hard to read but Nia hummed confidently and Kara wondered again how she understood him so completely.

     

    “We can always improvise anyway.” - Lex shrugged, fixing the white cuffs of his fancy suit and watching Lena scratching an itchy spot on her head. They both looked absolutely relaxed and it drove Kara crazy. She shifted uncomfortably under Reign’s gaze who scoffed at her again. The other Kryptonian looked as stressed as Kara herself. Reign frowned deeply as her eyes drilled holes in the encrusted pattern of the heavy double doors that finally opened slowly, letting them all into the spacious room.

Every swirl was coated with gold and a lot of different precious gems were scattered all over the pattern. Kara was initially struck by its richness, but, when she shook off her first impression, she saw how loud, gaudy and pretentious it was. Right in the middle of the room stood a huge bed covered in a golden sheet. It was round and  rested on a high pedestal, its legs made of golden plated material. On top of this bed lounged the Prince himself.

Cat didn’t lie - there were twenty of them, scattered all around the room and on the balcony outside. These bodyguards were armed with electrified staves regular for Daxamites and wore their usual armour. They looked a little bit more vigilant than those outside the Prince’s suite and Kara decided that she shouldn’t underestimate them.

Both girls wore the skimpiest outfits they could find, bras barely covering their nipples and small panties leaving almost nothing to the imagination. They both were smeared with oil that contained the finest glitter and made their skin gleaming softly in the bright white light of the beautiful yellow sun that shines upon Jambora. She saw the Prince ogling both girls and licking his lips and felt the power of Rao flowing through her veins. She breathed slowly, in and out, never letting her emotions take over, her mind clear and cold. 

     

    She took notice of every guard and their every movement, sharp and vigilant. Her eyes flicked on super speed, her hearing sharper than it ever was, her body tense like a string, ready to go right into the action. She heard a soft hum on her right and smiled, satisfied. Reign scanned the room with her X-Ray vision, picking up every crease and every dark corner of the room, watching for additional guards in the numerous secret rooms around them. She never knew that Kryptonian bodies made all these little sounds when their power activates, taking it for granted. But now, when the fates of her human teammates and the woman she loved depended on her, she felt like she could hear the wings of a fly in a room over a hundred miles away. 

     

    “These are nice slaves you have here, trader.”  - the Prince mumbled, looking exaggeratingly bored. He looked behind Lex’s back, ignoring both Nia and Lena and stared at the three guards with a weird interest. He searched the faces of Reign and Brainy but nothing seemed to catch his attention until he looked at Kara. She hoped that he just wanted to make sure they were at ease and not armed. It’s a completely normal thing to do, Kara thought, and breathed in and out slowly again, relaxing her tense muscles and slouching slightly to look less intimidating. - “But I am more interested in your guard.”

Kara was startled too, her eyes flicking from Lex to Prince and back. - “What is so interesting about the guard? She is a cheap muscle I hired on Megastructure.”

    “Don’t fret, come closer.” - the Prince beckoned her again. Kara stepped closer, watching his face. He looked like he was bored out of his mind and just looking for any entertainment he could find. It was one of those small devices that reacted to the residual genetic material of a felon, detecting them from a relatively short distance. Betty will appear on the radars of all the Daxamite ships and they will overrun them in minutes. Lena braced herself and pulled out the wires on her wrist to insert them in as soon as they were online.

     

    She did it as fast as she could - fired up the engine and pulled Betty’s comms from the sleep mode. The green round radar screen wasn’t so green right now - hundreds of small blinking dots painted it red. She put the wires into the communicator and sent a code to the Sawflies again. First one went into the void but with the second one she saw someone confirming the distress signal. She exhaled loudly, puffing the stray strand of hair out of her face and laughing happily, looking at Kara. 

     

    “We need to go!” - Sam jumped in, pulling Lena by the hand away from the control panel and out of the ship. Kara already opened the landing gear and flew out, only to see Daxamite ships landing all over the place and troops jumping out to the ground. Lena turned around and saw Sam putting a long purple crystal on the panel; she didn’t have time to ask about it, when the other Kryptonian dragged her out into the forest. 

     

    After just a few moments the area was crawling with Daxamite troops, metal plates of their armour glistening between branches. In hopes to at least try to save Betty they crawled farther away from it, making as much noise as they could, pulling the troops or at least part of them farther from the old ship. Kara and Sam exchanged some peculiar signs and Reign jumped into the thick bushes just to jump out on the other side and show Kara two fingers and an “o” sign that she made with her other hand. Kara nodded thoughtfully and Lena relaxed, knowing that even if she doesn't understand shit right now these Kryptonian girls know what they are doing. She crouched in the tall grass and froze on the spot, trying not to make any sound or movement that might disrupt any plan they had right now. 

     

    She understood what Sam meant when at least two dozen troops jumped out of the forest and attacked both Kryptonians. Not limited by any obstacles now they both launched into the air, gaining high ground instantly and burned a few down with their heat vision. Lena’s breath hitched when she saw one of the Daxamites taking out a gun loaded with something that glowed green. She pulled her rifle out as carefully and quietly as she could, praying to every deity she knew so they wouldn’t hear her and aimed for the one with a gun. Kara melted the face of one of the Daxamites and turned to this one, finally seeing the Kryptonite-loaded gun and sent two beams of blue energy in his direction, He tried to jump out of the harm’s way but stumbled and fell down on his face, a long nail loaded with led sticking out of back of his head.

     

    Reign and Kara floated low in the air, back to back, red and blue beams cutting Daxamite flesh open and melting their skin off the bones so fast they had little time to run away. Lena finished off at least two but she had a huge disadvantage, her rifle too heavy in her arms and without the possibility to set it down and aim it like it was designed to be. Unfortunately her fussing around attracted a lot of attention, at least three of the Daxamites turning around to look at her and one running to the very place she hid. 

     

    Lena cursed under her breath, aiming for his head but the recoil sent her shot too high. It was already too late to shoot a second time, her rifle was as useless in close combat as Lena herself, but the Daxamite didn’t even have time to lift his weapon against her, when Sam grabbed him by the head, squeezing her hand so hard her fingers poked into his eyes, messing them up and threw him away like a ragdoll. He landed on a broken tree branch with the nastiest crunch. She looked at Sam gratefully and the Kryptonian nodded, smirking at her triumphantly.

     

    They fought their way out onto some kind of clearing from the troops, just to have enough time to fly up and away but the skies were black with the Daxamite ships and the forest crawled with hostiles. Lena saw the Kryptonians getting tired - Kara still landed crushing punches but she swung much slower, letting more punches through and preferring to fight on the ground. Reign lost her cockiness, her breath heavy and movements less precise. She also seemed to conserve her power, landing on her feet and preferring fist fight over heat vision and flight. 

     

    Lena still had a few dozen nails which she used carefully, thrifty, willing herself to pull the trigger only when she was sure she would shoot to kill. She methodically took out the troops that were unnoticed by either of the Kryptonians and moved locations often not to allow herself to sit for too long in one place. There was a faint moment of hope they would make it, when it looked like Kara had punched down the last Daxamite so they could catch their breath only for more of them to come jumping out of the forest and start attacking them again. 

     

    Lena saw Kara’s heavy breaths and a thin trickle of blood tickling her brow. It was like the fight with the cyborgs all over again, but now they couldn’t fly up and away because the whole sky was hostile to them, too. There was nowhere to run and nowhere to hide and Lena started to accept the fact that this would be the end when she saw a Daxamile falling down with a bullet in his head… then a second one fell down right near her, and a third. She turned her head around, trying to see what was happening when she finally got a glimpse of familiar faces between the trees. The Sawflies.

     

    She didn’t know how and when they received the message and how they came here so fast but she was just happy to see them. They had overrun this Daxamite squad and scattered all over the forest to finish off everyone they met. It was the happiest surprise she had in ages and it gave her and the Kryptonians the opportunity to retreat and catch their breath.

     

    They crawled through the forest in search of any opening and any possibility to get back and regroup. The woodland there was really impenetrable, thick trees nestled close to one another and the thickest bushes covering the forest floor. In less than a hour of travelling through there Lena felt her face and hands bleeding, covered in dozens of small cuts from the thin spikes on the branches. Kara turned back and looked at her, her face distorting in sympathy but Lena offered her an encouraging smile and waved her tiny injuries off. They will heal, unlike those wounds that Daxamites will inflict on them if they stop and give up. 

     

    Finally they found the edge of the forest. It was still very dense and only went from tall thick trees to just less thick greenery growths but they could still see the area more clearly. But Lena regretted it as soon as they emerged from the woods. There were the Sawflies, almost all of them, but they were small in numbers, compared to Daxamites. As soon as the Queen’s army regained their footing after the initial attack by the raiders they overwhelmed them easily, throwing them around and killing them like livestock.

     

    It was a brutal sight - seeing how Kryptonians tossed Daxamites around. Lena forgot how powerful they were in comparison to mere humans, and here she saw it on full display. The Sawflies were crushed like an army of toy soldiers, their fragile bodies crumbling under the brutal attacks of the ruthless Daxamites. The Sawflies weren’t cowards either, jumping them again and again, shooting at them, sending them fleeing in droves, but they were overwhelmed, not only in terms of power but also outnumbered ten Daxamites to every one Sawfly.

     

    She saw them fall one by one, broken and bloody, despite Kara’s and Sam’s best efforts, going back into the battle as soon as they saw it. Daxamites were brutal - they crushed skulls and tore limbs out, breaking the fragile human flesh easily. She saw her friend Jess falling under the crushing strike of a Daxamite soldier, just to see him lifting his staff and admiring the blood and gore that stuck to it. She swallowed down a whimper and searched the area with her eyes. There wasn’t a lot she could do right now, just try to find someplace to lay down and shoot until her nails loaded with lead run out. 

     

    Her eyes suddenly found an abandoned Daxamite ship. She contemplated for a few moments before deciding to run for it. She needed to make it there, to send another signal - this time to The Exodus. The Sawflies weren’t enough, obviously, and now their only hope was a bunch of hammer-headed and arrogant Kryptonians. 

     

    She took off from her small hiding spot behind a pile of rocks as fast as she could, hoping that no one will see her or at least she will be faster than any of the Daxamites who were occupied with the fight, She made it quickly, jumped into the ship and looked back nervously, luckily seeing no one behind her. She walked towards the control panel, biting her lower lip and squinting at the alien runes. She didn’t have a lot of knowledge of the Daxam language but the comms looked almost the same in any spaceship so she stuck the wires from her wrist wrap in the slit that looked the most familiar.

     

    Not unexpectedly, the signal from the simpler hacking device was so weak it didn't even penetrate the firewall of the ship. Lena pulled it out, disappointed and confused. Daxamites had great protection from malware and other intrusions, which was somewhat unexpected but also logical. Lena looked at the panel, frowning, like it would give up from the sheer amount of her disappointment but it didn’t falter. Lena looked over her shoulder again, watching the tall grass bending from the weight of the wind when she saw blood being splattered over it and the body of one of the Sawflies falling down. A Daxamite walked over it, going to the next Sawfly, fighting him and overpowering them quickly, cracking his skull after a fight that was just a few seconds long. 

     

    Lena felt her eyes becoming wet. Funny, she didn’t even feel the tears staining her cheeks. She slowly took off her headpiece and felt a throbbing headache flashing just behind her eyes. It was nothing she couldn’t power through. She pulled the wires out from the back of her neck, putting them into the sockets of the control panel. She felt the familiar dizziness when her sentience went into the machine, probing it, searching for the familiar code.

     

    “Greetings, Lena Luthor.” - she felt an electronic voice inside her head. She didn’t understand what it was at first, her head felt too light and unaccustomed to the feeling. The crown dampened her ability to hack significantly and now she was like a toddler in an unfamiliar room. She panicked when the understanding hit her - it was an AI. And it knew her. She tried to get back to her senses but it didn’t allow her, wrapping her mind into the mist, like in a warm blanket. - “Don’t go, stay.” 

     

    “You are an AI.” - Lena said, just to say anything and prolong the time.

     

    “You are a smart one! Yes, I am. My name is Lars, I was named in honour of the late husband of Queen Rheia.” - it said and Lena felt a hint of pride in it. It was unusual for AI to feel this way and Lena understood now - it was an advanced one, and it will be the death of her. She braced herself for the mental fight, probably her last, and likely shortest one. No human brain could survive the attack of an advanced AI and she wasn’t an exception. - “I know what you are trying to do, it’s futile.”

     

    “Is it?” - Lena asked, searching for any weaknesses and openings in the system’s firewall. There weren’t any.

     

    “Yes. I am the most advanced AI in the galaxy and right now I will start to kill off your neurons to neutralise you. You are a threat, Lena Luthor. A minor one but still something I need to take care of.” - it asserted in a condescending tone. Lena would have chuckled if she could still feel her body.

     

    “That’s sad isn’t it? Flesh against a machine, and machines always win, don't they?” - there it is, the smallest opening in the firewall, an oversight of the creator, a tiny breakage in the code. Lena lingered around it, trying not to attract any attention just to squeeze only the smallest part of her consciousness through it, sending the most desperate signal to The Exodus. Now she could only hope it will get through. She saw the AI turning its attention to her motions, probing her again and playing on its vanity again.

     

    “I am sorry, Kara.” - her mind suddenly cried out, getting all the attention of the AI.

     

    “Ah, the pathetic notions of the weak organisms.” - it chuckled, proud of itself for never giving into such weak urges, like those living beings did. 

     

    “Funny, huh? It can be our weakness or our strength.” - Lena said, her inner voice steady. The signal went through and she was ready to pay any price that came with it. She braced herself when she felt the AI intruding into her mind, probing it thoroughly, dissecting it like it was a new curious organism.

     

    “That’s a shame, Lena Luthor. You are smart, you could rival me probably, if you were born in another age.” - AI contemplated, with a tint of regret.

     

    “Ah well, but I didn’t.” - Lena said calmly. She was irritated slightly, the AI played around, prolonging her suffering. 

     

    She didn’t feel her body but she felt the neurons reacting to the intrusion of AI intensely, probably sending her body in a violent seizure right now. She concentrated on the outward feelings of her body, trying to have at least the vaguest idea what is going on outside of her mind, just to feel, to see for the last time, but the AI held her tight, not letting her out, trapping her for good. She emerged just for a moment to hear Kara’s desperate voice somewhere over the ear of her unconscious body. 

     

    “No! Lena, No! Please, come back, Lena!” - Kara cried out with such desperation, and with such dread. 

     

    Lena tugged at her consciousness, it was dulled so much she really thought it was dead. Maybe it is, judging by Kara’s desperate cries, but is it better to go back into the dead body or should she choose to be destroyed as a being by the AI? It was a choice between two evils but there was also no inbetween so she made an inhuman effort and pulled off the AI. Her motion made him angry, which wasn’t really a surprise, knowing who created him. She pulled up, closer to the light, closer to her consciousness or what was left of it. 

     

    She emerged back, only a part of her consciousness comprehending the pain her body was in right now. It was a weird feeling, the sudden clarity, Kara’s face with tears pouring out of her blue eyes. Were her eyes really that blue or was the colour elevated when she cried? She didn’t know and probably never will, her mind falling into the pitch black darkness.

     

    LOG.14: mine is the fury

    Chapter Summary

    Kara is angry. VERY angry.

    Kara shot into the air fuelled by grief and rage. She let out a pained roar, melting Daxamites left and right, dipping down to grab the ones closest to her just to tear them apart like they were made of paper. She threw their remains into the crowd on the ground, scattering them. She sensed Reign finishing off a few people on the ground, exhausted already, but Kara didn’t feel fatigue, her grief fueling her, making her eyes burn like molten lava that she poured out onto the people that just killed Lena.

     

    Just a few minutes ago she saw her running into the safety of the spaceship. Kara never let the human out of her sight, always watching her every move and tracking her around the battlefield ready to protect with her life. She seemed completely fine, her heartbeat steady and breath calm. After all they’ve been through, concentrating on Lena’s heartbeat quickly became a habit of hers. It was not as slow and steady as the heartbeats of Kryptonians, it had its own unique rhythm. It was slower when she slept, faster when she was awake and fluttered unsteadily when she writhed under her, with Kara’s name on her lips. 

     

    Everything was fine for a few minutes, until Kara heard Lena struggling for breath, her heartbeat irregular. She wrestled with another Daxamite, eager to dash to her rescue. She didn’t know what she would see when she threw the Daxamite away with a broken neck and rushed inside to find Lena on the floor, her headpiece lying on the ground right near her. She was seizing violently, her body tilting up and muscles tied in knots. Kara rushed to her, holding her tight, not allowing her to hit her head on the ground anymore. 

     

    She looked terrifying, her eyes bloodshot, red tears pouring down her cheeks. Her ears and mouth were bleeding too, and Kara unintentionally smeared the blood all over Lena’s face and hair, while trying to calm her down. It looked bad. Kara saw the wires coming out of her neck into the ship’s control panel and tugged at them immediately. Her first instinct was just trying to disconnect Lena, tear loose of the thing that was obviously hurting her. But as soon as she touched them she heard Alex’s voice yelling something. Kara lifted her face stained with tears and Lena’s blood and looked at her sister with pain in her eyes.

     

    “Don’t do that, Kara!” - Alex repeated, extending her arm to the Kryptonian, trying to calm her down at least a bit. Kelly came in after Alex and gasped as soon as she laid eyes on Lena. Kara pulled her hands away from the wires and hugged Lena, humming tenderly, looking at her. She tried to wipe the blood off her face again but just messed her up more. 

     

    “It’s hurting her.” - she whined like a child, looking at Alex with pain in her eyes. 

     

    “I know, but we cannot disconnect her. It looks like Daxamite ship has some kind of AI and she is fighting it. If you sever the connection she will certainly die.” - Kelly kneeled near Kara and touched her shoulder tenderly.  - “While she is connected to it and fighting she still has the chance.”

     

    “Her heartbeat is slowing down, Kelly.” - Kara whispered wetly, looking at the human  beside her like she could do something. Kelly gave her a sad, calm smile and squeezed her arm, giving the only comfort she could in the moment - a little bit of support. It didn’t help at all. Kara squeezed Lena, who already went limp in her arms. She rocked with her slightly, lulling her to sleep, listening to her heart stopping.

     

    “She was here with us, Kara, surrounded by family, she…” - but Alex never finished, Kara jolting out of the ship with a pained growl, straight into battle, throwing Daxamites around. 

     

    She went through the battlefield like a hot knife through butter, cutting through the numerous enemy lines with her heat vision, screaming at the top of her lungs, pouring out all the sorrow that tore her apart. Tears rolled down her cheeks, heated up to the boiling point by her own heat vision, leaving deep burns around her eyes, but she felt nothing. She went numb as soon as she stopped hearing Lena’s heartbeat. She felt no pain and no exhaustion, tearing into the enemy lines like a tornado, throwing Daxamites around, breaking their bones and melting them with her heat vision. 

     

    “Kara Zor-El of The Exodus.” - she heard through the veil of her rage and turned to the source of the irritating noise. Who would divert her thoughts from her grief right now? The fact that someone was rude enough to talk to her right now, when she lost everything was infuriating. She watched the big Daxamite ship approaching, the holographic image of Queen Rheia herself appearing on the bow of the ship, her face triumphant. She looked down at Kara and pressed her lips in a thin line before speaking up again.. - “Your resistance will be crushed and you will be brought to justice along with the remainder of your disgusting, vile race that dared to survive after you destroyed our planet. Your pathetic human mate is dead and now it’s your time to die, too.”

     

    “You! Don’t! Speak! Of! Her!” - Kara roared, the pool of molten lava spreading behind her eyes, making the skin of her face glow from inside, her irises fiery and pupils white. 

     

    “You don’t speak of what I can or can’t do, Kryptonian. You are nothing and I will make sure none of your little human friends will survive today. We will finish the Kryptocannon and obliterate your precious spaceship from existence, and this will be just a start. I have big plans for this galaxy! I will be the Queen of Andromeda and you will die right now, Commander. Open fire!” - Rheia ordered, her image fading and laser cannons aiming at Kara, barrels lighting up with blue energy.

     

    The Kryptonian darted forward at a speed she herself didn’t know she could achieve. She evaded bullets, zooming between them, seeing them like they were in slow motion until she landed with her knees on the windshield. She looked inside, her overheated eyes darting from one terrified Daxamite to another, in search of Rheia. She could only imagine how she looks now, but she didn’t care about it. She died with Lena in that abandoned ship and now her lifeless husk, fuelled by hatred, seeks revenge. She didn’t see the queen, she must have hid somewhere, like the coward she is. 

     

    “You killed her!” - Kara growled, slamming her fists into the windshield. It cracked under the enormous strength of her blows, covering the glass in a cobweb pattern. - “You insulted her! You made her suffer! You framed her! You destroyed everything!” 

     

    She pummeled the glass with devastating blows, pinpointing her every exclamation. She saw the window cracking and breaking under her fists, revelling in the sight of the Daxamite guards that watched her raging just outside their spaceship. She let out a chilling scream, melting the window with her heat vision. She prayed to Rao with the last bits of her faith to give her more strength, no matter what it does to her body. She can burn alive along with this ship and it would barely bring her the content she seeked. 

     

    Kara saw Reign darting past her, her heat vision melting a Daxamite who jumped high into the air to knock Kara off the ship and landing on the roof of the ship. Two more figures landed near her, all wearing black, their dark cloaks flapping in the wind. Kara narrowed her eyes, looking at them, trying to ascertain who they were and stopping her assault for a short moment. Both women wore a Sigil of Yuda Kal, its skeletal outline glistening under the sun. They launched up, attacking the cannons that assaulted those on the ground. Reign tore into the roof, tearing the panel off it and grabbed a bunch of wiring, breaking it and disrupting communications between the ships. It sent a panic through the attacking drones and smaller shuttles which lost the precision of their attacks and scattered across the sky.

     

    Kara was so close to breaking into it and tearing those who were inside apart. Only a few more blows and she will bring them all to justice. It will not take her pain away, but it will at least give her a bit of satisfaction. They will never hurt anyone again, Kara will make sure of it. That’s what Lena would want her to do. They didn’t deserve to have their fun watching bloody Gladiator fights, raping helpless slaves and indulging in their gluttonous pleasure parties while people outside starved. They didn’t deserve to live. None of them. Kara roared again, pouring all her rage out and landing a few devastating hits on the window, watching it give way under her fists.

     

    “Commander!” - she heard a familiar voice but it was faint, sounding like it came from far away. She ignored it, digging her fingers into the broken glass of the window. She smirked dangerously when it moved under her fingers. This glass was made to withstand the pressure of hyperspace travel but it crumbled under her might which gave her almost ecstatic satisfaction. - “Kara! It’s me, Kal!”

     

    “Who?” - she tore her blurry vision away from the business at hand and looked at the vaguely familiar Kryptonian who floated beside her and watched her with a mix of pity and dread. She knew that she looked terrifying right now - her hair dishevelled, heat vision scorched skin around the inside of her eyes, tear streaks burned deep into her cheeks. She watched his eyes darting from her face to her bloodied hands and back, his mouth opening and closing a few times, like he was a fish that was thrown out of the water by the current.

     

    “It’s me, Kal-el, your cousin.” - he tried again, watching her face for traces of recognition. She hummed in passing acknowledgement, her eyes heavy with sadness suddenly, but after just a moment of weakness her face hardened again. She squeezed her jaw together, turning back to the Daxamite ship, ready to get back to breaking through that stubborn window. - “Kara, listen to me. They are done for, we had them surrounded. Lena, your mate, she sent a signal to The Exodus. We came as soon as we could. A whole flotilla of both the Space Guards and the Exodus is here, rounding up the remaining Daxamite troops.”

     

    “You are too late. She is dead.” - Kara heard her own voice like it didn’t belong to her. It was raspy and hollow, stripped of any emotion. 

     

    “Kara!” - she looked down, seeing Alex waving to her. She felt a wave of jealousy and bitterness. Alex had Kelly, she saw that budding relationship, ready to bloom into something more than amicable respect and friendship. Kara just lost everything, her love, her life, her shining star and guiding moonlight. She wasn’t sure she was ready to look at other people being happy. Yes, it was selfish but the wound on her soul was still too fresh. She saw a worried smile on Alex’s face and it angered her. There was no place for it and she scoffed, irritated. - “Lena is still alive!”

     

    Kara looked at her, offended deeply. This was not the right time for jokes, especially cruel ones like this. She felt heat behind her eyes, burning the cracked skin on her face. If it had been anyone else she would melt them where they stood but it was Alex and as hard as it was, she stopped herself and closed her eyes to calm the storm inside. She breathed deeply and exhaled slowly, trying to concentrate on something that didn’t exist anymore. She felt another wave of grief, overtaking her again and she tried to shake it off, lifting her face to the sky, and taking a long deep breath through her nose. 

     

    And then she heard it. It was quiet, slightly unsteady and weak, but it was there - the familiar sound of Lena’s heartbeat. Kara couldn’t believe it at first, dismissing it as delirium after such a devastating fit of grief. Kara froze in the air, listening to it and waiting with dread for it to disappear at any moment, but it was still here even after she waited. 

     

    She finally found the strength to go down and landed on her feet just near Alex, who looked at her with sympathy, rubbing her hand on Kara’s shoulder. Kara hesitated for a bit before going back into the ship, dreading what she would find inside. She inhaled like before diving into the cold water and entered it without further contemplation. 

     

    Lena was laying on the floor, her head on Kelly’s lap. Kara lowered herself on the floor beside them, trying to blink her heat vision away. She squeezed her eyes a few times, calming her fire and trying to cool down. It was hard to do, having overheated so much that she was just able to bring back the blue tint of her irises, hoping the rest of her eyes' normal colour would return when she let them rest and cool down some more. 

     

    Lena’s face was deathly pale, the streaks of caked on blood staining her upper lip from the nose down. It seems like Kelly tried to wipe it off but only partly succeeded. She was completely limp, all her muscles relaxed so much her jaw sagged and eyes were half opened. Kara bent over her, putting a palm on her cheek and looking for any sign of life but Lena was completely still, one of her pupils dilated and other constricted. Her head rolled to the side helplessly when Kelly moved under her, letting Kara take her into her arms. Her condition looked as severe as Kara dreaded but she was still alive, her heart beating steadily, albeit weak.

     

    Kara scooped her into her arms, Alex helping to rest Lena’s head on her shoulder so as not to allow for it to roll back. She walked out holding her carefully like a fragile treasure, afraid to breathe on her so as not to worsen her condition somehow. Kal waited for her outside, his face contorted into a pained expression when he saw Kara with her mate unconscious in her arms. It was silent around them, the sounds of battle fading as soon as they took the mother ship with the Queen on board and the rest of Daxam troops surrendering. 

     

    Kara saw her parents walking toward her, her father smiling at her and mother wearing a restrained expression. Kara looked her into their eyes, searching for something and saw nothing but uncertainty. She was too numb to be disappointed in the lack of emotion on her mother’s face and the cluelessness of her father so she just looked them in the eyes, clutching Lena in her arms. She felt a gush of air when all three Children of Juru landed near her, surrounding them in a protective circle. Reign came forward, throwing a worried gaze upon Lena and staying close enough to jump to protect her, but far enough not to obstruct the line of sight between Kara and her mother. Her sisters Purity and Pestilence stayed back, guarding them from behind.

     

    “You have proof of her innocence now, mother. She sent out the signal, she saved The Exodus risking her life for the people who betrayed her.” - Kara watched her mother’s face drop, her lips pressing in a thin line again. She knew this stubborn expression and knew that no matter what, she wasn’t ready to accept Lena but she was for sure ready to leave her to die on this planet, despite all that she’s done. Kara’s eyes lit up again and she floated, looking at her parents from up in the air. - “Let me take her to The Exodus so we can heal her.”

     

    “You are asking for too much, Kara. We have no free resources to help her.” - her mother tried to argue stubbornly. Kara wondered, what hardened her heart so much. Was it the fact that she was forced to make the decision back on Krypton about who would live and who would die, condemning most of her race to perish, or was she always like this. Kara had no desire or strength to argue, so she just squared her shoulders and looked her mother straight into the eyes.

     

    “I am not asking, mother. I am demanding.” - Kara thundered, drilling holes in her mother’s skull with her intense fiery gaze. She saw the Kryptonian Space Guards landing around her, and forming a protective circle around her along with the Children of Juru. The most cherished virtue of any Kryptonian was honour and Lena was a hero now - a hero that the ruler of the most honourable people was trying to abandon and betray again. It wasn’t a popular decision, Kara saw disillusionment in the eyes of the Kryptonian warriors, and knew that if her mother pushed it farther that her rule could end right here and now.

     

    “You are playing a dangerous game, Alura.” - Reign stepped forward, turning to the ruler of The Exodus. She scrutinised the few guards that remained on Alura’s side and they shifted uncomfortably under her gaze, unsure what to do next. - “Tread carefully now, the decision you make about this human will also affect you directly. And no one will help you now, not even your daughter.” 

     

    Alura threw a worried gaze upon Kara, who still floated a foot off the ground, Lena’s limp body clutched carefully in her arms. She watched her mother indifferent, her eyes heated up, fire reigniting under her skin, cracking it open around her cheekbones again. Her white pupils were void of any feelings, only her tightly squeezed jaw showing her inner tension.

     

    “If it is what you wish, daughter. Bring her to The Exodus and I will allow our best doctors to work on her, but then she must leave our home and never come back.” - she said, deciding to give up. Kara noticed how much strength it took for her to give in but she was too emotionally drained to care.

     

    The following days were like a fever dream. As soon as she brought Lena on board the Kryptonian’s lead battleship, she was taken from Kara and placed into a medi tank. Doctors put a breathing tube down her throat and submerged her into a viscous liquid to slow her bodily functions to prevent internal damage from spreading in hopes that she would make the trip back to The Exodus alive . 

     

    She sat in the hallway of the hospital wing, waiting for the surgery to end. It felt endless, many hours passed and many surgeons came and went, working hard. One or two sometimes came out to inform her that Lena was still hanging in there but the damage to her brain was so extensive they weren’t sure what to expect. They looked at her with sympathy and then went back to work, kind and attentive, full of respect to the human hero who just saved their race from full extinction. 

     

    Many of Kara’s fellow Space Guards came and went, comforting her, bringing water and food, begging her to take a bite or drink and never leaving until she obliged. Kal was always near her, along with Alex and Kelly, who curiously started all around, fascinated by the advanced technology of the Kryptonians. Kara felt numb and empty, just squeezing out a thankful smile to those around her not to seem like a complete asshole. 

     

    “The surgery is complete and it’s a success, Commander. Your mate will live.” - the head surgeon finally emerged from the room, looking completely spent, beads of sweat covering his forehead. Kara perked up but saw his eyes and felt a cold dread creeping up her spine. - “We…aren’t sure if she will regain her mental capability. The damage to her brain was widespread, we pumped out a lot of blood and some of her blood vessels ruptured. She also suffered widespread damage to the brain matter itself, and we were forced to dissect and remove some parts of it that were irreparably damaged. We placed implants and stem cells at the most damaged areas but only when she wakes up…if… if she wakes up from her coma, will we be able to see how severe the damage is.”

     

    “So she could remain comatose?” - Kara asked calmly. She felt like her soul already left her body, so everything that the doctor said was, albeit scary and shocking, accepted and understood. Kara just wanted to clarify a few things before settling into her new reality. 

     

    “Yes, she may, but we are hopeful. She is a strong human girl, Commander. She should have been brain dead, that lamely installed hacking device in combination with the advanced AI that attacked her should have fried her brain to the point of no return, but she pulled through it. Yes, the damage is widespread and deep, but her brain functions are still active, she survived the trip here. What is worrying is that a lot of time passed between her injury and this operation.”  - The doctor sat near Kara, exhausted, his voice quiet and raspy from the dehydration and hours of hard work. - “I cannot promise you anything, but we should have hope, Commander.”

     

    “I lost hope at some point but she managed to bring it back. She managed to save my life in more ways that you can imagine, Doc.” - Kara said, rubbing face with her palms. She leaned her elbows on her knees to rest her head on her hands a bit. She suddenly felt very tired so she stayed like this, quiet for a few moments, gathering her thoughts. - “But you know, no matter how it takes her to wake up and how severe the impact on her mental capabilities is, I love her, and I will love her no matter what.”

     

    She heard the doctor humming quietly, wholeheartedly and felt his hand patting her on her knee before he got up and walked away leaving her surrounded just by her worried family, new and old. She leaned back, resting her head on a cool wall behind her and looked up at the white ceiling brightly lit with the cool lights. She believed that Lena will pull through, but most importantly she will live and no matter how hard it will be for them both, Kara will be here for her, always.

     

    LOG.15: nevertheless, she persisted

    Chapter Summary

Hope was the only thing that kept her afloat right now, as it was the only thing that doctors could say. "We hope she will wake up", "we hope her mental capabilities will not change drastically", et cetera. And Kara clung to this hope all these months. Without it she would drown in her own misery, destroyed by grief and depression.

He also said that, because I was very helpful to the people of The Exodus I will probably be found not guilty and will be let go.”
