    “You were right, Kara. The Exodus is suffocating.”

     

    LOG.16: still I rise

    Chapter Summary

    Lena finishes her business on The Exodus and tries to leave it behind just to find out that people she love will always stay by her side.

    “I believe you received your summons to appear before the court last week, Miss Luthor?” - the Kryptonian man with an oily stare in his small eyes asked her again, as if she didn’t hear him the first time. He walked around the room, his arms folded behind his back and looked at the weird shaped vase that stood on the ground like he was ready to buy it. It was filled with colourful flowers that Kara loved to give to Lena so much. He stopped before the big digital frame with the picture from Esme’s sixth birthday. He scrunched his face in disdain - apparently this eclectic bunch wasn’t to his liking.

     

    “Yes I did!” - Lena finally snapped, watching his expression change. She turned around, but apparently it was a tad too fast for her. She felt her head spinning and a slight cramp going through her body, causing a tremor in her hands. She saw the man’s gaze dropping from the picture to her trembling fingers, his lips pressing in a thin line. She squeezed her fists tight and turned to the window to hide any other sign of micro seizure. She saw pity in his eyes, of course he thought that she was pathetic. - “And I will remember to attend it, thank you very much for your timely reminder.”

     

    “I know that the General will try to stop you. You have an awful lot of support from her and around The Exodus.” - She turned around and watched the man look her up and down, as if determining her worth to high society and, finally, deeming her a piece of garbage. - “Which is not bad, of course. But knowing about your influence over her and how she can turn you off the trial… You do understand that we, as a society, value honesty and justice above all else.”

     

    “Oh, of course I know that, Kelex.” - Lena rolled her eyes and walked towards the door, opening it and showing the Kryptonian the way out of the spacious apartment that she shared with Kara. - “I will respect your customs and show up. It’s my obligation, after all, as I am, by the merits of your ‘honourable and just society’, just a felon. And Kara will not stop me, don’t worry about it, she will respect any of my decisions.”

     

    “You have a tight grip on her.” - Kelex looked at her, pressing his lips together again. - “Whatever you are thinking, remember, you are just a human. She is the daughter of a ruling family of The Exodus, and despite the matrix matching you, it means nothing. The ruling Kryptonian society will never accept you two as mates, and prolonging this unlikely union will only bring you both more… inconveniences.” 

     

    “I understood you the first hundred times you said that. Now do me a favour and leave.” - Lena opened the door wider and motioned in the direction of the exit with her head. Kelex stomped past her, only pausing a little with the intentions of telling her something else, but then decided not to and stomped away towards the pod that awaited for him outside. 

     

    Lena closed the door and sighed, pressing her forehead over its cool surface. She was discharged just a week ago and every time Kara went to her office, she was pestered by Alura’s minions. Kelex was the most common visitor - he sprouted out of nowhere ever since she woke up from her coma just to remind her how small and insignificant she was and that she was still a felon, despite everything she had done for this damned ship. What was craziest of all - it felt like it was an everyday occurrence. Yes, you are a hero but also a felon, even if you are obviously innocent and still need to go through the judging process just because that’s the rules.

     

    Rules were everywhere, stiff, sometimes dumb and horribly suffocating. Lena observed enough while she was too weak to walk and was brought around by Kara, who either carried her in her arms or wheeled her around in a wheelchair. The secluded white Kryptonian world was blindingly clean, roads were wide and straight, people were stiff and stubborn. Even clothes were made not for comfort but for looking immaculate with their straight lines and clean cuts. Lena’s blue jacket didn’t allow her to slouch, it made her feel like a mannequin every time she went out for a walk in the clean, brightly lit park where people moved around quietly, or sat on the benches spewing boring nonsense at each other.

     

    There was nothing Lena wanted to do more than to leave this place behind forever. And she would, with the first shuttle she could steal, but she didn’t have a heart to do it because of Kara. She just started a new life, she was promoted to their highest military rank, she was loved and revered as a hero and a protector and Lena just couldn’t ruin it all for her. Kara finally seemed happy with her life, settled down and doing what she loved the most - helping people. 

     

    Kelex was right, Lena was superfluous here. It wasn’t her world, she was a stranger here and she couldn't help but think that she was dragging Kara down, first with her illness, then with her disability and now with her uncertainty about what to do with her life. Kara spoke about how she wants to spend their lives together, of marriage and a better place to live together, some place nicer, but despite her melting in Kara’s arms and nodding to her dreamy words the gritty voice of Kelex invaded her mind every time, reminding her of the fact that she is not welcome here, that she is not and will never be one of them and there will never be any nice place for her to settle down.

     

    She wouldn’t drag Kara down, not when Kara is finally happy. So she decided to leave as soon as possible. It is only a week until the second trial and only a week until she will be able to get away from here. Maybe Kara will be better off without her. It will be hard for her at first, and Lena will probably break her heart. But in the long run it will be easier for her to just live her life like she wants to, without anyone who is so weak and broken. Lena sat on the couch and stared at the wall for hours, until Kara came home.

     

    “Hey, baby!” - Lena almost jumped when Kara’s voice sounded right over her ear. She squeezed out a small smile and looked at the Kryptonian who radiated energy and confidence. She was wearing a blue uniform, now in a more deep, dark colour, with golden epaulettes and a golden belt that bore a symbol of the house of El. Kara looked at Lena with a mixed expression - a little bit of concern, a little bit of sympathy and a lot of love. It made Lena’s heart tighten uncomfortably when she remembered her plan to leave. But she reminded herself again, that Kara will be better off without her. - “I think you will be happy to hear this! A letter came in today and it seems like they will finally hold a trial for Rhea.”

     

    “Oh, really?” - Lena tried to sound interested but her thoughts wandered. 

     

    She watched Kara who walked towards the closet and took off her jacket, hanging it and dusting the invisible specks of dirt off it lovingly. She was so visibly proud of her new rank, Lena suppressed a sigh and averted her gaze. She just hoped that Kara would understand her with time. Someday she will see the truth and live her life happy and free, like she would want to, without someone who would only make things worse. Who would render her unwelcomed in the circles she belonged to, and cut off future possibilities to grow and develop. 

     

    “They want to make it an intergalactic trial, which is, I think, the most optimal decision for now. She proudly admitted that she wouldn’t stop with The Exodus so she brought it in herself.” - Kara shrugged and plopped on the couch beside Lena, who just tried to look interested. She squeezed a strained smile and rubbed Kara’s knee in silent endorsement. 

     

    “Are you okay, Lena? You look sad.” - Kara put her hand on Lena’s hand that rested on the Kryptonian’s knee and smiled at her. A hint of sadness darkened her features and Lena perked up, flashing the widest, fakest smile she could muster, trying not to sour the mood. 

     

    She was genuinely happy that Kara is helping to bring Rhea to justice. She was a menace to society, regardless if you are a Kryptonian or a human. After her capture her son assumed the position of the ruler of Daxam, but he didn’t lie when he told them about his distaste for politics of any sorts. He became a harmless, useless ruler to his people and it was just a matter of time when he would be overthrown by someone stronger. If he is smart, he will be just an official royal figurehead and give the rights to rule to his people’s council. 

     

    “No, I am okay.” - Lena flashed a fake smile at Kara. She hated lying to the Kryptonian or hiding anything from her but there was no other option right now. - “When do you leave?”

     

    “How did you know I was invited to the trial?” - Kara laughed out and looked at Lena with the earnestness so pure her heart squeezed painfully. She looked into Kara’s blue eyes and swallowed the words of truth again, pushing them down and hating herself for it.  

     

    “I.. well, you were the main witness of her atrocities, so I just assumed you would be there as one.” - Lena looked down at her own hands and tugged on her index finger as hard and discreetly as she could. The knuckle popped from its place but the jolt of pain grounded her. 

     

    “Yeah.” - Kara let out a short laugh and rubbed at her face. She was stressed right now, uncertain and scared. Lena leaned on her shoulder to offer as much comfort as she could, feeling Kara relaxing under her weight and putting an arm around her waist. - “As much as I hate to leave you alone, I need to go there. I will leave tomorrow for a week. Did you get any news about your trial, baby?”

     

    “No, I have not.” - after spitting these lies out, Lena squeezed her jaw so hard she could swear Kara heard her teeth creaking but she didn’t say anything, just held her closer and sighed heavily. Lena breathed steadily in and out, calming her banging heart. It will be better for them both, she reminded herself. 

     

    True to her words, Kara left the next morning, placing a kiss on Lena’s lips and giving her a long sad stare. Lena didn’t know what that meant, maybe Kara knew that she lied, maybe her heart was broken already, but she will understand with time. Even if it seems impossible right now, and she will feel betrayed and hurt, she will understand that she is better off without the human. She is a strong woman who will do great things. She is an amazing person with a huge heart, she is meant to protect people, to be the hero everyone deserves. And for it to happen, she needs to be free, or she will be damned by society for a lifetime. 

     

    Lena barely survived the remaining few days prior to her new trial, roaming around the city like a mindless zombie and praying to every deity she knew for Kara not to come back earlier. She heard the rumours about the trial and saw it on the hologram screen in her house and outside on the street, where a small crowd gathered before the wide translucent screen to gawk and gossip.

     

    She caught a glimpse of Kara a few times, standing there in all her military glory, giving testimony against the Queen who looked at her with badly concealed hatred. The stash of Kryptonite and half-built bomb launcher were enough to lock her up for years to come, but Kara focused on the killing of the Sawflies and her crimes against the civilians, the slave trade and, finally on Lena. She testified with such passion and ferocity Lena’s heart ached. She caught a few quizzical gazes from people around her, who recognised her as ‘that human’ and returned home just to lay in bed and stare at the wall for hours.

     

    She even felt relieved when the day of her trial came finally and she was brought before the Council of Krypton. Funny enough it consisted only of Alura and the Judge. Kelex motioned to the pedestal in the middle of the room and Lena chuckled, remembering her first encounter with their trial system. At least they now allowed her to walk in on her own, unlike her first time when they just threw her around like a ragdoll. 

     

    “Lena Luthor, felon number 16975, accused of stealing Kryptonite, standing here before you, honourable council members, ready to testify and receive any punishment you will see fit.” - Kelex proclaimed, gesturing widely in Lena's direction. She looked at him with a mix of disgust and fatigue and turned to face Alura, who watched her with indifference. It was the same face of the woman who was ready to send her off to the Phantom Zone as an unnecessary nuisance but Lena held her gaze nonetheless. 

     

    “Felon number 16975. We uh… We decided to go without the defence or prosecution as the council has enough evidence about your involvement in the robbery of the warehouse and stealing a large amount of Kryptonite.” - he cleared his throat and looked at Lena who watched him with calm eyes. She was too tired and too dead inside to care right now, so she just observed it all like a disinterested bystander. - “There is no evidence of you being in the warehouse that night and there was no Kryptonite found on you at any time. There was also indisputable evidence about the involvement of Queen Rhea of Daxam, who took full responsibility for stealing Kryptonite to then use it against The Exodus.”

     

    He ruffled through some papers before continuing. Lena saw that the Judge felt uncomfortable, his brow sweaty despite cool air in the courtroom. She saw a complete absence of any sentinels in the room. Alura played it safe right now, knowing that Lena was loved by the Space Guards and a lot of sentinels of The Exodus looked up to them and were also wary of hurting the human. 

     

    “As there is no evidence of your involvement, the High Council has found you innocent of this crime.” - he banged a small hammer on the marble surface of the pedestal and looked at Lena with tired eyes. - “Although, the court has evidence of your involvement in other petty crimes - robbing other warehouses, selling stolen goods, and other forceful break ins. We considered all the evidence against you and found you guilty.”

     

    Lena exhaled loudly, startling the Judge who looked at her like an insolent child. He watched her tensely while she shifted from one foot to another, looking extremely bored. She just started to fantasise how funny it would be when they will be explaining to Kara why they put her girlfriend in jail, but then the Judge cleared his throat and continued.

     

    “But as the court had taken her heroic deeds into consideration, the High Council had decided to drop all charges and grant Lena Luthor, felon number 16975 a full pardon.” - he put his papers back and peered at Alura who hadn't moved for the whole trial. 

     

    “As you obviously didn’t do it, I will let you go, Luthor girl.” - Alura spat out after a long pause. She watched Lena with wary eyes all this time. It was obvious that she tried to remain at least neutral, but her face still shifted through various shades of disappointment the longer she watched the human who stood before her. 

     

    “I bet it’s only because the resistance have a tight grip on you now, ‘Honourable Councilwoman’?” - Lena laughed out suddenly. It startled both Kelex and the Judge, both of them panicking for a brief moment before giving her a scandalous gaze. 

     

    “No, it’s because you are innocent. “ - Alura asserted, looking Lena sternly in the eyes.  - “As much as I want you gone I will not condemn you to the Phantom Zone once again, as you know, we, Kryptonians, value honour and justice above all else.” 

     

    “Justice and honour didn’t stop you the first time.” - Lena sighed, crossing her hands behind her back and giving Alura the same hard stare. - “You were ready to throw me out without any real investigation just because I was chosen as the mate for your daughter by your own matrix.”

     

    “Even the most perfect creations make mistakes, and we, living creatures, are here to fix them.” - she looked at Lena and nodded to the Judge, who fidgeted with a plastic card. Its bluish translucent surface held information about her full pardon and the permit to leave the premises of The Exodus. He walked to Lena and shoved the card in her hand, looking everywhere but not into her eyes. Lena felt a bit of a pity towards him, Alura really held some higher ranked Kryptonians by the balls and made them follow her every whim, but now she just wondered for how long.

     

    Rhea played a dangerous game and was burned by it, Alura was also walking on the thinnest edge and it was just a matter of time before her people would get tired of her rule and overthrow her, making not only her life hell but also Kara’s. Lena took the card and put it in her pocket, not looking at it, just turning around and walking towards the exit.

     

    “You should leave as soon as you can, and better do it before Kara comes back.” - Alura deadpanned, stopping Lena in her tracks. She turned around and looked at Kara’s mother, who watched her with tense eyes. 

     

    “You won’t even let me say my goodbye?” - Lena laughed out loud, but her voice cracked on the way out and ended up breaking in the middle of a sentence.

     

    “No. It will only break her heart. You know she is a kind and sensitive person and I am sure you don’t want to cause her more pain than you already did.” - Alura looked at Lena with unblinking eyes, her chin lifted up and lips pressed in a thin line. The Judge looked highly uncomfortable near her but didn’t do anything. It was a pathetic sight, so Lena just sighed and turned around, stopping briefly before leaving the room for good.

     

    “Don’t worry, my brother will pick me up today.” - she said and walked out of the chamber toward her future. The empty streets were devoid of any movement, except small pods jolting back and forth on the highway, and Lena shivered uncontrollably when the stale air of the ship hit her nostrils.

     

    “Hey.” - the sound of a familiar voice stopped her in her tracks and she turned around abruptly. Reign stood near the entrance into the Hall of Justice, her black clothes making her almost invisible in the shadows. She came out of the dark corner and walked towards Lena, looking at her with a mix of sympathy and concern.

     

    “Sam!” - Lena opened her arms and was wrapped in a hug by the Kryptonian who rubbed calming circles on her back. Lena relaxed against her shoulder and let out a deep sigh. She felt her senses coming back and the unwelcomed anxiety was already clutching onto her from inside. 

     

    “I still don’t understand why you like calling me by that name.” - Reign hummed, smiling into Lena’s dark hair. She smiled, hearing the name that the human made up for her years ago, rocking them both in hopes to calm the human down a bit. Lena knew that Sam heard her heart beating erratically. She herself felt every flutter against her ribcage, and accepted the embrace with gratitude.

     

    “I don’t know, I just like how it sounds.” - Lena finally pulled out of the hug and looked at her friend, giving her a sad smile. She felt absolutely spent. All she wanted was to either cry her eyes out or just lay on the bed staring at the ceiling unblinking, until her eyes went dry. 

     

    “Lex is not coming.” - Reign deadpanned suddenly and Lena felt the panic rising.

     

    “What do you mean? I need to leave, and you know it!” - She looked at Sam with desperation. Her plan just crumbled down like a sand castle on a hot day and she felt a stress headache coming up and spreading out somewhere behind her eyes.

     

    “It’s okay! You’re still leaving, but Alex will bring you to the Megastructure and Lex will meet you there.” - Reign panicked a little, watching Lena’s face. Her eyes probably started to twitch in a micro seizure and it sent the Kryptonian into panic mode. Lena blinked a few tries to chase it away and looked back at Sam who held her by the shoulders and scanned her face with her deep brown eyes. - “We all know how important it is, and that you need to leave, Lena. This place is killing you.”

     

    “Funny, they put my brain back together to keep me alive, but did not take into the consideration the fact that this place itself can suck the life out of a person.” - Lena hummed, looking around thoughtfully.

     

    “It’s not the place, it’s the people.” - Reign folded arms on her chest and looked at the building behind them with undisguised hatred. - “And it’s not even all people, it’s just a bunch of high ranking crooks, who think that they are better than anyone else. It’s the government that destroys you and everything they deem not to meet their standards of how the ‘right people’ should look or behave.”

     

    “Come with us, Sam. You don’t belong here either.” - Lena suddenly pleaded, taking Reign’s hand in hers. She was surprisingly cold, in comparison to Kara who was always warm.

     

    “I can’t. There are people here who depend on me, if I leave now I will be just like any other coward who saw injustice and ran away, thinking that they cannot change anything. But I can, I can change things around here, Lena.” - Reign squeezed Lena’s hand in hers and let her go. She watched Lena’s arm dropping down helplessly and exhaled shakily, blinking a few times to chase the unwanted tears away. 

     

    “Look out for Kara, Sam. I am sure it will be hard for her.” - Lena looked at the Kryptonian with big teary eyes, which made Reign swallow down the knot in her throat. - “Just… When the time comes and she calms down, tell her I love her. And that I hope she will not hate me for it, and that I did it out of love, because I want her to be happy and thriving.”

     

    “I am sure she will be okay.” - Reign averted her gaze suddenly, a strange expression on her face. She turned around, went to the panel and pressed on a screen, calling for a pod to take Lena home. - “Pack your things and come to the Space Guard station. Alex will be waiting for you there. Take your time, she will wait for you no matter how long it takes.” 

     

    “Sam.” - Lena stopped her and walked around the Kryptonian to look into her eyes. - “Promise me that you will tell Kara what I just told you. Promise me!”

     

    “Okay! I promise.” - Sam lifted her hands in defence. Just a few moments later the pod came and she helped Lena to get inside, closing the lid over her head and looking at her with an odd expression. Lena turned around to ask what was going through her head but Reign sent the pod on its way, bolting away from the spot quickly leaving her tall dark figure behind.

     

    There wasn’t too much to pack, she came to The Exodus naked in a medi tank. Thankfully Kara got her armour from the extraction team and cleaned it up nicely. She threw the small amount of Kryptonian clothes she had into the bag in a hurry, intending to discard them somewhere far away. Lena wanted to minimise the amount of stuff she will leave behind and not expose Kara to more trauma by making her clean up her stuff. She took anything that could be a reminder of her and either threw it away or packed it. Finally she stepped away to admire her work and chuckled loudly - all her stuff fit into one medium travel bag. 

     

    She found Alex perched at her usual spot in her office, going through piles of papers and clicking through three body cam recordings at the same time. She walked past a bunch of lower ranked officers, who looked at her with the badly disguised adoration. It would be funny if it wasn’t so sad how differently people of The Exodus perceived her. The elite saw her as dirt staining their immaculate jackets and the ordinary people saw her as someone who helped prevent the full eradication of their race. She greeted them warmly, watching them following her with sadness in their eyes. 

     

    “Knock-knock! Major Danvers?” - Lena opened the door, startling Alex who had just started to fall asleep in front of the screens. - “Did I just wake you up?

     

    “Come in, sassypants.” - Alex stretched her limbs and yawned loudly. - “You cannot imagine how boring my evening was. I much prefer being in the field over falling asleep over the papers.” 

     

    “But at least your salary is better.” - Lena chuckled, putting her bag on the floor and walking into the office. She saw Alex’s eyes dropping to it and then back to Lena’s face. She chewed on her lips thinking about something deeply but then decided against speaking up and just pulled her legs down off the table and sat up.

     

    “So you are ready?” - she asked instead of whatever she was trying to say and Lena nodded. - “And you are completely sure this is what you want?”

     

    “Yes.” - Lena answered shortly. She watched Alex nodding calmly and standing up, pulling her own small bag from under the table.

     

    “A gift for Esme.” - she shook it slightly and threw the strap over her shoulder.

     

    “It is a little bit weird that you aren’t trying to guilt trip me into staying here.” - Lena mumbled, taking her own bag and walking out of the office. Alex stopped before the doors for a short moment, looking at the golden plate on the door. She fondly rubbed her finger over “Major A.Danvers” written in Kryptonese and turned around to face Lena.

     

    “Do you want me to?” - she lifted her eyebrows and smirked. Lena saw a hint of sadness on her face but decided not to pry further.

     

    “No, not really. Just knowing you I expected more resistance, but I appreciate the fact that you understand me.” - Lena sighed and walked with Alex towards the exit. - “After all Kara had done for me, it seems… It seems like I am betraying her, you know? And I hope… I hope she will not be mad at you. I don’t want to put a strain on your relationship, that’s why I wanted Lex to get me.”

     

    “It’s your choice, Lena. The Council decided that you should leave anyway, the fact that you are leaving in secret is just how you want to do it.” - Alex helped her into the shuttle and put their bags on the backseat. - “Kara will understand, I am sure she will.” 

     

    “I hope so.” - Lena answered, watching Alex moving around the shuttle.

     

    “Listen, I have to relay a few orders to the team before we leave. I will be back in a few.” - Alex closed the doors and went back to her team. 

     

    Lena watched her going to the officers who came out of the Space Guard station. She shook hands with them and hugged a few, other’s patted her on the back. Lena tilted her head to the side, watching them. This seemed a little too dramatic for a simple goodbye and she crossed arms on her chest, waiting for Alex to come back, dropping into the driver seat beside her. 

     

    “It looked like you were saying goodbye forever.” - she looked into Alex’s teary eyes and lifted her brow. - “What is going on?”

     

    “Oh no! Not at all! Tyr’s grandma just died. Amazing woman - made the best pies in the galaxy.” - Alex blinked her tears away, starting the engine and waving back to her officers who still stood on the street, watching them leave.

     

    “Why do I have the feeling that you are lying to me?” - Lena waved too, flashing a fake smile to the officers until the shuttle took off into the air and shot towards the exit of the spaceship. 

     

    “I don’t know? Listen, I know you didn’t have the easiest time with us so just try to relax and enjoy our ride.” - Alex mumbled not looking at Lena and taking a sharp turn to dock at the checkpoint before taking them out of The Exodus and into outer space.

     

    She watched Alex accelerating the ship by pressing some buttons on the shuttle’s touch screen and relaxed into her seat, watching the stars shooting past them as they went into the hyperlight speed. Alex fell silent as soon as they left the Exodus which didn’t bother Lena as she couldn’t think of  anything to talk about. The silence wasn’t heavy, despite the overall gloomy mood inside the shuttle, they both gave each other space to think and to come to peace with their new reality. As the shuttle finally slowed, Lena held her breath for some reason when she saw the huge disfigured outline of the Megastructure.

     

    They breached the upper layer of the planet, flying through a wide opening and passing a lively neighbourhood, bright neon sign boards blinding Lena for a second. She squinted her eyes, still wary of the blinding lights that could provoke a seizure, but nothing happened and she relaxed her shoulders, looking around. The sight of the motley crowd on the streets walking to and from their own business finally made her feel like she was home. Maybe the Megastructure was a shithole with horrible living conditions and high crime rates, but she was happy and free here. Much more happy than in the white walls of The Exodus, with the people who were slaves of their own suffocating rules. Here she had family and friends, and a person she could call “mother” even if she was just someone who provided for her when she was nothing more than a junk item sold for a few credits. 

     

    Alex tinkered with the control panel, putting in the location of their new hideout. They passed numerous bars and nightclubs going through the narrow tunnel of the higher level, Lena taking in the familiar streets and dark alleys. She smiled, noticing a drunkard sleeping in a mud pool just outside the bar entrance, hugging his bottle. A few thugs lazily fought over something and pushed an unfortunate member of the rival gang around, laughing loudly when he stumbled over his own feet and fell down.

     

    Someone who grew up in a less hostile environment would probably ask why she is so eager to go back to a lifestyle that was as dangerous as this one. Life like this wasn’t fit for a lot of people, in fact it was merely an attempt at survival. But it’s what she was used to - the freedom of the streets, rules that were made by the people for people. To the possibility of overthrowing the criminal mastermind who grew too greedy and bold. To the freedom of being herself without the fear she might disappoint some people that she never cared about. 

     

    Alex circled a building with a huge neon green sign of a winged dragonfly and landed on a wide platform just under it. Lena admired their new house - Lex did a good job choosing a new headquarters for them. A place not in any of the gang territories, far from any turf wars, in a safe and secure position right in the middle of the City of Thousand Lights. It was a town full of opportunities and a haven for local businesses, the perfect place to house the fortress of the newest mercenary group working for the biggest security company of the galaxy. 

     

    “Here it is - your new life.” - Alex exhaled as soon as they landed and looked at Lena with the strangest expression she ever saw on Alex. She opened her mouth to ask what is going on, and why she is like this but Alex opened the shuttle door and jumped out of it like her ass was burning. She walked around the vehicle and opened Lena’s door for her, pulling her out of it by the hand like a carrot out of its garden bed and crawling into the backseat to get their stuff. 

     

    “Welcome back, dear sister!” - Lena tore her irritated gaze from Alex’s ass that peeked out of the shuttle’s backdoor and turned around to meet her brother. He stood there, his arms opened in a gesture that welcomed her into the hug and Lena happily obliged. He squeezed her tightly before breaking the hug and stepping back, looking at her with slight concern. He smiled when he found that Lena was not missing anything important, except maybe some parts of her brain but who counts little things like that. - “No more undercut, huh?” 

     

    “The Exodus has shitty hair salons.” - Lena joked and ran hand over her hair unconsciously. She felt  thick scar tissue under her fingers and jolted her hand away abruptly. Her hair grew out a lot, now making her sport an uneven hairdo with one side long and the other just barely covering her left ear. She wasn’t sure she would be able to ever go back to the old undercut without being super self-conscious about the long, wide scar that went from her temple around her ear and ended on the back of her head.

     

    “As I can imagine.” - he chuckled and pulled her towards the doors that led into their new hideout. She would have to keep reminding herself it wasn’t really a hideout now, though, but more of a headquarters. Its wide, brightly lit corridors with some doors that led into offices and others that guided them into the depths of the building. Lex had really done so much in a short time to transform their gang of battered scavengers into a private security company. Lex pointed to the doors each stencilled with the names of their surviving team members who all now had an official rank. - “I scrapped together every credit I could get and rented this place. The old bastard… I mean our landlord didn’t think we were fit for a ‘fancy pants place’ like this but I managed to shake enough money over his nose to convince him that we are capable of paying for this. And we already had a few assignments which paid us really well. A few private parties to guard, a few commissions from the Space Guards to guide them into places we know better than them and we are thriving.”

     

    “I am happy the partnership with Space Guards went well, despite all our differences.” - Lena hummed, looking at the door with a plate that read “W. Schott” . 

     

    “Oh don’t get me wrong, our differences are all still there, they just found out that we are much better as collaborators than as enemies, so we made an agreement to work together towards a better future for all of us.” - Lex shrugged and walked towards the big double doors that led into a common room. He stopped there and turned to Lena who looked around, taking in all the new information.

     

    “So we still aren't a legitimate company?” - she chuckled, looking at her brother with a smirk. She knew there was a catch, the sly fox had wiggled out of trouble with the most powerful policing power in the galaxy and was now making profit off of them. Lena was always fascinated by her brother’s wit but this time he had outdone himself.

     

    “We don’t judge, sister! We are a small mercenary company that helps people for money. We don’t discriminate and don’t look into people’s business, our only job is to provide security and get paid for it.” - Lex put his fingers together and looked at his sister over them, his eyes sparkling with menace. - “And the Space Guards pay really well!”

     

    “No wonder.” - Alex finally caught up with them, putting Lena’s bag on the floor under her feet and looking at them both, hands on her hips. - “With your knowledge of so many sketchy places I would say that you are a great addition to the team. Even if you, yourself still aren’t the lawful type.”

     

    “You know that sometimes to catch a big criminal, you might need a little help from a smaller criminal” - Lex beamed at her, locking his arms together behind his back. - “And actually because the Space Guards pay so well, we have managed to bring on a few new hires!”

     

    “Oh! This is amazing!” - Lena gasped, catching Lex’s joyful demeanour. 

     

    “After all the losses The Sawflies suffered, we were in need of new, powerful teammates so we decided to bring more aliens into our ranks. You will meet them soon.” - Lex opened the door and walked inside into a big room that was meant to be a common room in the future. Much of the furniture was still packed but a big leather sofa and a low sitting table with a few drinks were already in their designated places.

     

    “Aunt Lena!” - Esme squealed, jumping off the sofa and running towards the human, almost knocking her off her feet. - “I missed you so much! You haven’t seen my new toy that mommy gave me for my birthday!  I know you were sick and I wanted to save you some cake, but I ate it.”

     

    “It’s okay, munchkin, I am just happy to see you again.” - Lena ruffled Esme’s hair and looked up, watching Kelly who walked towards her and wrapped her in a hug. Nia jumped at them both, squeezing them and nuzzling into Lena’s hair, giggling happily. She was torn away from the girls and hugged again by James now, who lifted her up and squeezed the air out of her, making her let out a strangled sound. He let her go finally just to be caught by J’onn and Winn who took their turns to hug her. Brainy fist bumped her while she was clutched by the Martian who anxiously and gently probed her brain and exhaled softly when he found her memories intact. 

     

    “If you like these dudes, wait until you will meet our new hires!” - Lex announced, eliciting  a chuckle out of everyone. 

     

    “Oh, my word, I cannot wait!” - Lena laughed out in a fake excitement, playing along with Lex’s obvious joke.

     

    James’ loud cough startled her and made her look up at him, her brow lifted in a silent question. He looked at her with the same weird expression Alex had as they left The Exodus and stepped to the side, revealing a person behind his back. Lena felt tears instantly stinging her eyes as soon as she realised who it was. Still wearing the same old armour that they were given by the old race in the depths of Megastructure, Kara stood there silently, watching Lena with tender eyes. 

     

    The team stepped away, giving them space and Kara walked towards Lena, taking her hand and looking into her eyes. It made her gasp for air suddenly, tears pouring out of her eyes. She touched Kara’s face, still in disbelief that she was real, and felt the soft lips of the Kryptonian grazing over her palm. 

     

    “What have you done? Why are you here?” - Lena whispered, swallowing down the lump in her throat. She caressed Kara’s face, watching how she leaned into the touch, her blue eyes glistening with unshed tears. - “I thought you were finally happy at home… With your job, with your life.”

     

    “Don’t you understand?” - Kara looked deep into Lena’s eyes, taking her face into her palms. - “You are my happiness. I can love my job and love helping people but that can never compete with my love for you. I will give up anything just to be with you. If you are gone, then I have nothing left. I already lost you once, and I will never allow it to happen again.” 

     

    “I just thought that without me holding you back you will be happier. You will be where you belong and have everything that you deserve, the respect you’ve earned and your place in society. What will you have with me? I am sick and broken, damn it, I cannot even hold my rifle now, much less shoot it. My hands are trembling, I cannot even watch a hologram screen without risking a seizure. Is it what you really want?” - Lena sniffled, shrugging. She wanted to back away from Kara, a wave of shame overtaking her. She never felt more useless than now, the joy of returning to her team again vanishing quickly. 

     

    “Lena, listen to me.” - Kara took her hands again and kissed her knuckles, urging her to look into her clear blue eyes, - “You are the strongest person I have ever known. You are brave, fierce and smart. You are courageous and kind. You survived things I cannot even begin to imagine. You fell and rose again, and never let any obstacle destroy you. You are amazing, Lena! Each of your injuries is a reminder of your power. You never gave up, despite everything that happened to you and I will never give up on you.” 

     

    “So you really are ready to leave everything behind just to be with me?” - Lena exhaled shakily, watching Kara’s face. She saw a content smile on her, that lit up her features. 

     

    “Of course I am. I love you and there is no line in this universe I wouldn't cross for you. You are my shining star, my guiding moonlight and I am no one without you. A tall building, the House of Research, stuck up in the middle of it, its metallic surface reflecting the blue sky of the Avalon - the name of the planet they finally settled on. At first this close coexistence with other aliens created a huge divide between Avalon and The Exodus, which was still ruled exclusively by the House of El.
